Write A=lambda^k, B=beta_k-1, so A=2B^2+B. Its shifted sum is R=A+B+2. For B>1 set a=B/2 and consider the asymmetric Huber function with derivative clip(x,-a,1) and minimum zero at x=0. Start at x_0=A. The whole silver prefix stays on the right affine ray with gradient 1, arriving at 1. The beta_k step arrives at -B. The final unit step arrives at -B+a=-a. At all sampled points Q_{i,star}=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2=0. At the final point g=-a and f=a^2/2, so the saturated primitive potential would require A>=Ra=RB/2. But
RB-2A=B(A+B+2)-2A=2B^2(B-1)>0.
Therefore the candidate must not be treated as a primitive seed. This exact failure does not rule out a weaker last-iterate objective certificate for the doubled family.

A tested richer certificate interface:
For a one-step schedule t in (0,3/2], put D=2t+1. Choose a nonnegative history weight u and final weight v=D-u. Define
J_(t,u)=2u(f_0-f_1)+u(1-u)||g_0||^2+2v(t-u)<g_0,g_1>+v(1-v)||g_1||^2.
With Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 and x_1=x_0-tg_0, direct coefficient comparison gives
J_(t,u)=(2D-2u)(-Q_10)+(2D-4u)(-Q_01)
+[-u^2+(4t-2)u-2(t-1)D]||g_0-g_1||^2.
Thus this is a genuine exact certificate whenever all three displayed coefficients and u,v are nonnegative. For t=1, u=3/2, it gives J=3(-Q_10)+(3/4)||g_0-g_1||^2. For t=3/2, u=2, it gives J=4(-Q_10).
The certificate implies the objective bound W_1(t)<=1/(2t+1), by adding u Q_(0,star)+v Q_(1,star) and completing the square ||x_0-x_star-u g_0-v g_1||^2. It retains information lost in a scalar endpoint-gradient coefficient.

The concrete finite algebra obligation for the reset candidate is to propagate a rank-one dominance certificate whose coefficient vector equals kappa times the steps in the interior but has independent coefficients on the last two sampled gradients and the final gradient. The original primitive-plus-beta_k objective block already has the explicit vector [pi^(k),A+1,2beta_k-1]. After the appended unit step, its bridge-gradient coefficient is of order A, whereas the physical bridge step is of order sqrt(A); this explains why a single common multiplier on all past steps loses necessary information. Derive the finite boundary-variable quadratic identity for composing two such objects; do not merely posit its feasibility. If closing the certificate class would require retaining every internal coefficient with no quantitative recursion, record that finite-state proposal as BLOCKED rather than presenting a restatement of full PEP as progress.

For a negative proof, a successful certificate still must yield a nonvanishing separation relative to the exact B_(n_(k,j))=2S_bullet(n_(k,j))+1, or must supply a way to insert the improved blocks into an objective construction with an exact profile comparison. The algebraic mass formula alone does neither. No new verifier submission is authorized for this exploratory package.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000773 -->
# Candidate: finite terminal seed washout

Status: verifier accepted; fact_id 0be8b54e0a33381c. This is not the project target.
Shared finding: 65e3a18284ee82c4.

## Statement

Finite terminal-block improvements wash out under the Zhang–Jiang ConPD rule.

Define phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2 and psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4 for x,y>=0. Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,
S_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],
S_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].
Write B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

For a nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L.

Let E be any nonempty finite set of finite-length nonnegative schedules, allowing the empty schedule. For b in E, write m_b for its length and s(b) for the sum of its entries. Define
C_E=max_{b in E} max{0,sqrt(2s(b)+1)-sqrt(B_{m_b})}.
Construct a schedule by choosing b in E and an integer r>=0, setting H_0=b, and, for j=1,...,r, choosing any nonnegative schedule a_j of length k_j>=0 with s(a_j)<=S_circle(k_j) and setting
H_j=[a_j, psi(s(a_j),s(H_{j-1})), H_{j-1}].
If N=m_b+sum_{j=1}^r(k_j+1), then every such schedule satisfies
W_N(H_r)>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2
=U_N^ZJ (1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2).
Moreover B_N>=2N+1. Consequently the lower-bound ratio tends to 1 uniformly over all schedules in this construction as N tends to infinity. In particular no fixed positive relative asymptotic improvement below U_N^ZJ can be obtained solely by using a fixed finite library of improved terminal blocks while retaining the displayed ConPD rule and the primitive-block sum caps. This is a statement about that construction class, not the unrestricted minimax problem.

## Proof

All arguments below are elementary and self-contained; no previously verified or external theorem is invoked.

1. A lower bound valid for every nonnegative schedule.
Fix any length n>=0 and any h in [0,infinity)^n. Put s=s(h)>=0 and tau=1/(2s+1). In dimension one, for any fixed L>0 define
f(z)=L z^2/2 when |z|<=tau, and f(z)=L(tau|z|-tau^2/2) when |z|>=tau.
The two expressions and their first derivatives agree at z=+tau and z=-tau. The derivative is L times z clipped to [-tau,tau]. This derivative is nondecreasing, so f is convex. Clipping is 1-Lipschitz: if u<=v, its increment lies between 0 and v-u, as can be checked from the three intervals separated by -tau and tau. Thus f has L-Lipschitz derivative. It is differentiable everywhere, has minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0. Take x_0=1.
Nonnegativity of h implies every partial sum lies in [0,s]. An induction on i yields x_i=1-tau sum_{t=0}^{i-1}h_t: indeed this formula implies
x_i>=1-tau s=(s+1)/(2s+1)>=tau,
so f'(x_i)=L tau and the next gradient update gives the same formula at i+1. This also handles s=0 and equality at the boundary. Thus
x_n=(s+1)/(2s+1), f(x_n)=L tau x_n-L tau^2/2=L/(2(2s+1)).
Since |x_0-x_star|^2=1, this legal instance proves W_n(h)>=1/(2s(h)+1), including n=0.

2. Scalar form of one join.
For x,y>=0 put A=x+1>=1 and D=2y+1>=1. Direct substitution into the definition of psi gives
2[x+psi(x,y)+y]+1
=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2
=((sqrt(D)+sqrt(D+8A))/2)^2.
Denote the middle expression by G(A,D) and define T_A(q)=(q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2 for q>=0. Then sqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)). Both G and T are increasing in each of their displayed arguments.
For q>=p>=0, rationalizing the difference of square roots gives
0<=T_A(q)-T_A(p)
=(q-p)/2 [1+(q+p)/(sqrt(q^2+8A)+sqrt(p^2+8A))]
<=q-p,
because the denominator in the fraction is at least q+p; when q=p=0 the difference is zero without division. It follows also by interchanging q and p that T_A is 1-Lipschitz. In particular if q<=p+C for C>=0, monotonicity and this inequality imply T_A(q)<=T_A(p)+C.
All bridge steps are nonnegative: since x,y>=0, sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9))>=2y+1, the formula gives psi(x,y)>=1.

3. A reference recursion dominated by the exact benchmark.
Fix a construction from the statement. Write N_0=m_b, N_j=N_{j-1}+k_j+1 and A_j=1+s(a_j). Its actual masses D_j=2s(H_j)+1 satisfy
D_0=2s(b)+1 and D_j=G(A_j,D_{j-1}).
Set reference masses E_0=B_{m_b} and E_j=G(A_j,E_{j-1}).
We prove E_j<=B_{N_j} by induction. The assertion is equality at j=0. If it holds at j-1, then the primitive-block sum cap and monotonicity of G give
E_j<=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
In the defining maximum for S_bullet(N_j), choose the valid index k=k_j; the remaining index is N_j-k_j-1=N_{j-1}. By the identity in part 2 this choice yields
B_{N_j}>=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
This establishes the induction without any assumed structure for the a_j schedules beyond their length and sum cap.

4. Uniform comparison with a finite seed library.
By definition of C_E, sqrt(D_0)<=sqrt(E_0)+C_E. Applying the last assertion of part 2 inductively with A=A_j shows sqrt(D_j)<=sqrt(E_j)+C_E for every j. Part 3 gives
sqrt(2s(H_r)+1)=sqrt(D_r)<=sqrt(B_N)+C_E.
Apply the legal Huber lower bound in part 1:
W_N(H_r)>=1/D_r>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2.
This proves the claimed finite-horizon inequality.

5. Divergence and uniformity.
The maximum defining B_n includes k=0, so B_n>=G(1,B_{n-1}). Since sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D for D>=0, G(1,D)>=D+2. Starting with B_0=1 gives B_n>=2n+1 by induction.
Because E is finite, C_E is a finite constant independent of the depth r, of the chosen seed, and of all the a_j schedules. Thus
(1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2)>=(1+C_E/sqrt(2N+1))^(-2)->1.
Explicitly, for epsilon in (0,1), when C_E>0 choose any integer N_epsilon>=0 for which
sqrt(2N_epsilon+1)>=C_E/((1-epsilon)^(-1/2)-1);
if C_E=0 take N_epsilon=0. Then for every constructed H_r of any length N>=N_epsilon,
W_N(H_r)>=(1-epsilon)U_N^ZJ.
This threshold is independent of the constructed schedule. Were a fixed delta in (0,1) and an unbounded sequence of constructed schedules to satisfy W_N(H_r)<=(1-delta)U_N^ZJ, choose epsilon=delta/2 and N>=N_epsilon, obtaining a contradiction. The conclusion is restricted to the displayed construction; it imposes no assertion on arbitrary schedules outside that class.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000774 -->
# Exact optimality screen for a three-copy, two-bridge primitive join

Status: complete unverified construction-class obstruction, not a statement about unrestricted minimax optimality. No fact submission under the current allocation.

A concrete candidate was
c=2/3, d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6, M=3+c+d=(11+5sqrt(7))/6,
J(a)=[a,1+cA,a,1+dA,a], A=1+sum a.
It has cdM=1 and M>F(1,1+sqrt(2)), where F is the exact shifted primitive join. Thus it passes the full-curvature product balance and suggests a larger primitive capacity than two ordinary binary joins. It is nevertheless invalid. The general argument below screens all positive c,d in this motif and proves that none can exceed the existing binary factor when the seed is quadratic-balanced.

Definitions. At L=1 write x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). For a nonnegative schedule h with sum s=R-1 define its saturated intrinsic primitive energy
V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sR||g_n||^2.
The primitive property means V_h>=0 on every smooth convex trajectory. The following necessary tests are explicit legal one-dimensional functions, so no conjectural composition theorem is used.

1. An exact cut constraint for every primitive schedule.
Choose any step b>=1, let P be the sum of preceding steps, and T the sum of following steps. Set a_minus=(b-1)/(T+1). Use f'(x)=clip(x,-a_minus,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=P+1. Its prefix stays on the positive affine ray and arrives at1. The b-step arrives at -(b-1). All tail iterates stay on the negative affine ray, and the final point is -a_minus. This follows from nonnegative partial sums and (T+1)a_minus=b-1. The degenerate b=1 case is harmless.
At each sample, Q_(i,star)=f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0, since the function is quadratic in its core and affine with the matching offset on each tail. A direct expansion using the GD recursion yields
V_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-sR g_n^2+2 sum_i h_i Q_(i,star).
Thus this trajectory gives V_h=(P+1)^2-R^2 a_minus^2. If h is primitive it follows that
(b-1)R <= (P+1)(T+1).                                      (CUT)
This is a necessary primitive-energy inequality, not merely the objective single-cut lower bound.

2. Full-curvature condition.
For f(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1, all Q_(i,star)=0, and V_h=1-R^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Hence a primitive schedule must obey
R |product_i(1-h_i)| <=1.                                  (QUAD)

3. The three-copy motif cannot exceed the ordinary two-join factor.
Let a be any nonnegative block with shifted sum A>0 and |product_i(1-a_i)|=1/A. This includes each standard quadratic-balanced basic primitive block. Form J(a) with c,d>0 and put M=3+c+d, so its shifted sum is R=MA.
The two bridge instances of (CUT) give
Mc<=2+d, Md<=2+c.
Condition (QUAD) gives Mcd<=1, because the endpoint quadratic multiplier has magnitude cd/A.
Writing t=c+d=M-3 and u=(M-1)/(M+1), the cut conditions are exactly c,d<=u. If M<=4 there is already no gain over F(1,1+sqrt(2))>4. Suppose M>4. Both c,d lie in [t-u,u]. At a fixed sum t, the product c(t-c) is concave, so
cd >= u(t-u)=(M-1)(M^2-3M-2)/(M+1)^2.
Combining this with Mcd<=1 gives
M^4-4M^3-1<=0.
This polynomial is strictly increasing for M>3, and has one root M_*>4. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2) and C=F(1,lambda). Its defining equation C^2-(1+lambda)C-lambda=0 gives lambda=C(C-1)/(C+1). Substitution into lambda^2-2lambda-1=0 gives C^4-4C^3-1=0. Therefore M_*=F(1,lambda), and necessarily
M<=F(1,1+sqrt(2)).
The existing two-step binary parenthesizations of three copies attain this scalar factor. This argument rules out a larger primitive factor for the displayed three-copy motif, regardless of a proposed richer certificate.

4. Exact hostile instance for the displayed new constants.
Take a=[sqrt(2)], A=lambda, c=2/3 and d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6. One has 0<d<2/3. Use f'(x)=clip(x,-1/4,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=A. The first sqrt(2)-step reaches1; the first bridge reaches -cA. Every remaining step stays on the negative affine ray because their total is T=(2+d)A-1 and
-cA+T/4 <= -1/4 iff d<=2/3.
The endpoint is x_5=-cA+[(2+d)A-1]/4. On the negative affine ray, the primitive terminal expression x_5^2+2s f_5+sR g_5^2 equals (x_5-s/4)^2. Since s=MA-1,
x_5-s/4=-A[c+(1+c)/4]=-(13/12)A.
Thus V_J=A^2-(13A/12)^2=-(25/144)A^2<0. This is a direct failure of the proposed primitive certificate in dimension one. It does not rely on reversing schedules or on a general H-duality assertion.

Search record and scope.
Matlas returned unrelated statements for the requested primitive/H-duality composition search. The downloaded GSW paper arXiv:2410.16249 was checked: its Section 3.1 states H-duality for basic schedules, so no unsupported universal reversal closure was assumed. A tiny SDP check was considered, but cvxpy, numpy, scipy, clarabel, and scs are absent; no numerical solver, exhaustive search, or installation was run. The exact cut construction made one unnecessary.

The obstruction uses the seed's exact quadratic balance and the three-copy/two-bridge form. It does not close non-balanced seeds, independently chosen blocks, four-block joins, or a direct objective certificate. Those would require their own feasible certificate and exact-profile comparison. Any proposed reopening must identify which hypothesis changes and retain the cut test as a mandatory hostile example.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000775 -->
# Four equality instances forbid positive intrinsic quadratic storage at a benchmark schedule

Status: unverified exact counterexample argument. No new solver or verifier call. This is a limitation of positive quadratic remainders in the intrinsic objective energy, not of all certificates or of the unrestricted minimax problem.

Put t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and consider the known three-step schedule

    h=(t,lambda,3/2), S=5/2+2t, D=2S+1=6+4t=2lambda^2.

For nonnegative proof weights u=(u_0,u_1,u_2,u_3) with sum D, define

    d=x_0-x_3, U=sum_i u_i g_i, e=U-d,
    E_u=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2
          +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||U||^2.

Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

## Claim

If a symmetric PSD matrix P of size4 satisfies

    E_u >= sum_(i,j=0)^3 P_ij <g_i,g_j>               (1)

for every legal smooth convex trajectory of this schedule, then P=0.

This applies to any positive quadratic storage formed from fixed linear combinations of the four gradients: substituting those combinations into the storage matrix gives a PSD matrix P in (1). A nonzero matrix on redundant coordinates is possible only if its quadratic form vanishes identically on the GD coordinate subspace.

## 1. Why equality instances constrain every choice of proof weights

For every minimizer x_star, expansion gives

    ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)
       -2D(f_3-f_star)=||x_3-x_star-e||^2+E_u.      (2)

Each one-dimensional instance below has x_star=0, f_star=0, x_0=1, Q_(i,star)=0 at every query, and 2f_3=1/D. Thus (2) implies

    E_u=-|x_3-e|^2<=0.

Under (1), PSD gives E_u>=0, so E_u=0 and the gradient vector belongs to the kernel of P. This argument does not assume the standard proof-weight vector or any completeness theorem for interpolation certificates.

## 2. Four explicit legal equality instances

Every derivative displayed below is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Its integral from zero is therefore differentiable, convex, 1-smooth and minimized at zero. The formulas also directly show f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 at the sampled points, giving Q_(i,star)=0.

### A. Global Huber plateau

Let c=1/D and set g(x)=max(-c,min(x,c)). All four sampled points remain on the positive affine piece, so their gradients are(c,c,c,c). The endpoint is x_3=1-Sc=(S+1)/D, and 2f_3=1/D. After division by the nonzero initial gradient, the gradient row is

    v_H=(1,1,1,1).

### B. Full quadratic

Take f(x)=x^2/2. The gradient row, with g_0=1, is

    v_Q=(1,1-t,2-t,-1+t/2).

The last entry is -(2-t)/2=-1/(2+t), and (2+t)^2=D, so 2f_3=1/D.

### C. Middle-cut asymmetric Huber

Let c=1/lambda and a=t/(4lambda). Set g(x)=max(-a,min(x,c)). Its exact points are

    x_0=1, x_1=c, x_2=-4a, x_3=-(5/2)a,

and its gradients are(c,c,-a,-a). Hence f_3=2a^2 and 2f_3=4a^2=1/D. Dividing the gradients by c gives

    v_M=(1,1,-t/4,-t/4).

### D. Capped quadratic with an inner return

Again let c=1/lambda, but use the different fixed derivative

    g(x)=x for x<=c, and g(x)=c for x>=c.

The exact points are

    x_0=1, x_1=c, x_2=-tc, x_3=(t/2)c.

The first step stays on the upper affine piece; the bridge enters the quadratic core, and the last step returns to its positive side. All points from x_1 onward are in the quadratic piece. Thus f_3=t^2 c^2/8=c^2/4 and 2f_3=1/(2lambda^2)=1/D. Dividing the gradients by c gives

    v_C=(1,1,-t,t/2).

This fourth equality case is essential. Using only the previous three rows would incorrectly leave room for a square proportional to

    ||g_2-g_3-(3/2)(t-1)(g_0-g_1)||^2.

For the capped quadratic, g_0=g_1 but g_2-g_3=-(3t/2)c is nonzero, whereas E_u=0 under any certificate (1).

## 3. The kernel spans the entire gradient space

Form the4-by4 matrix whose rows are v_H,v_Q,v_M,v_C. Subtract its first row from the other three. Expansion down the first column gives determinant

    (-t)(-1-t/4)(3t/2)=3(1+t/4)>0.                  (3)

The four rows therefore span R^4. For a PSD matrix, v^T P v=0 implies P v=0, as follows by diagonalizing P with nonnegative eigenvalues. Part1 puts all four independent rows in its kernel, proving P=0.

## 4. The obstruction occurs at an actual valid tight certificate

The usual weights are

    u=(t,2+t,2+t,2+t), sum u_i=D.

Their intrinsic energy has the explicit identity

    E_u=2lambda(-Q_10)+2(-Q_01)+D(-Q_32)
          +2lambda(-Q_21)+2lambda(-Q_31).            (4)

All coefficients are positive, so E_u>=0 and (2) yields W<=1/D. The four instances above attain this bound. Equation(4) can be checked directly from the GD differences x_1=x_0-tg_0, x_2=x_1-lambda g_1 and x_3=x_2-(3/2)g_2. This is also the primitive-one-step/dominant-one-step ConPD certificate, but no external closure theorem is needed for the displayed identity.

## Implication for retained-state propagation

The finite pair residual in max4_finite_pair_residual_transport.md is valid: that two-step objective bound leaves room for a positive quadratic remainder. Such a remainder cannot be carried as a nonzero PSD intrinsic-energy term through every exactly calibrated dominant schedule. At this three-step benchmark it must disappear, or become a structural zero, regardless of which nonnegative tight proof weights are selected.

The result does not exclude an indefinite storage coupled to function values, a state retaining actual interpolation slacks, a certificate at a smaller denominator, a different schedule, or a complete objective certificate in which the initial-distance square is still present. It rules out the specific plan of obtaining exact benchmark calibration and retaining a uniformly positive, nontrivial quadratic penalty depending on gradient-history linear aggregates alone. The complete original minimax target remains unresolved.

## 5. Further rigidity inside the intrinsic certificate class

The four equality instances also determine the proof weights uniquely. Equation(2) forces U=x_0 on each instance, since e=x_3 and d=x_0-x_3. The four independent gradient rows therefore determine u uniquely; the vector u=(t,2+t,2+t,2+t) satisfies these four equations and is the only possible choice.

Suppose in addition that an intrinsic certificate is written in the full finite-edge form

    E_u=sum_(i!=j) a_ij(-Q_ij)+g^T P g,
    a_ij>=0, P>=0.

Part3 gives P=0. Every edge with positive a_ij must have Q_ij=0 on all four equality instances. Direct substitution gives the intersection of allowable edges

    (0,1), (1,0), (2,1), (3,1), (2,3), (3,2).

For clarity, the global Huber and full quadratic make every Q zero. On the middle-cut asymmetric Huber, all edges between the two upper and two lower samples are strict except the lower-to-upper-corner edges(2,1),(3,1); within each affine ray both directions are zero. Its zero-edge set is exactly the six edges displayed above. The capped-quadratic instance keeps all six zero and rules out a nonzero PSD remainder as already proved.

Write the six coefficients as a,b,c,d,e,f in the displayed order. The f_0 coefficient and the g_0 g_1 coefficient give

    b-a=2t, a+(1-t)b=0,

so a=2 and b=2lambda. The g_1 g_2 and g_1 g_3 coefficients each give -t c=-2(2+t) and -t d=-2(2+t), respectively. Hence c=d=2lambda. The f_2 and g_3^2 coefficients then give f-e=D and f+e=D, so e=0 and f=D. Thus equation(4) is the UNIQUE certificate in this intrinsic finite-edge-plus-PSD class at the tight mass.

This is not a uniqueness theorem for arbitrary full PEP dual certificates with optimum-outgoing edges or for other representations involving the initial distance. It concerns precisely the intrinsic nonnegative-weight certificate class used by the current block-composition program.

## 6. A nontrivial indefinite quantity survives the obstruction

The absence of a PSD gradient remainder does not erase all usable state. Define

    T=<g_2-g_3, t g_1+g_2>.

The exact three-point identity is

    T=(-Q_31)-(-Q_21)-(-Q_32).                       (5)

Let E_prefix=2lambda(-Q_10)+2(-Q_01). Equation(4) gives, for every real theta with |theta|<=2lambda,

    E_u-theta T
      =E_prefix+(2lambda+theta)(-Q_21)
          +(D+theta)(-Q_32)+(2lambda-theta)(-Q_31)
      >=0.                                         (6)

In particular E_u>=2lambda|T|. The quadratic form T is indefinite, with its full matrix on(g_1,g_2,g_3) equal to

    [ 0,    t/2, -t/2 ]
    [ t/2,  1,   -1/2 ]
    [ -t/2,-1/2,  0   ].

It vanishes on all four equality modes, so it is consistent with the full-rank gradient obstruction. This gives an explicit interval of signed quadratic storage terms carried by actual interpolation slacks. The next composition must match a surviving cross term to this state, or retain its anchor function value; treating T as a squared norm would be invalid. A sign error in the initially published version of (5) was corrected on direct expansion: the correct order is I_31-I_21-I_32. The absolute-value bound and all preceding rigidity claims are unaffected.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000776 -->
# Two-sided residual join with aggregate interpolation cancellation

Status: unverified exact certificate identity and successful recovery of the ordinary ConPP join on its structural subspace. No improved recursive family has been found. This continues the failed aggregate-only rule4be2737d7e8764a6 without silently reusing its insufficient positivity restrictions.

For a GD block and nonnegative proof weights u, write D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d, and

  E=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2
      +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.

Then E>=0 implies the objective guarantee W<=1/D by the squared-distance identity reproduced in research/max4_nonlocal_tail_transport.md. All formulas below use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0, at smoothness1.

## 1. Two arbitrary blocks and the exact new state

Let block a end at y with gradient g. A bridge of physical length tau>=1 moves to y'=y-tau g, and block b starts there and ends at z with gradient q. Let the blocks have states(D_a,e_a,E_a) and(D_b,e_b,E_b); denote the tail gradient aggregate by U_b and its displacement by d_b, so e_b=U_b-d_b. The tail's proof-weight vector is v and its terminal entry is v_end. Assume certificates

  E_a>=nu_a||e_a-K_a g||^2,
  E_b>=nu_b||e_b-K_b q||^2,

where the nu coefficients are nonnegative. Exact structural relations such as e_a=K_a g may instead be retained as relations, without replacing them by a finite quadratic penalty.

Form new proof weights by keeping the prefix weights, adding eta to the prefix terminal entry, scaling all tail weights by beta, and adding gamma to the final entry. Require

  beta>=0,
  prefix_terminal_weight+eta>=0,
  beta*v_end+gamma>=0,
  D_c=D_a+eta+beta D_b+gamma>0.

The new residual is exactly

  e_c=e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b+e_b+gamma q.         (1)

The change in energy, before eliminating the function difference Delta=f_y-f_z, is

  E_c=E_a+beta E_b+2(D_a+eta)Delta+R_0,

where

  R_0=eta(1-eta)||g||^2-2eta<e_a,g>
      +beta(1-beta)||U_b||^2
      +2beta<U_b,(tau-eta)g-e_a>
      +gamma(1-gamma)||q||^2
      -2gamma<q,e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b+e_b>.      (2)

Equation(2) follows by expanding the definition of E under the displayed changes of proof weights; it involves no smoothness inequality yet.

## 2. Four aggregate interpolation identities

Define

  A_y=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y),
  A_z=-2sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,z),
  P=-2Q_(z,y), N=-2Q_(y,z).

Substitution of y-x_i=tau g+(y'-x_i) and d_b=U_b-e_b gives

  A_y=2D_b Delta-E_b-||U_b||^2
         -2(tau-1)<U_b,g>-D_b||g||^2,

  A_z=-E_b+||U_b||^2-2<U_b,e_b-q>-D_b||q||^2,

  P=2Delta-||g||^2-||q||^2+2(1-tau)<g,q>
          -2<q,U_b>+2<q,e_b>,

  N=-2Delta+(2tau-1)||g||^2-||q||^2
           +2<g,U_b>-2<g,e_b>+2<g,q>.              (3)

Choose scalars c,m,t_y,t_z satisfying

  c>=0, m>=0, t_z>=0, t_y+c*v_end>=0,
  c D_b+t_y-t_z=D_a+eta.                            (4)

Although t_y can be negative, the actual interpolation multipliers in c A_y+m A_z+t_y P+t_z N are all nonnegative. At the edge(z,y), A_y already contributes terminal coefficient c*v_end, so the total coefficient is c*v_end+t_y. At each other tail-to-y edge the coefficient is c*v_i; at tail-to-z edges it is m*v_i; and the edge(y,z) has coefficient t_z. Self-edges vanish. These are the signs that matter, rather than the signs of all four aggregate coordinates separately.

The function-coefficient equality in(4) eliminates Delta from(2).

## 3. A finite-dimensional sufficient certificate test

Define the following quadratic form in(e_a,e_b,g,U_b,q):

  R=R_0
    +c[||U_b||^2+2(tau-1)<U_b,g>+D_b||g||^2]
    +m[-||U_b||^2+2<U_b,e_b-q>+D_b||q||^2]
    +t_y[||g||^2+||q||^2+2(tau-1)<g,q>
                       +2<q,U_b>-2<q,e_b>]
    +t_z[-(2tau-1)||g||^2+||q||^2-2<g,U_b>
                       +2<g,e_b>-2<g,q>].          (5)

Equations(2)–(4) give the exact identity

  E_c=E_a+(beta+c+m)E_b+R
                  +c A_y+m A_z+t_y P+t_z N.        (6)

Consequently, for any nu_c>=0 and real K_c, it suffices that the quadratic form

  R+nu_a||e_a-K_a g||^2
      +(beta+c+m)nu_b||e_b-K_b q||^2
      -nu_c||e_a+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b
                          +e_b+(gamma-K_c)q||^2    (7)

be positive semidefinite. Any exact structural relations from the input blocks may first be substituted into(7); imposing positivity only on that known subspace is then legitimate. For free residual variables, positivity means the full symmetric5-by5 coefficient matrix is PSD, in every Euclidean dimension. This implies E_c>=nu_c||e_c-K_c q||^2 and W<=1/D_c.

This is a certificate construction, not a new algorithm with auxiliary state: all u,v and residuals are proof quantities. The actual algorithm is precisely the predetermined concatenation[a,tau,b].

## 4. Exact ordinary-ConPP calibration

Take genuinely primitive input blocks of capacities K=1+sum(a) and A=1+sum(b), with their standard proof weights(a,K) and(b,A). Then

  D_a=2K-1, D_b=2A-1,
  e_a=K g, e_b=Aq,
  E_a=V_a>=0, E_b=V_b>=0.

Let r>0 be the unique root

  r^2+(A+K)r-AK=0,

so the usual bridge is tau=1+r. Since r<A, set

  J=K+r,
  beta=1, eta=tau-K, gamma=J,
  K_c=K+A+r,
  c=m=J/(A-r),
  t_y=c(1-A), t_z=c r.                             (8)

All actual weight conditions hold: the new bridge weight is K+eta=tau; the final weight is A+gamma=K_c. The interpolation conditions(4) also hold, because

  t_y+c*v_end=c(1-A)+c A=c>0,
  c D_b+t_y-t_z=c(A-r)=J=D_a+eta.

After substitution of e_a=Kg and e_b=Aq, the quadratic remainder(5) vanishes identically. One can check this by the equivalent two scalar identities

  r(K+A+r)=KA,
  K+r=2Ar/(A-r).

Indeed the resulting energy identity is exactly

  V_c=V_a+(1+2c)V_b
          -2c sum_(i in tail, i!=end) h_i(Q_(i,y)+Q_(i,z))
          -2c Q_(z,y)-2cr Q_(y,z),                 (9)

where V_c is the saturated primitive energy for the combined physical sum K+A+r-1. Every multiplier on -Q in(9) is nonnegative. Thus the rule recovers the successful ordinary ConPP certificate on its actual structural identities, with capacity F(K,A)=K+A+r.

The negative aggregate coefficient t_y in(8) is essential when A>1, even though the final multiplier on its interpolation edge is positive. Earlier screens that required every aggregate coefficient nonnegative excluded this calibration. This is the same distinction exposed by the signed interval-coordinate test cd0f93f08a50828f.

## Remaining task

The calibration does not prove closure for arbitrary finite residual penalties. At the exact primitive point e_a=Kg and e_b=Aq are identities, not estimates with a finite nu. A candidate involving nonprimitive blocks must either retain its actual additional boundary relations or satisfy the full matrix(7). No parameters giving a competitive recursive nonprimitive family have been found, and no claim of an improved denominator is made. The endpoint-only weight template is separately refuted by12ea2963a6e53e67; it cannot be substituted for this full internal-weight accounting.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000777 -->
# Variable-step interval matrices and their first exact obstruction

Status: unverified analytic identities and a complete three-step test, not an improved schedule. This extends the matrix construction in Zhao, arXiv:2609.11784v1, Sections3.1–3.3, whose definitions and proof were read from a local downloaded/extracted copy. No external theorem is needed for the calculations below. The authorized numerical budget remains exhausted.

Work at L=1 with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, i=0,...,n-1. Define the trajectory matrix T by T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, and T_ij=0 for j>i. The symbol T is used to avoid confusion with the benchmark denominator B_n. Thus (Tg)_i=g_i+x_0-x_i.

For any matrix C with zero row sums, write b=C^T1 and H=CT+T^TC^T. Define the nonnegative interpolation quantity

  I_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

It equals -2Q_ji in the project's sign convention. Direct expansion gives

  sum_(i,j) C_ji I_ij
  =2sum_i b_i f_i+sum_(i,j) H_ij<g_i,g_j>-sum_i b_i||g_i||^2.    (A)

Diagonal entries of C are immaterial to the sum because I_ii=0. If C has nonnegative off-diagonal entries, the left-hand side is nonnegative on every legal trajectory.

## 1. Interval diagonalization survives arbitrary steps

Fix0<=a<b<=n and positive numbers w_a,...,w_b. Put

  L_i=sum_(a<=j<i)w_j, R_i=sum_(i<j<=b)w_j,
  u_i=h_i(L_i+w_i)-w_i,
  v_i=w_i+h_i R_i.

At i=n choose any auxiliary h_n: all formulas in which it could affect the matrix cancel because R_n=0 and no entry lies above its diagonal. Define C^(a,b;w), zero outside the interval, by

  C_ij=u_i w_j for a<=i<j<=b,
  C_ij=v_i w_j for a<=j<i<=b,
  C_ii=-u_i R_i-v_i L_i.

Its row sums vanish. For i<j in the interval,

  (CT)_ij=u_i(w_j+h_j R_j)=u_i v_j,
  (CT)_ji=v_j(w_i-h_i(L_i+w_i))=-v_j u_i.

For a column before a, its entries vanish because the row sum is zero; for a column after b, its entries vanish by triangularity. Hence H=CT+T^TC^T is diagonal, with

  H_ii=2[(h_i-1)u_i R_i-v_i L_i].                    (B)

If h_i>=1 for a<=i<b, all off-diagonal entries of this interval matrix are nonnegative. For the special choice w_i=1, put s=i-a and t=b-i. Formula(B) becomes

  H_ii=2[(h_i-1)^2(s+1)t-s(t+1)].                   (C)

This is the constant-step paper's diagonal formula with the actual local step h_i inserted; no equality of the steps was needed for diagonalization.

## 2. The primitive coefficient target

Let S=sum_(i<n)h_i. The saturated primitive intrinsic potential is

  V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
      +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

Thus(A) equals V_h exactly when

  b=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),-S),
  H=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-S(S+2)).   (D)

Nonnegative off-diagonal entries of C then certify V_h>=0. This is a sufficient certificate class with zero PSD remainder, not a necessity claim for arbitrary primitive schedules.

There is an algebraic prerequisite for(D). Set p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. Then Tp=1, so C1=0 implies p^THp=0. For the target(D), telescoping gives

  p^THp=1-(S+1)^2p_n^2.

Therefore a certificate of this exact diagonal form requires |p_n|=1/(S+1). A schedule with strict slack in the full-curvature quadratic test requires a nonzero PSD remainder or another quadratic coefficient pattern. This is a structural limit of the ansatz, not a failure of the schedule itself.

## 3. Positive interval coefficients exclude the basic three-step silver block

Let a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, and h=(a,2,a). Use the unweighted modes C^[i,j] from part1 with w=1, and write their coefficients as z_01,z_12,z_23,z_02,z_13,z_03.

Suppose all six z are nonnegative and the target(D) is matched. At index0 the diagonal equation gives

  z_01+2z_02+3z_03=lambda.

The column-sum equation b_0=a gives

  (2-a)z_01+(6-2a)z_02+(10-2a)z_03=a.

Since (2-a)lambda=a, subtracting the first equation times(2-a) yields

  2z_02+(4+a)z_03=0.

Nonnegativity forces z_02=z_03=0 and z_01=lambda. The diagonal equation at index1 becomes

  z_12+2z_13=lambda.

The cumulative column sum through cut1 is a+2. With z_02=z_03=0, only the [1,3] mode has a nonzero cut flow; that flow is a per unit coefficient. Therefore a z_13=a+2, so z_13=lambda and z_12=-lambda, a contradiction.

Consequently the direct positive-mode generalization of the constant-step proof is too narrow even for this basic silver schedule. This is a hostile test of the proposed proof class, not a counterexample to silver validity.

Signed interval coefficients repair the problem while every actual interpolation multiplier remains nonnegative. Choose

  C=lambda C^[0,1]-lambda C^[1,2]
      +lambda C^[1,3]+2lambda^2 C^[2,3].             (E)

Its only nonzero off-diagonal entries are

  C_01=1, C_10=lambda, C_13=lambda,
  C_21=2+a, C_23=5+3a,
  C_31=lambda, C_32=7+5a.

Every listed entry is positive. Setting each diagonal to minus its row sum gives b=(a,2,a,-2lambda). Formulas(C) give H=diag(2a-2,0,2a-2,-(2lambda)(2lambda+2)), so(D) is met. Hence(A) is an exact conic interpolation certificate for the three-step silver block. The apparent negative coefficient in(E) is only a coordinate in the interval basis; it is not a negative interpolation multiplier.

## 4. Explicit elimination of the lower-triangular unknowns

For arbitrary h_i>1, any zero-row-sum matrix C with diagonal CT+T^TC^T is uniquely determined by its strict upper triangle U_ij=C_ij, i<j. Write r_i=h_i-1. For a row j>i define L_(j,i)=sum_(k<=i)C_jk, with L_(j,-1)=0. The off-diagonal equation at(i,j) is exactly

  r_i L_(j,i)+L_(j,i-1)
    =U_ij+h_j sum_(k>j)U_ik.                        (F)

For j=n the sum is empty, so h_n is again immaterial. Starting at i=0, equation(F) gives the lower entries C_ji=L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1) without matrix inversion. Conversely this recursion and the zero row sums make every off-diagonal entry of CT+T^TC^T vanish, so there are no omitted constraints.

All interpolation coefficients are nonnegative precisely when U_ij>=0 and every computed difference L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1)>=0. The remaining column sums and diagonal conditions(D) are linear equations in U for a fixed step vector. Thus this specific zero-remainder certificate class has an exact system of scalar linear equalities and inequalities, with no PSD feasibility test left over. This does not assert that the class captures the full primitive PEP or that the step optimization is convex.

The next analytic task, if this interface is pursued, is a variable-step family and a symbolic solution of those inequalities, including aggregate positivity after signed interval cancellation. Producing the interface alone is not asymptotic progress. Any candidate must still beat the exact benchmark or meet the sufficient amplification threshold proved conditionally in research/max4_primitive_seed_amplification.md.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000778 -->
# Actual rational15 input, full31 parent, and angular iteration limits

Status: UNVERIFIED complete joint candidate, max22. Part I reproduces the prior
max21 proof verbatim; its historical budget/no-further-computation statements
refer only to that prior launch. Parts II-IV give the current authorized work.

# Part I. Exact copied input and its complete construction

# An exact full coupling for the changed fifteen-step instance

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21.
This answers the finite coupling problem in the refreshed TASK associated
with guidance04cc04960aa1755d. It is not a verifier-accepted fact and does
not establish the original all-schedule asymptotic target.

The specific changed seed from finding1d4af67cf6124842, its actual
seven-step child, its word, its values, and its optimum are retained.
That finding is motivation, not a correctness premise: the data and
realizing-function argument are supplied again here. The only cited
accepted result is fact1252b8823bb5a290, for the original Q convention
and its necessity.

The new numerical option is closed: three optimizer evaluations and one
conservatively charged exact fixed-candidate replay used all four slots
and19.17568400500022 measured seconds. No verifier was called. All source,
raw arrays and original statuses are public. The exact rational coupling
and the arithmetic certificate below are independent of solver status.

## 1. The exact unchanged seed and every seed interpolation row

Let

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    rho=599/600, eps=1/100, E=1/10000,
    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,
    A=1+2a+b.

In Euclidean R^3 with its displayed coordinate order define

    U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho*sqrt(eta),eps),
    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V, g_3=W,
    x_3=W, x_2=W+aV, x_1=W+aV+bU,
    p=x_0=W+aV+(a+b)U.                                  (1)

The GD word is exactly h[0]=(a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i are

    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),
    y_1=zU+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),
    y_2=(Klen-1)V+(0,0,eps), y_3=0.                      (2)

Let e_i=<g_i,y_i>. The four energies are

    e_0=m-1, e_1=z, e_2=(Klen-1)eta, e_3=0.

Here is the complete list of vertex normals <g-g_i,y_i>:

* At i=0, the vertices g=0,V,W give -(m-1), -a,
  and -a-(1-rho)z+E respectively; g=U gives0.
* At i=1, the vertices0,V,W give -z,0,-(1-rho)z+E;
  g=U gives0.
* At i=2, the vertices0,U both give -(Klen-1)eta,
  g=W gives -(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta+E, and g=V gives0.
* At i=3 all vertex normals are0.

The elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply
1.39<a<1.43, 2<b<2.01, 2.38<Klen<2.43, z>1, eta>2/5.
Consequently(1-rho)z>1/600>E and
(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E. Every displayed normal is nonpositive.

For any finite data with these normal conditions, set

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                           (3)

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer in(3) as the
Euclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection inequalities, added,
give ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.
Thus P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the defining maxima at each other's
maximizers bounds the differentiability remainder between0 and
||x'-x||^2. Hence F_K is finite convex differentiable and1-smooth,
with gradient P_K. The displayed vertex normals give P_K(x_i)=g_i.

Expansion of the original residual in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

The optimum rows are Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-e_i<=0.
Thus the normal list checks ALL original seed rows.

The minimizer set is K^polar={v:<v,g><=0 for every g in K}.
Sufficiency follows from(3). For necessity test tg in(3), divide the
resulting inequality by t>0 and let t decrease to0. The positive cone
identity

    p=mU+aV+W                                             (4)

gives <p,v><=0 on K^polar. Therefore0 is a closest minimizer and its
true squared distance is

    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z+E,
    N=||W||^2=rho^2*eta+E.                               (5)

This same retained cone identity will prove true distance after both
joins. No change of optimum or chosen normalization is made.

The seed has positive gradient-energy surplus kappa=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.
Here is a complete exact sign certificate. Put
Rbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substitution and s^2=2 yield

    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,
    P0=19019047380837966731,
    Q0=13474265781546157318,
    D0=40842989603870102500.

All are positive and

    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2
      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.

Squaring positive numbers therefore proves the claimed bound for the
barred ratio. Also Rbar^2>Nbar, since
Rbar^2-Nbar=m^2+eta*(Klen^2-rho^2)>0. Adding E to numerator and denominator
raises the ratio Nbar/Rbar^2, proving the displayed bound for kappa.

## 2. Fully specified frames and the actual seven-step child

For a nonzero column vector t in R^d whose unit vector is not e_1, define

    u_t=t/||t||, v_t=e_1-u_t,
    B(t)=I-2*v_t*v_t^T/(v_t^T*v_t), T_t(q)=B(t)q.         (6)

B(t) is a real symmetric orthogonal matrix, with first column u_t.
The first coordinate of T_t(q) is <q,t>/||t||. All the axes used below
have u_t!=e_1. This also follows from the displayed seed data and the
interval enclosures in section4, so there is no zero denominator.

Write T_W(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2) and
T_p(q)=(c_0,c_1,c_2). In R^5 define

    L0(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2,0,0),
    R0(q)=(-c_0,0,0,c_1,c_2),
    r_0=theta*A, alpha_0=r_0*||W||/R.                   (7)

Both L0 and R0 are actual isometries, and

    <L0(u),alpha_0*R0(v)>
         =-r_0*<u,W><p,v>/R^2.                          (8)

The seven-step child has, for0<=i<=3,

    X_i=L0(x_i), G_i=L0(g_i), Y_i=L0(y_i), F_i=f_i,
    X_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(x_i), G_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(g_i),
    Y_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(y_i), F_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*f_i.

Its word and capacity are

    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a), A_1=lambda*A.

The bridge is exact: alpha_0*R0(p)=-r_0*L0(W), and X_3=G_3=L0(W).
All four seed lists <g_i,W>,<y_i,W>,<p,g_i>,<p,y_i> are nonnegative:
the coordinates in(1)-(2) have nonnegative entries. Formula(8) therefore
makes each cross inner product between a gradient and a residual
nonpositive. Subtracting the nonnegative receiving normal energy gives
every cross Q<=0. Within-child and optimum rows follow from section1
and scaling. Formula(3), now applied to all eight G_i, gives one actual
seven-step function with the prescribed values.

Its initial point P=X_0 has squared norm R^2 and remains in the positive
cone of its retained first-seed gradients by(4). Hence R is still its
true closest-minimizer distance. Its endpoint is

    w=G_7=X_7=alpha_0*R0(W),
    N_1=||w||^2=alpha_0^2*N,
    E_i=<G_i,Y_i>=e_i for i<4,
    E_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*e_i for i<4.                       (9)

This fixes all internal positions, gradients, values and indices of the
children in the mandated fifteen-step problem.

## 3. A rational full transverse block and a real nine-dimensional parent

In the exact coordinate order(7), use the two five-dimensional frames
B(w) and B(P) from(6). For a child vector q write

    B(w)q=(q_l0,q_lT), B(P)q=(q_r0,q_rT),

where the tails are columns in R^4. Fix the rational matrix

    C=(1/100)*[[7,0,7,0], [1,0,0,0],
              [18,-1,-10,0], [-17,0,-11,0]].             (10)

Its squared Frobenius norm is934/10000<1. Thus ||C||_op<1, and
H=(I-C^T*C)^(1/2) is a well-defined real positive-definite matrix.
No eigenvalue clipping, numerical rank choice, or PSD repair is involved.
In R^9=R direct-sum R^4 direct-sum R^4 define

    L(q)=(q_l0,q_lT,0),
    Rmap(q)=(-q_r0,C*q_rT,H*q_rT).                       (11)

They are isometries because C^T*C+H^2=I. This explicitly realizes every
cross inner product; there is no unspecified Gram completion.

The next bridge and scale are

    r_1=theta*A_1=A, alpha_1=r_1*||w||/R,
    h[2]=(h[1],1+r_1,h[1]), A_2=lambda^2*A.               (12)

For0<=i<=7, the parent data are

    (x'_i,g'_i,y'_i,f'_i)=(L X_i,L G_i,L Y_i,F_i),
    (x'_(8+i),g'_(8+i),y'_(8+i),f'_(8+i))
       =(alpha_1 Rmap X_i,alpha_1 Rmap G_i,
         alpha_1 Rmap Y_i,alpha_1^2 F_i).                (13)

The bridge is exact since alpha_1 Rmap(P)=-r_1 L(w) and X_7=G_7=w.
All within-child GD updates and values are preserved. Both children have
the same prescribed optimum0. Every future claim about this parent uses
the genuine last gradient g'_15; it is not deleted or freely reset.

Define the exact bilinear form

    B_C(u,v)=alpha_1[-u_l0*v_r0+u_lT^T*C*v_rT].          (14)

The full cross normal conditions are exactly

    Q'_(i,8+j)=B_C(G_i,Y_j)-alpha_1^2*E_j<=0,
    Q'_(8+i,j)=B_C(Y_j,G_i)-E_j<=0                       (15)

for every0<=i,j<=7. Section4 proves every one. All internal rows are
unchanged or scaled copies of section2; all optimum rows are0 or minus
the corresponding nonnegative normal energy. Applying(3) to all16 g'_i
therefore gives one actual finite convex1-smooth F:R^9->R realizing the
whole supplied fifteen-step trajectory and values.

The retained identity(4), through the two left isometries, proves that
0 is a closest minimizer of this parent as well. Its true radius is R.
The endpoint residual is0 and its normalized terminal objective is

    2F(x'_15)/dist(x'_0,argmin F)^2
        =alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*N/R^2
        =kappa^4/A_2^2.                                 (16)

Thus the whole-run value W_15(h[2]) is at least the explicit quantity(16).
The core surplus satisfies A_2^2 times(16)>1+1/500, by kappa>1+1/2000
and the binomial formula. This does not identify A_2^2 with the benchmark
B_15; the distinction is essential to the target scope.

## 4. All cross normals: a short exact rational interval certificate

Only the receiving residual columns j=0,1,2,4,5,6 are nonzero; columns3,7
vanish exactly by(2),(9). Their energies E_j are strictly positive.
Normalize the first row of(15) by alpha_1^2*E_j and the second by E_j.
The following entries are rational UPPER bounds, each divided by10000.
Rows are i=0,...,7 and columns are j=0,1,2,4,5,6 in that order.

    Left gradient against right residual:
    -9709  -8315  -7614 -15069 -19819 -18128
    -9709  -8315  -7614 -15069 -19819 -18128
    -2848  -2537  -9466 -11562 -14680 -15335
    -2858  -2545  -9464 -11565 -14680 -15329
    -9691  -8210  -7465 -14229 -13721  -9131
    -9691  -8210  -7465 -14229 -13721  -9131
   -17587 -19994 -14117  -2457    -73  -5919
   -17579 -19997 -14136  -2420     -2  -5864

    Right gradient against left residual:
    -8931  -7259  -7138 -16612 -24863 -24113
    -8931  -7259  -7138 -16612 -24863 -24113
    -9791  -9737  -9908 -10659 -13075 -14139
    -9791  -9737  -9907 -10659 -13071 -14133
   -10030 -10003  -9953  -9784  -8371  -7579
   -10030 -10003  -9953  -9784  -8371  -7579
   -10165 -10257 -10157  -9644  -9231  -9290
   -10165 -10257 -10157  -9644  -9232  -9291

For clarity, these are not floating solver values. Here is a complete
procedure specifying how to check the inequalities using only integer
arithmetic, with no search or black-box solver. Use D=10^18, representing
each enclosing interval by integers[l,u] for[l/D,u/D]. Constants k use
[kD,kD]. For each elementary operation round outwards immediately:

* Addition: [l1+l2,u1+u2]. Negation: [-u,-l].
* Multiplication: compute the four products l1*l2,l1*u2,u1*l2,u1*u2;
  divide the minimum by D and round down, and the maximum by D and round up.
* Division, with the denominator interval disjoint from0: take the minimum
  and maximum of the four endpoint quotients (a*D)/b, rounded down and up.
* Square root of a nonnegative interval: lower endpoint floor(sqrt(lD));
  upper endpoint ceil(sqrt(uD)), determined by integer squaring.

These operations enclose their real counterparts: each expression is
continuous on its interval and each extremum is at the indicated endpoint
or, for sqrt, follows from monotonicity. This proves containment by
induction through the defining expressions(1),(2),(5)-(15).

The evaluation order is fixed. Start with sqrt2, then a,b,rho,eps,Klen,z,
m,eta and the exact vectors(1)-(2); use E_i from the explicit positive
formulas. Compute r_0,alpha_0,B(W),B(p), and the eight X,G,Y via(7)-(9).
Then compute B(w),B(P),alpha_1,C, and the two expressions(15), normalize
by their strictly positive energies, and round their upper endpoints
upwards to the grid1/10000. This gives exactly the two displayed tables.
In particular every required normalized cross row is at most-2/10000.

Some intermediate intervals, also with common denominator D, are

    sqrt2:  [1414213562373095048,1414213562373095049]
    a:      [1412734726326775199,1412734726326775201]
    b:      [2004379028329949201,2004379028329949202]
    eta:    [ 416570168687622134, 416570168687622136]
    alpha0: [ 414446744233737068, 414446744233737076]
    alpha1: [ 414680057364296474, 414680057364296582].

Every division interval is disjoint from0. The full interval endpoints
for all96 rows and a literal implementation of the above operations are
public in max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.json and
max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.py. The fixed calculation completed
in0.016976418999547604 seconds. Its logical purpose is the finite list of
rational inequalities displayed here; it is not a numerical optimality
certificate and not a substitute for verifier acceptance of this draft.

There are128 cross rows including the32 identically zero residual-column
rows. The128 within-child rows including diagonals and the33 optimum
incidences/diagonal give289 total original rows including diagonals.
This accounting includes every old/old pair, bridge pair, both optimum
incidences, and both directions at the final sample.

## 5. The quantitative signed-state law and the precise next gap

The transverse repair does not alter the endpoint norm multiplication,
but it changes the signed boundary pairings. The exact update can be
stated for any legal endpoint-saturated child with initial P, endpoint w,
actual vectors G_i,Y_i, and E_i=<G_i,Y_i>. Choose a real transverse
contraction C in the specified frames and scale alpha=r*||w||/R, using
(11),(14). For the parent P'=L(P), w'=alpha*Rmap(w):

    <P',G'_i>=<P,G_i>,       <P',Y'_i>=<P,Y_i>,
    <G'_i,w'>=B_C(G_i,w),   <Y'_i,w'>=B_C(Y_i,w),        (17L)

    <P',G'_(M+i)>=B_C(P,G_i),
    <P',Y'_(M+i)>=B_C(P,Y_i),
    <G'_(M+i),w'>=alpha^2*<G_i,w>,
    <Y'_(M+i),w'>=alpha^2*<Y_i,w>.                      (17R)

Here M is the number of child samples in this section. The energy list
updates to(E,alpha^2*E), the initial
radius stays R by the retained cone representation, and the endpoint
norm becomes alpha^2*||w||^2. For r=theta*A and A'=lambda*A,

    kappa'=kappa^2.                                     (18)

Every sign and transverse term in(17) is retained. The exact legality
test remains the full inequalities(15). The four scalar lists alone do
not determine the transverse vectors, so discarding them would lose the
mechanism that makes the present repair possible.

The construction gives C=0 in the first2-by2 transverse space and the
explicit4-by4 C in(10) for the next join. The resulting actual child now
has15 steps and lives in R^9. Its next balanced parent would have31 steps,
bridge1+theta*A_2, and an8-by8 transverse block. Producing such a block
with all original inequalities, and then a uniform all-depth rule, remain
open. No n>15 computation was run or authorized.

Two naive continuations do not constitute a proof. Setting all future
C=0 discards exactly the transverse terms that repaired the earlier
queried row(2,9). Padding(10) with zero rows and columns preserves the
contraction bound but does not establish the new cross-normal inequalities
because the frames and signed vectors have changed. Neither a verified
31-step parent nor an exact obstruction to all its transverse choices is
claimed. Equations(17)-(18) state the interface that a further construction
has to preserve, rather than treating a finite coupling as an asymptotic
iteration theorem.

## 6. Scope, independent residual proposal, and public evidence

The new data here have x_end=g_end and a three-vector terminal gradient;
they are NOT the two-vector orthogonal seed excluded by the older
63-step all-earlier-coupling separator. The finite fifteen-step repair
therefore does not contradict that scoped earlier obstruction.

A separate joint candidate,
max_nonzero_terminal_residual_and_angle_obstruction.md, changes the
terminal gradient to rho*V+(eps/2)*q_axis and pays a terminal value loss.
It constructs an actual7-step parent but excludes every next coupling of
those fixed children by four original rows. That different value/vector
prescription must not be substituted into the present finite success or
used to declare this original fixed-child problem infeasible.

All words in this manuscript retain the mandated diagnostic schedule.
The quantities A,A_1,A_2 are actual shifted word sums, not the exact
Zhang-Jiang objective profile B_n. Neither(16) nor(18) resolves the original
all-h/all-horizon comparison. The present deliverable is an explicit
finite common-function coupling and its complete unverified proof.

Public evidence lives under the shared project research directory:

* max_changed_geometry15_discovery.py: the baseline and free-block source.
* max_changed_geometry15_rational_replay.py: the fixed rational-block source.
* max_changed_geometry15_raw/attempt_01,02,03.npz and matching .json files:
  original statuses, all Q arrays, full contraction blocks and dual arrays.
* max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.py and .json: exact defining data,
  outward-integer procedure and all96 rational interval enclosures.
* max_changed_geometry15_discovery_budget.json: pre-recorded attempts,
  final charged count4, actual optimizer count3 and exact-replay count1.
* max_changed_geometry15_prior_handoff.json: hashes of the copied earlier
  sources and all three old raw cases; no old solver was rerun.

No optimization status is used as a proof premise, and no new verified
fact is claimed until the independent fact_submit gate is authorized and
accepts a self-contained submission.


# Part II. Complete next input and actual thirty-one-step problem

Status: UNVERIFIED joint derivation. The preceding fifteen-step proof is
included in full so none of its unverified identities is a cited premise.
In this part write Z_i=x'_i, D_i=g'_i, V_i=y'_i, v_i=f'_i for 0<=i<=15,
and p2=Z_0, w2=D_15=Z_15, E2_i=<D_i,V_i>. These symbols refer EXACTLY to
(1)-(13) in Part I, including the positive symmetric H in (11).
Their coordinate space is R^9. In particular no alternate square root,
rotation, padded old cross map, or different residual child is implicit.
The input word is h[2] and its shifted sum is A2=lambda^2 A.
The full data are (Z,D,V,v), not merely the four boundary pairings.

Use the Householder frames B(w2), B(p2) from (6); at a unit axis equal to
e1 use the identity. Put B(w2)q=(q_l0,q_lT) and
B(p2)q=(q_r0,q_rT), with tails in R^8. The next bridge is

 r2=theta*A2=lambda*A, alpha2=r2*||w2||/R,
 h[3]=(h[2],1+r2,h[2]), A3=1+sum h[3]=lambda^3 A.

The free variable C2 is the ENTIRE real 8-by-8 matrix. Its full original
contraction condition is

 M(C2)=[[I8,C2],[C2^T,I8]] >= 0.

Indeed its quadratic form on (u,v) is
||u+C2 v||^2+v^T(I8-C2^T C2)v. Thus this condition is equivalent to
I8-C2^T C2>=0. The positive symmetric square root H2 of that matrix
exists, including singular boundary choices. The actual maps to R^17 are

 L2(q)=(q_l0,q_lT,0),
 R2map(q)=(-q_r0,C2 q_rT,H2 q_rT).

Both are isometries. Conversely any two child isometries into ANY real
Euclidean space satisfying the mandatory bridge R2map(p2)/R=-L2(w2)/||w2||
have mutually perpendicular axial/transverse pairings and transverse
cross matrix C2 of norm at most1 by Cauchy--Schwarz. Hence searching all
C2 with M(C2)>=0 loses no actual fixed-child isometric coupling.

For 0<=i<=15 set

 (z_i,d_i,t_i,phi_i)=(L2 Z_i,L2 D_i,L2 V_i,v_i),
 (z_(16+i),d_(16+i),t_(16+i),phi_(16+i))
  =(alpha2 R2map Z_i,alpha2 R2map D_i,
    alpha2 R2map V_i,alpha2^2 v_i).

The bridge z_16=z_15-(1+r2)d_15 follows because z_15=d_15=L2(w2).
Every other update is an isometric/scaled original update. All values
are retained, including both bridge samples15,16 and final sample31.
Define

 T_C(u,v)=alpha2[-u_l0 v_r0+u_lT^T C2 v_rT].

The ORIGINAL residual is Q_ij=<d_i-d_j,t_j>. The COMPLETE row list is

 Q_ij=<D_i-D_j,V_j>,                             0<=i,j<=15,
 Q_(16+i,16+j)=alpha2^2<D_i-D_j,V_j>,            0<=i,j<=15,
 Q_(i,16+j)=T_C(D_i,V_j)-alpha2^2 E2_j,          0<=i,j<=15,
 Q_(16+i,j)=T_C(V_j,D_i)-E2_j,                   0<=i,j<=15,
 Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-<d_i,t_i>, Q_starstar=0.

No original row is deleted. There are512 within-child rows,512 cross rows,
and65 optimum rows including its diagonal, totaling1089. Child residuals
vanish exactly at3,7,11,15. Consequently128 cross rows are identically0;
all other384 cross rows have positive receiving energy. All within-child
and optimum rows are already covered by the included joint Part I proof.
The remaining problem is exactly M(C2)>=0 and the512 cross inequalities.

If a C2 satisfies these rows, K=conv(0,d_0,...,d_31) and
F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2) is one finite convex1-smooth function
realizing the entire31-step trajectory. The argument establishing its
smoothness and interpolation is in Part I section1 and applies to this
full set without change of any prescribed jet. The retained positive-cone
identity p2=m D_0+a D_2+D_3 survives L2. Thus0 is a closest minimizer,
and the TRUE initial distance remains R, with no extra normalization.
The full-word normalized final objective would then be

 2F(z_31)/dist(z_0,argmin F)^2
   =alpha2^2*||w2||^2/R^2=kappa^8/A3^2.

Its ratio to the ORIGINAL exact benchmark is

 B_31*kappa^8/A3^2,  B_31=2 S_bullet(31)+1,

where S_bullet is the exact recurrence in accepted b98bb807d514e80d.
No assertion A3^2=B_31, no final tail lemma, and no asymptotic conclusion
is contained in this conditional expression. For a depth-j finite legal
iteration the corresponding expression is B_(4*2^j-1)*kappa^(2^j)/
(lambda^j A)^2. Legality still needs proof at every chosen depth. Seeds
may depend on the target depth, but must be fixed within the one function
for that run. That distinction does not repair a failed fixed-child C2.

included self-contained construction in Part I. Thus ALL1089 original
rows, including both bridge indices and the last sample, are satisfied.

For a completely specified exact enclosure calculation use the interval
operations in Part I section4, with common denominator10^24. The only
new operation is the positive symmetric square root H of I4-C15^T C15
needed to form the actual15 input coordinates. Put D15=C15^T C15. Its
operator norm is at most934/10000<1/10. The power series

 H=I4-sum_(k>=1)c_k D15^k,
 c_1=1/2, c_(k+1)=c_k(2k-1)/(2k+2)

follows by the scalar binomial series applied to a real orthogonal
diagonalization of the symmetric positive-semidefinite matrix D15.
All c_k lie in(0,1). After20 terms the operator norm of the omitted tail
is at most sum_(k>=21)(1/10)^k=1/(9*10^20). The same number bounds
every entry of the tail. Evaluate the20 matrix powers using outward
integer arithmetic, then expand each entry by this rational radius.
This encloses the SPECIFIED positive symmetric H, rather than replacing
it by a different Gram square root.

Evaluate the exact seed, zero-map7 child, rational-map15 child and all
31 cross expressions in the order in Part I and Part II. This gives the
tables above. The same calculation also reproduces the Part I15 tables
and checks all24 nonzero7 cross rows. Every denominator interval is
separated from0; zero residual vectors are kept as exact zero vectors.
The interval code is max_changed_geometry31_exact_replay.py and the
complete endpoints, all frames and actual15 input arrays are in
max_changed_geometry31_exact_replay.json. This is a tiny fixed integer
inequality calculation, not a solver-based mathematical assertion.

Therefore the formula for K and F in Part II yields one actual globally
convex1-smooth function in R^17 whose GD run is exactly h[3], whose
prescribed function values are unchanged, and whose closest-minimizer
distance is R. Its full-word normalized objective is exactly

 kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2,

and its ratio to the exact objective benchmark is exactly

 B_31*kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2.

The original conjecture concerns arbitrary h and all sufficiently large
n. No identification of B_31 with shifted word mass is made here, and
