## A quantitative family of legal couplings on this same actual input

This finite result is robust with a completely explicit radius. Every
real8-by8 perturbation Delta with ||Delta||_op<=1/2000 may be added to
the displayed C2 and remains legal for the SAME actual15 input.
Here is a direct bound, avoiding any unpriced coordinate.

The seed estimates give max_i||g_i||<=1, max_i||y_i||<3,
min_(e_i>0)e_i>1/2 and R<4. Each of the three right scales lies between
2/5 and1/2, as their exact formulas and the enclosing arithmetic show.
Consequently the actual15 child has max_i||D_i||<=1,
max_i||V_i||<3 and min_(E2_i>0)E2_i>(1/2)(2/5)^4=8/625.
For the normalized left-to-right rows the perturbation is bounded by

 ||D_i||*||V_j||*||Delta||/(alpha2 E2_j)
   < (9375/16)||Delta|| <600||Delta||.

For the other direction it is bounded by

 alpha2||D_i||*||V_j||*||Delta||/E2_j
   < (1875/16)||Delta|| <120||Delta||.

Thus all normalized nonzero rows are at most
-4037/10000+600/2000=-1037/10000<0.
Also ||C2+Delta||<1/4+1/2000<1, so the real contraction condition holds.
The old and optimum rows are unchanged. This establishes an explicit
parameterized finite family, not merely the floating optimizer.
It is NOT an invariant region under another join: the new full signed
state, all norms and receiving energies must still be transported.


# Part IV. Parameterized angular continuation obstruction

# Angular limits of the actual three-vector continuation

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate, max22. No63-step or127-step
numerical problem is evaluated. This manuscript is appended to the complete
seed/7/15/31 construction in max_actual31_parent_problem.md for the joint
submission candidate. Its inequalities stand on direct definitions and the
original Q convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, not on an unverified fact.

The concrete seed has

 s=sqrt2, lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
 a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600, eps=1/100,
 K=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/K, A=1+2a+b,
 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W), x=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W),
 f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, p=x_0,
 R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz+eps^2, N=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+eps^2.

The word is(a,b,a), the last residual is0, and the penultimate residual is
y_2=(a-1)V+W. The companion joint construction supplies every original
seed row and its global realizing function, then reconstructs the actual
zero-map7, rational-map15 and rational-map31 children. Here we prove a
new obstruction applying to this ACTUAL zero-residual three-vector data.

## 1. A parameterized necessary condition for every next cross map

Suppose a left child's last leaf is beta times an isometric copy J of the
displayed seed, beta>0. Thus its endpoint and penultimate residual are

 w_L=beta J(W), y_L=beta J(y_2),
 e=<V,y_2>=(a+rho-1)eta>0, E_L=beta^2 e.

Let a right child have initial point p_R, R_R=||p_R||>0, and let v_R be a
convex combination of its retained gradients. Define

 q=-<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2,
 T_R=||v_R-(<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2)p_R||.

For a bridge excess r>0, the scale is alpha=r||w_L||/R_R. Let L and Rmap
be ANY child isometries into ANY real Euclidean space satisfying
alpha Rmap(p_R)=-r L(w_L). All preceding cross maps may be different.
Put h_angle=<W,y_2>=rho(a+rho-1)eta+eps^2. Orthogonal decomposition gives

 y_2=(h_angle/N)W+t, <t,W>=0,
 ||t||=(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N).                            (1)

Indeed y_2=(a-1)V+W and
||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta eps^2/N. Here a>1 and eps>=0.
The exact bridge forces

 <alpha Rmap(v_R),L(w_L)>=r beta^2 N q.

The component of alpha Rmap(v_R) perpendicular to L(w_L) has norm
alpha T_R, because Rmap preserves inner products and takes the p_R-axis
to the negative w_L-axis. Its inner product with beta L J(t) is at least
-alpha beta T_R||t|| by Cauchy--Schwarz. Hence

 <alpha Rmap(v_R),beta L J(y_2)>-beta^2 e
    >= beta^2 e(r H-1),                               (2)
 H=(h_angle/e)q-((a-1)eps sqrt(eta)/(R_R e))T_R.               (3)

Because the weights of v_R sum to1, the left side is EXACTLY their
convex combination of ORIGINAL Q rows receiving the left penultimate
sample. All values and the optimum are retained. Thus every legal parent
must satisfy r H<=1 when H>0. The nonzero terminal angle is fully charged
by the second term of(3); it has not been discarded by setting eps=0.

For identical balanced levels A_j=lambda^j A and r_j=theta A_j, this is
theta lambda^j A H_j<=1. Any horizon-dependent seed family retaining
these anchors must therefore force H_j=O(1/r_j) or leave this geometry.

## 2. The actual31 input has no63-step isometric-copy parent

Every later left embedding retains the initial point P and gradient G_4
of the first7 child. Its first transverse map was exactly zero. The
cross pairing in that first join is

 <L0(u),alpha0 R0(v)>=-r0<u,W><p,v>/R^2,
 r0=theta A, alpha0=r0 sqrt(N)/R.

Consequently, writing D=<p,W>=rho(b-1)+eps^2,

 <P,G_4>=-r0 D m/R^2, ||G_4||=alpha0.

Use the right child's retained gradient4 alone as v_R in(3). Then

 q=r0 D m/R^4, T_R<=alpha0.                            (4)

The left child's last leaf still has(1), no matter how its15 and31
couplings were chosen. At the63-step parent the original row in(2)
is Q_(36,30).

The following rational estimates are uniform over the parameter range

 7/5<a<283/200, 2<=b<401/200,
 99/100<rho<=1, 0<=eps<=1/100,
 eta=(b-1)/(a+rho).                                    (5)

The actual seed belongs to(5); 41/29<sqrt2<99/70, proved by squaring,
gives its a,b bounds. Within(5) one has

 29/5<A<1167/200<6, 17/5<m<171/50,
 239/100<K<483/200, 1<=z<201/200,
 2/5<eta<201/478, e=(K-1)eta>1/2,
 R^2=m^2+Kz+eps^2<564943/40000<71/5,
 R>17/5>3, N<421/1000<4/9, D>99/100.

For N use rho<=1 and201/478+1/10000<421/1000. Since
12/29<theta<5/12, these bounds also give

 12/5<r0<5/2,
 alpha0<[(5/2)(2/3)]/(17/5)=25/51<1/2.

Equation(4) implies

 q>[(12/5)(99/100)(17/5)]/(71/5)^2
   =5049/126025>1/25.

Since h_angle/e=rho+eps^2/e>99/100, the positive term in(3) is greater than
99/2500. The subtracted term is less than
[(1/2)(1/100)*1*(1/2)]/[3*(1/2)]=1/600. Therefore
H>99/2500-1/600>3/80.

For a31-step child the next bridge excess is
r3=theta lambda^3 A=lambda^2 A>(29/5)^2=841/25. Thus every possible
next transverse map and every ambient dimension obey

 Q_(36,30)/E_L>(841/25)(3/80)-1=523/2000>0.             (6)

This excludes the actual rational31 child's63-step parent. It also
allows arbitrary different15 and31 maps in the two children, provided
the fixed zero-map7 child and the same last leaf are retained. It does
not transfer the obstruction from a different residual family. The
successful31 construction and(6) concern different next-parent depths.

## 3. Different first maps and nonidentical horizon-dependent leaves

Changing the first map removes(4), so(6) must not be asserted for that
larger class. Instead a convex combination of three retained gradients
produces another uniform obstruction at a later finite depth.

Allow each three-step leaf to have its OWN parameters in(5), its own
seed vectors, and the displayed legal projection jets and values.
Build balanced binary concatenations with arbitrary child isometries.
For masses A_L,A_R prescribe a source-matched bridge by

 r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R, r>0,
 A_parent=A_L+A_R+r.                                  (7)

Its scale is r||w_L||/R_R and all child jets are retained. Identical
masses give r=theta A_L and A_parent=lambda A_L. There is no reset,
extra coordinate, changed inherited value or deleted terminal gradient
in this defined family.

In the RIGHT child of a proposed new join consider its first two leaves.
The first leaf has initial point p0, endpoint W0 and initial norm R_R.
Let the second leaf's parameters be a_b,b_b, with A_b=1+2a_b+b_b.
The first bridge gives the EXACT vector relation

 (a_b+b_b)G_4+a_b G_6+G_7=-r_first W0.

After dividing by A_b its positive coefficients sum to1. The resulting
convex combination is

 v_R=-tau W0, tau=r_first/A_b.                        (8)

All subsequent left embeddings retain(8), its norm and its pairing with
the initial point. It is independent of the first transverse map.
Both first-leaf masses are in(29/5,1167/200), so their ratio is between
14/15 and3/2. Substitution r=tau A_b in(7) gives

 A_b tau^2+(A_first+A_b)tau-A_first=0.

This increasing polynomial has negative value at2/5 and positive value
at1/2, using those two mass-ratio bounds. Therefore2/5<tau<1/2, and(8)
implies

 q=tau<p0,W0>/R_R^2>99/3550,
 T_R<=||v_R||=tau||W0||<1/3.

The LEFT child's final leaf can have different parameters in(5).
For that leaf h_angle/e>99/100, while the negative term in(3) is less than
[(1/2)(1/100)*1*(1/3)]/[3*(1/2)]=1/900. Hence

 H>9801/355000-1/900>1/40.                             (9)

The last inequality follows from9801*1800>47*355000. This bound is
uniform over all the leaf choices, all the earlier maps and dimensions.
For this larger class a legal new parent must have r<40.

The large bridge is forced at a fixed balanced depth. Equation(7)
implies0<r<min(A_L,A_R). Implicit differentiation yields
partial r/partial A_L=(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0, and the analogous right
derivative is positive. The mass join is increasing as well. Induction
therefore places every depth-j mass above lambda^j(29/5). The join of
two depth4 children (each63 steps) has

 r>theta lambda^4(29/5)=lambda^3(29/5)>80.

Equations(2),(9) make the weighted original-row sum greater than E_L>0.
At this127-step parent the rows are Q_(68,62), Q_(70,62), Q_(71,62), with
the positive weights from(8). No such parent is legal.

Thus even nonidentical boxed leaves chosen separately for the full
target horizon, with completely new maps at EVERY internal node, cannot
iterate this source-matched family indefinitely. This respects the
original non-anytime quantifiers: the parameters may depend on the full
schedule and target horizon; the constant1/40 is uniform over(5).
Changing the leaf angles outside(5), changing the actual atomic vectors,
using longer atomic trajectories, or a rule that does not retain these
anchors lies outside the result. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h).

## 4. The true31 objective and its exact benchmark comparison

The companion full31 construction has true-distance normalized gap
kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2, kappa=A^2 N/R^2. It is a legal positive witness,
but its exact benchmark ratio is less than1/4, as follows analytically.
The bounds from(5) give

 A<1167/200, N<421/1000,
 R^2>(17/5)^2+239/100=279/20,
 kappa<573355269/558000000<21/20.

Moreover(21/20)^8<3/2 since2*37822859361<3*25600000000.
Since lambda>12/5 and A>29/5, lambda^3 A>50112/625>80.
Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives, for p_exp=log_2 3,

 B_31<=1+4*31^p_exp<1+4*32^p_exp=973.

Therefore the actual full-word witness ratio satisfies

 B_31*kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2
   <973*(3/2)/6400=2919/12800<1/4.                    (10)

This bounds ONLY THIS WITNESS, not the supremum W_31(h[3]). No objective
tail was appended, and no boundary-sign hypothesis for a tail was
silently imported. A new continuation must alter a substantive geometric
ingredient and independently account for any final function-gap
conversion against the exact B_n sequence.


# Part V. Larger angles and their exact surplus cost

# Legality prices larger terminal angles

Status: UNVERIFIED joint continuation of the actual31 construction and its
angular obstruction. This is a textual proof, with no new optimizer or
fixed-candidate replay. Its purpose is to distinguish changing a seed
parameter from changing the actual geometric mechanism.

## 1. The legal three-vector seed family

Take

 353/250<a<1413/1000, 501/250<b<401/200,
 0<=rho<=1, eps>=0, E=eps^2,
 z=b-1, K=a+rho, eta=z/K, m=a+b, A=1+2a+b,
 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W), x=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W),
 y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The actual fixed a,b from the rational31 construction lie strictly in
this range. Its slight width also permits different leaf words.
Put e=(K-1)eta, R^2=m^2+Kz+E, N=rho^2 eta+E and
kappa=A^2 N/R^2.

These exact projection jets satisfy every original interpolation row
with the optimum0 if and only if

 0<=E<=(1-rho)e.                                      (11)

To see this, residuals are
y0=(m-1)U+KV+eps e3, y1=zU+KV+eps e3,
y2=(K-1)V+eps e3, y3=0, with energies m-1,z,e,0.
Here e3=(0,0,1) is a coordinate vector. At residual0 the nontrivial
gradient vertices0,V,W give normals-(m-1),-a,-a-(1-rho)z+E;
at residual1 they give-z,0,-(1-rho)z+E;
at residual2 the vertices0,U,W give-e,-e,-(1-rho)e+E.
All remaining normals vanish. Since0<e<z and rho<=1, (11) is necessary
from the last displayed row and sufficient for every listed row.
As in the complete joint construction, the convex hull of0 and these
gradients realizes the data by its quadratically regularized support
function, and p=mU+aV+W makes0 a closest minimizer. Thus R is the true
distance, and kappa is the actual core gradient-energy ratio.

## 2. Positive surplus forces rho>19/20

Set Delta=A^2 N-R^2. The coefficient of E in Delta is A^2-1>0.
Using the largest legal E=(1-rho)e bounds every legal Delta above by
Delta_max(rho), where direct expansion yields

 (a+rho)Delta_max(rho)=Dlin(rho),
 Dlin(rho)=z[A^2((a-1)+(2-a)rho)
                -(a^2+a-1+(a+2)rho)]-m^2(a+rho).       (12)

This expression is affine in rho. Its slope is
z[A^2(2-a)-(a+2)]-m^2, which is strictly positive: using
A>29/5, 2-a>1/2, a+2<7/2, z>1, m<171/50 gives the lower bound

 (29/5)^2/2-7/2-(171/50)^2>0.

At rho=19/20, the bracket in(12) is

 B=A^2(9/10+a/20)-(a^2+39a/20+9/10).

Here A<5831/1000, so A^2<34001/1000 and
A^2(9/10+a/20)<33004/1000. Also a>353/250 gives
a^2+39a/20+9/10>5647/1000. Thus B<27357/1000.
If B is nonpositive then Dlin(19/20)<0 directly. If B is positive,
z<201/200 gives z B<27494/1000. On the other hand
m>427/125, m^2>11669/1000, a+19/20>1181/500, so
m^2(a+19/20)>27562/1000. In either case

 Dlin(19/20)<-68/1000.                                (13)

The positive slope of Dlin therefore implies Delta<0 for rho<=19/20.
In particular, any legal leaf with kappa>=1 must have rho>19/20.
This deduction used the FULL terminal normal constraint(11), including
the positive normal charge E, rather than treating the extra coordinate
as a free norm increase.

There is a uniform cost on the excluded side. For rho<=19/20,
a+rho<5/2, so(12)-(13) give Delta<-17/625. All legal leaves satisfy
R^2<16: m<171/50, K<483/200, z<201/200, E<=e<z imply this bound.
Hence

 rho<=19/20 ==> kappa<1-17/10000=9983/10000.            (14)

## 3. A uniform angular obstruction for every nonnegative-surplus leaf

Allow nonidentical legal leaves with the a,b range above. Assume for
now that each leaf has rho>19/20, which is forced if each has kappa>=1.
Keep the actual isometric source-matched joins: for child masses A_L,A_R
the bridge excess r is the positive solution of
r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R, and the scale is r||w_L||/R_R.

In any right child with at least two leaves, the first bridge gives a
convex combination v_R=-tau W0 of its gradients4,6,7, where W0 belongs
to its first leaf and2/5<tau<1/2. The coefficients are the second leaf's
(a_b+b_b,a_b,1) divided by its mass1+2a_b+b_b. Their source identity,
and the range of tau, follow from the exact bridge and the mass bounds
29/5<A_leaf<1167/200. These facts are unchanged by EVERY preceding map.

For the first and last leaf data separately, (11) and rho>19/20 imply

 R^2<71/5, R>17/5,
 D=<p,W>=rho z+E>19/20,
 K-1>4/3, e=(K-1)eta>1/2,
 eta<43/100, E<201/4000, N<49/100.

For example R^2<(171/50)^2+(483/200)(201/200)+201/4000<71/5.
Also eps sqrt(eta)/e<=sqrt((1-rho)/(K-1))<sqrt(3/80)<1/5.

We give the original-row argument again for completeness. If the final
left leaf has scale beta, its endpoint is beta J(W) and penultimate
residual beta J(y2), where y2=(a-1)V+W. Put h_angle=<W,y2> and
q=-<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2, T_R=||v_R-(<p_R,v_R>/R_R^2)p_R||.
The new bridge sends the p_R-axis to the negative left endpoint-axis.
The axial component of the weighted cross normal is beta^2 r h_angle q.
The perpendicular component is at least
-beta^2 r(a-1)eps sqrt(eta)T_R/R_R, because the part of y2 perpendicular
to W has norm(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N), and the right gradient combination's
perpendicular norm is alpha T_R, alpha=r beta sqrt(N)/R_R.
Subtracting the receiving energy E_L=beta^2 e gives exactly

 sum weights Q >= E_L(r Gamma_ang-1),
 Gamma_ang=(h_angle/e)q
             -(a-1)eps sqrt(eta)T_R/(R_R e).           (15)

No interpolation row is deleted, and the positive weights sum to1.
Now q=tau D/R_R^2>19/710 and h_angle/e>19/20. Also
T_R/R_R<=tau sqrt(N0)/R_R<7/68, using N0<49/100. The previous estimate
on eps sqrt(eta)/e and a-1<1/2 prove

 Gamma_ang>361/14200-7/680>1/70.                       (16)

The last rational inequality is equivalent to361*4760>117*14200.
It remains valid when the right initial leaf and the left final leaf
are different, since all component bounds are uniform.

Balanced source-matched masses at depth j exceed lambda^j(29/5), by
monotonicity of the positive bridge root; partial r/partial A_L is
(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0 since r<min(A_L,A_R). Thus a join of two depth4
children (a127-step parent) has r>lambda^3(29/5)>80. Its weighted original
rows Q_(68,62),Q_(70,62),Q_(71,62) therefore obey

 sum weights Q/E_L>80/70-1=1/7>0.                     (17)

This excludes that parent even with nonidentical positive-surplus leaves,
arbitrary earlier maps and horizon-dependent rho,eps choices. More
precisely it excludes any such subtree all of whose leaves have rho>19/20.

## 4. Mixed leaves have a concrete, nonzero price

Every legal balanced depth-J tree, J>=5, in THIS same three-vector family
must therefore contain a leaf with rho<=19/20 inside each of its disjoint
depth5 subtrees. There are2^(J-5) such subtrees. Hence at least one leaf
in32 must incur the uniform core deficit(14). This is a necessary density
condition, not a claim that paying it is sufficient for interpolation.

The normalization cost is exact. For a legal node, its true initial
distance is R_L by the retained positive-cone identity, its endpoint norm
squared is r^2 N_L N_R/R_R^2, and A_parent*r=A_L A_R. Consequently

 kappa_parent=kappa_L*kappa_R,
 kappa_root=product_(all leaves)kappa_leaf.             (18)

No lost leaf factor may be omitted. If M_plus denotes the supremum of
kappa over this legal atomic family, the density condition gives

 kappa_root <= M_plus^(2^J-2^(J-5))
                  *(9983/10000)^(2^(J-5)).             (19)

The explicit actual seed in the full joint manuscript has kappa>1, so
M_plus>1; replacing any further deficit factors by M_plus preserves(19).
A zero endpoint makes kappa_root=0 and satisfies(19) directly; the axis
arguments only concern nonzero endpoint nodes.
For an elementary finite bound, R^2>m^2>11, A<6 and
N<=eta+e<1+z<3 imply M_plus<10. The sharper value is left open; the
coarse bound does not claim decay of(19). In particular this result does
NOT exclude all mixed-leaf policies. It identifies the exact normalization
cost that any such horizon-dependent escape must pay. The original
objective-profile target is still unresolved, including the conversion
from these core energies to a final function gap.

## DOC-000779 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max_arc_minimum_reset_obstruction.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/max_arc_minimum_reset_obstruction.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000779 -->
Exact local bottleneck in minimum-reset arc relays. Let one two-dimensional future cell start at a prescribed point p+D a, where p is a GLOBAL minimizer of the final one-envelope function, D>0, and a,b are orthonormal input/output axes. Its intended arc data have initial unscaled state r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q) and endpoint normal(epsilon,-1). Scaling by D rho and centering at
z_circ=p+D[1-rho(epsilon+P)]a+D rho(1+Q)b
makes the cell start at p+D a. Suppose its nonsmooth local piece is the nonnegative support maximum
Phi(z)=D rho max_(v in V)<v_a a+v_b b,z-z_circ>,
where V includes0 and(epsilon,-1). A maximum of such pieces can vanish at p only if THIS endpoint plane is nonpositive there. Its value before the positive D rho factor is
D[-epsilon+rho epsilon(epsilon+P)+rho(1+Q)].
Thus necessarily
rho <= 1/[epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon].
This is precisely the large A^arc budget in b70f11135ec8688e. It is invariant under rotating a,b or embedding in higher dimension. Passing from the origin to the true closest minimizer cancels this scale only at the first cell; it does not remove the subsequent requirement that an already visited proximal point be a minimizer.

Concrete failed mutation: align the next incoming axis with minus the preceding endpoint gradient and choose rho=1/(epsilon+P), so each center would advance only in a fresh orthogonal direction. At the preceding minimum point p, the new endpoint plane then has strictly positive value D^2 rho^2(1+Q)>0. Consequently the preceding proximal point ceases to be globally minimizing and its prescribed gradient is no longer certified. This is an ADJACENT future-plane failure, independent of all remote Markov comparisons. Adding rotations alone does not repair it.

To avoid this specific bottleneck, a new model must change at least one hypothesis: the checkpoint's proximal value may be positive (carry genuine function-value levels), the local support geometry may change, or the endpoint normal may change. Replacing the required reciprocal-scale bound by an unproved covariance choice would hide the same missing condition. No claim is made that positive-value constructions are impossible or that this obstructs the unrestricted target.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000779 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000780 -->
# Random-threshold Huber obstruction — unverified candidate

UNVERIFIED COMPLETE FAMILY CALCULATION. Normalize L=1 and initial distance1. For tau>0 define H_tau(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=tau and tau|x|-tau^2/2 otherwise, minimized at0. Its derivative clips x to [-tau,tau], hence is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, so the function is a legal smooth convex instance. It is even, so x_0=1 suffices for both normalized initial signs.

For a>=0 define M_a(z)=z-a clip(z,-1,1). Let D_n be the composition of these maps in chronological order 2^{n-1},2^{n-2},...,1, and put S_n=2^n-1. Claim D_n(z)=sign(z)(|z|-S_n)_+ for every real z. For n=1, M_1(z) has exactly this form. Suppose the identity holds at n-1 and set A=2^{n-1}, so S_{n-1}=A-1. The maps are odd, so take z>=0. If 0<=z<=1, M_A(z)=(1-A)z lies in[-(A-1),0]. If 1<=z<=2A-1, M_A(z)=z-A lies in[-(A-1),A-1]. The induction hypothesis sends both ranges to0. If z>2A-1, then M_A(z)=z-A>A-1, so D_{n-1}(M_A(z))=z-A-(A-1)=z-S_n. Oddness treats z<0. This proves the exact identity.

For the GD schedule d^(n)=(2^{n-1},...,1), rescale z=x/tau. The identity gives x_n=(1-tau S_n)_+. Write S=S_n. If tau>=1/S then x_n=0. For 0<tau<=1/(S+1), the endpoint is in the affine Huber region and
2H_tau(x_n)=2tau-(2S+1)tau^2<=1/(2S+1),
with equality at tau=1/(2S+1). For 1/(S+1)<=tau<=1/S, the endpoint lies in the quadratic region and
2H_tau(x_n)=(1-S tau)^2<=1/(S+1)^2<=1/(2S+1).
Consequently
sup_{tau>0} 2H_tau(x_n)=1/(2^{n+1}-1).
This holds for every n>=1. Thus for every law on tau (even one selected after seeing the entire binary schedule), its expected normalized error is at most1/(2^{n+1}-1). No minimax interchange is used: the deterministic pointwise upper bound is integrated.

Translations and orthogonal embeddings preserve the scalar trajectory and the normalization. Radial Hubers H_tau(||x-x_star||) have collinear GD trajectories, so the same identity holds for any starting displacement; after scaling its length to1 the parameter is tau/||x0-x_star||. A finite direct sum of these full-curvature scalar/radial components has a normalized error equal to the initial-squared-distance weighted average of component normalized errors. Hence it has the same cap; any distribution over these direct sums does too.

This is a limitation of full-curvature symmetric Huber thresholds, embeddings, and separable direct sums. It does not include arbitrary lower core curvatures, asymmetric tails, sums of overlapping ridge Hubers, or general convex functions. The schedule d^(n) is not claimed to have small unrestricted W_n; other legal functions can make it unstable. In particular this is not a negative answer to P. For a benchmark comparison one may use the already verified fact b98bb807d514e80d, which proves B_n=1/U_n^ZJ<=1+4n^{log_2 3}; the restricted-family ratio is consequently at most (1+4n^{log_2 3})/(2^{n+1}-1), tending to0.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000780 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000781 -->
# P5 retains inner returns that the outward-ray family omits
Status: new analytic candidate; not verifier-accepted. The only cited correctness bricks are facts 1fca3e5dd555cb4a and b98bb807d514e80d. This document gives a self-contained legal-function calculation, without relying on another unverified construction as a brick.

For n>=8 let h_t=(t+1)/2 for 0<=t<n. Set c=2/5 and define the global derivative
q(x)=x for x<=2/5,
q(x)=2/5 for 2/5<=x<=1,
q(x)=x-3/5 for x>=1.
Let f(x)=integral_0^x q(z)dz. The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with q(0)=0. Thus f is convex differentiable, 1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0. The initial point is1, so the normalized error is2f(x_n).

The exact first eight steps are
x_0=1, x_1=4/5, x_2=2/5, x_3=-1/5,
x_4=1/5, x_5=-3/10, x_6=3/5, x_7=-4/5, x_8=12/5.
Direct substitution into q and x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t q(x_t) verifies all these values.
In particular x_4 and x_6 return inside the previous positive-ray record1. An adversary restricted to successive outward ray records cannot follow this chronology.

For every t>=8 the signs alternate and |x_t|>=12/5. If x_t<0 then
|x_{t+1}|=((t-1)/2)|x_t| >= (t/4)|x_t|.
If x_t>0 then
x_{t+1}= -((t-1)/2)x_t+3(t+1)/10.
Using x_t>=12/5 gives
|x_{t+1}| >= [(t-1)/2-(t+1)/8]|x_t|
 =((3t-5)/8)|x_t| >= (t/4)|x_t|.
The last bound holds for t>=5; with t>=8 it also preserves the magnitude lower bound and proves the claimed alternating signs. Hence
|x_n| >= (12/5) product_{t=8}^{n-1}(t/4).

For x<0, 2f(x)=x^2. For x>=1,
2f(x)=(x-3/5)^2+12/25.
This is at least x^2/4: the difference is
(3/4)x^2-(6/5)x+21/25
=(3/4)(x-4/5)^2+9/25 >0.
Therefore the legal one-dimensional objective satisfies
W_n(h)>=2f(x_n)>=(36/25)[product_{t=8}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2.
The product is (n-1)!/[7! 4^(n-8)] for n>=8. This grows faster than any fixed power. No computation or exhaustive search is part of the proof.

## Membership in the concrete P5 action tree
The seed j=2 in P5 forces c=1/(1+h_0+h_1)=2/5, agreeing with the displayed first three queried gradients. Thereafter taking the signed steep envelope at every step reproduces q. Here is the direct envelope check, so this assertion is not a reliance on an unverified earlier finding.

The stored knots at(0,0),(2/5,2/5),(1,2/5) force the upper gradient envelope on the positive half-line to be exactly q there: the origin bounds it by x, the knot at2/5 bounds it by2/5 for smaller x and by x for larger x, and the knot at1 bounds it by2/5 below1 and by x-3/5 above1. Taking the minimum gives the displayed q. Every other positive queried knot lies on q, so cannot tighten this envelope below q. On the negative half-line the lower envelope supplied by the origin is x. All positive knots have v<=y, so v-(y-x)<=x and cannot raise it. Every negative knot on q(x)=x likewise preserves that envelope. Thus every subsequent signed steep query equals q(x_t), including the interior returns.

Consequently this single leaf is present in P5. Under the original fair law its probability is2^{-(n-3)}, which already leaves a diverging lower bound after multiplication by the factorial-square payoff. Under the separately derived power tilt its expected payoff is within2^{-1/n} of the maximum leaf; that probability repair is an unverified companion candidate, not needed for the present legal-function lower bound.

## Scope
Finding97c780b4975678c7 gives an unverified analytic argument that the accepted functional F_ray_n from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a collapses to K_n and is O(n^-2) on this same half-linear family. The present construction does not depend on that argument: it independently establishes a large legal objective and membership in the richer P5 tree. It shows that failing the outward-record restriction is not a reason to abandon scalar envelope branching.

No all-schedule comparison to U_n^ZJ is claimed. In particular an instability test on this family does not supply the still-missing inequality on schedules with bounded worst-case objective.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000782 -->
# P5: a concrete consistent branching policy
Status: complete support construction and two quantitative tests; unverified. No new verification allocation requested. The exact-profile comparison remains open.

## 1. Scalar interpolation state

Normalize L=1, choose minimizer0 and initial point1. A history H is a finite set of gradient knots (y,v), always including(0,0), satisfying
0 <= (v-w)/(y-z) <=1 whenever y>z, and v=w when y=z.
Define
ell_H(x)=max_{(y,v) in H} [v-(y-x)_+],
u_H(x)=min_{(y,v) in H} [v+(x-y)_+].
Every monotone1-Lipschitz derivative interpolating H lies between these envelopes. Conversely both envelopes are global monotone1-Lipschitz functions interpolating H: max/min preserve nondecreasingness and the upper bound1 on increments, and the pairwise history conditions force equality at each stored knot. In particular they agree at0 with value0. Thus ell_H<=u_H. Adding a knot(x,g) preserves the history conditions exactly when ell_H(x)<=g<=u_H(x).

For an endpoint x, the largest normalized objective among all convex1-smooth functions whose derivatives interpolate H and whose value at0 is0 is
Phi(H,x)=2 integral_0^x u_H(z) dz if x>=0,
Phi(H,x)=-2 integral_x^0 ell_H(z) dz if x<=0.
For the first case take derivative u_H globally, and for the second take derivative ell_H globally. These derivatives are continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz, vanish at0, and integrate to globally convex1-smooth functions minimized at0. The envelopes bound every possible derivative pointwise, which proves maximality with the appropriate integration orientation. The chosen objective therefore reproduces every stored gradient. It is piecewise quadratic with finitely many pieces and lives in dimension1.

This is a derivative-oracle construction: no function values were supplied to GD and none need to be preserved except the final chosen value. The whole h is fixed first. An adversary's random choices construct a function afterward whose exact gradient trajectory is that path; they do not change the GD steps adaptively.

## 2. Actual policy, not optimization over all gradient choices

Fix h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) with n>=1 and arbitrary finite nonnegative entries. For each j in{0,...,n-1}, put P_j=sum_{s<j}h_s and c=1/(1+P_j). Policy D_j first forces g_i=c for0<=i<=j. Its points are x_i=1-c sum_{s<i}h_s>=c for i<=j, so these knots are legal with(0,0). After step j,
x_{j+1}=c(1-h_j).
For every t=j+1,...,n-1 let S_t=sum_{s=t}^{n-1}h_s, let H_t be ALL prior knots together with(0,0), and compute
v_t = u_{H_t}(x_t) if x_t>=0, and ell_{H_t}(x_t) if x_t<0,
w_t = projection of x_t/(1+2S_t) onto [ell_{H_t}(x_t),u_{H_t}(x_t)].
With probability1/2 choose g_t=v_t; otherwise choose g_t=w_t. Coins at successive stages are independent. Add(x_t,g_t), then take the fixed GD step x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t g_t. Repeated points, zero steps and x_t=0 are harmless because their feasible intervals force the stored gradient.

After the final step select the global objective attaining Phi(H_n,x_n). This is a finite law on at most2^{n-j-1} legal functions, each with initial squared distance1. The expected normalized error D_j(h) has the exact recurrence
E_n(H,x)=Phi(H,x),
E_t(H,x)=(1/2)E_{t+1}(H union{(x,v)},x-h_t v)
        +(1/2)E_{t+1}(H union{(x,w)},x-h_t w),
with v,w defined by the displayed policy at state(H,x,t).
D_j(h) is E_{j+1} evaluated after its forced prefix. This recurrence is the payoff of two specified actions, not a supremum over all feasible gradients. It is an exact symbolic specification; no exponential enumeration has been run.

Each selected function is legal path by path. Thus W_n(h)>=D_j(h) for every j. Combined with the verified global/single-cut functional K_n from fact b98bb807d514e80d,
W_n(h)>=B_n^branch(h):=max{K_n(h), max_{0<=j<n}D_j(h)}.
One may choose the j with largest expectation after seeing h, or choose a point mass on a sufficiently good leaf. No infimum/expectation interchange is used. This notation B_n^branch is distinct from the benchmark denominator B_n.

## 3. Exact stopping event recovers the selected-cut value

Suppose h_j>1 and j<n-1. Write R=c(h_j-1)>0 and T=S_{j+1}. The first point on the negative half-line is -R, with no negative gradient knots yet. Therefore the feasible interval is[-R,0]. On its w-branch, the chosen gradient is -a, where a=R/(1+2T).
All later points remain <=-a, because total remaining displacement is aT and -R+aT=-a(1+T)<=-a.
The steep envelope at these points is -a. The later tail proposal is at most -a: if s is the tail sum already used, the ratio of its magnitude to a is
(1+2T-s)/(1+2T-2s)>=1.
Thus clamping selects -a. Both actions hence coincide thereafter, so this branch exactly follows an asymmetric Huber tail.
The terminal envelope on the negative half-line is max(z,-a); hence its normalized objective is
R^2/(1+2T)=(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T)].
The event has probability1/2, giving D_j(h) at least half of that cut term. At j=n-1 there is no coin; the maximal endpoint value is R^2, exactly the cut term with T=0.

This confirms normalization and chronology, but the half-cut bound alone is only a check: K_n already retains the full cut maximum. The benefit sought from D_j is in branches that continue through multiple sign changes and record excursions.

## 4. Quantitative multi-excursion test on the linear schedules

Take n>=4 and h_t=t+1. Choose j=2, so P_j=1+2=3 and c=1/4. The forced prefix has
x_0=1, x_1=3/4, x_2=1/4, x_3=-1/2.
On the event that every later coin takes the steep action, the trajectory is exactly GD on the function with derivative
q(z)=z for z<=1/4,
q(z)=1/4 for1/4<=z<=1,
q(z)=z-3/4 forz>=1.
This is a single global monotone1-Lipschitz derivative. Its derivative values equal the selected extremal envelope at every new query; adding such a knot does not change the pertinent extremal envelope. Positive knots cannot tighten the negative lower envelope beyond z, and negative knots on q(z)=z cannot tighten the positive upper envelope below min(z,1/4+(z-1)_+). Thus induction proves this entire event, not only its initial portion.
In particular x_4=3/2. Thereafter signs alternate and |x_t|>=3/2. For negative x_t the next magnitude is t|x_t|. For positive x_t, t>=4,
x_{t+1}=-t x_t+(3/4)(t+1)<0,
and
|x_{t+1}|>=((t-1)/2)|x_t|>=(t/4)|x_t|,
using |x_t|>=3/2. The last inequality and t>=4 preserve the lower bound3/2.
Therefore
|x_n| >= (3/2) product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4).
The chosen final envelope has objective at least that of the displayed global q. Integrating q shows 2f(z)=z^2 for negative z; for positive z>=3/2 it is (z-3/4)^2+3/8>=z^2/4. Hence every terminal point on this event satisfies
2f(x_n) >= (9/16) [product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2.
There are n-3 fair coins after the forced prefix, so
D_2(h) >= 2^{-(n-3)}(9/16)[product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2. (L)
The right side diverges faster than any fixed power of n. For example, from t>=8 each factor in the product is at least2, and the factorial expression gives the full growth:
product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)=(n-1)!/[6*4^{n-4}].
This analytic event calculation uses no numerical sweep or path enumeration.
By contrast, accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d proves K_n(h)<=16/n^2 for the same linear schedules. Thus this actual branching policy detects objective mass missed by every single-cut term on that family. It is a mechanism advance, not an all-schedule lower bound.

## 5. Guarded-long-step test

For h=(1,H,1), H>1, choose j=1. Then c=1/2 and the forced points are1,1/2,-(H-1)/2. On the final interior branch, probability1/2, the gradient is x_2/3 and the envelope is a left Huber tail. Its normalized error is (H-1)^2/12. Consequently
D_1(1,H,1)>=(H-1)^2/24.
This avoids the fixed-one-curvature annihilation that affected the previous two-curvature subclass. It does not imply any bound for arbitrary schedules by itself.

## 6. Remaining obligation and next adversarial tests

No proof presently compares B_n^branch(h) uniformly to U_n^ZJ. The statement
for every epsilon>0 there exists N_epsilon independent of h such that
B_n^branch(h)>=(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ for all n>=N_epsilon and h>=0
would by itself solve the affirmative direction; it cannot be called a support lemma.
The concrete next test is whether the fair-coin continuation loses nonvanishing mass on recursive schedules near the ZJ sum threshold, even when B_n^branch includes the exact K_n fallback. If it does, change the continuation probabilities or replace the scalar geometry, not merely append another restricted-family no-go. The successful linear/guarded tests do not establish that the comparison is true.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000782 -->

# P5 probability repair: finite power weighting
Status: unverified derivation. Companion to research/max_branching_policy.md. No new verifier request.

The fair law need not attain the strongest witness already in its support. At h=(3/2,19/10), seed j=0 has payoffs Q=81/400 (steep quadratic) and C=5/96 (tail Huber), whose fair average is611/4800. Seed j=1 gives81/625. Combined with K_2, the fair value is81/625<U_2^ZJ; see a2e802d2fcd0c0b5. This finite failure does not refute the asymptotic target, but it exposes a concrete probability loss.

Retain the exact legal prefix seeds and two-action tree of54496c04e3d34eb2. For seed j let m=n-j-1 be the number of coins, label the2^m paths by sigma, and let Z_sigma=Phi(H_n^sigma,x_n^sigma)>=0. Let M_j(h)=max_sigma Z_sigma. Put p=n^2 for n>=1. If every Z_sigma=0, set T_j(h)=0 and use any leaf. Otherwise choose the finite law
Pr_p(sigma)=Z_sigma^p / sum_tau Z_tau^p.
Each sampled leaf still defines a fixed globally legal one-dimensional objective with initial squared distance1. This changes only the probabilities in a specified finite family; it does not choose the schedule adaptively or optimize over arbitrary functions.

The exact expected normalized objective is
T_j(h)=sum_sigma Z_sigma^{p+1}/sum_sigma Z_sigma^p.
Define A_r=2^{-m}sum_sigma Z_sigma^r. Convexity of t -> t^{(p+1)/p}, applied to the numbers Z_sigma^p, gives
A_{p+1}>=A_p^{(p+1)/p}.
Therefore
T_j=A_{p+1}/A_p>=A_p^{1/p}>=M_j 2^{-m/p}>=M_j 2^{-1/n}.
Also T_j<=M_j because it is a convex combination. Thus
2^{-1/n} M_j(h)<=T_j(h)<=M_j(h)
uniformly over every finite nonnegative schedule and seed. The relative loss tends to zero with a threshold independent of h. This compares two quantities INSIDE the concrete policy family, not either quantity with the target profile.

An equivalent conditional sampling rule chooses a child in proportion to the sum of Z_sigma^p over its descendant leaves. Multiplication of conditional probabilities telescopes to the displayed leaf law. This is a finite mathematical specification; no exhaustive computation or enumeration has been run. The allowed adversary can depend on the entire predetermined h; complexity in the task measures GD iterations.

The repaired lower-bound functional is
B_n^tilt(h)=max{K_n(h),max_{0<=j<n}T_j(h)}.
Every T_j is the expectation of legal normalized instances; hence W_n(h)>=B_n^tilt(h). The K_n term is the already verified witness family from fact b98bb807d514e80d. A maximizing seed exists because there are n choices, and choosing a distribution or deterministic K_n witness after inspecting h incurs no mixture penalty and needs no minimax interchange.

At n=2 and h=(3/2,19/10), m=1,p=4, and M_0>=81/400. Hence T_0>81/500 because2^{1/4}<5/4. The exact benchmark recurrence gives S_bullet(2)>=3/2+sqrt3>3, so U_2^ZJ<1/7<81/500. Thus the repaired law detects the concrete fair-policy failure. On the linear schedules h_t=t+1 of the companion file, the large all-steep payoff is retained with factor at worst2^{-1/n}; its exponentially small fair probability no longer weakens this comparison.

This repairs probability weighting only. The unproved statement
for every epsilon>0 there exists N_epsilon independent of h such that
max{K_n(h),max_j M_j(h)}>=(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ
for all n>=N_epsilon and every h in[0,infinity)^n
is still a theorem-strength geometric comparison. It is BLOCKED until an actual inequality is found. If it were established, the displayed power-weight inequality would transfer it to a legal distribution with no asymptotic constant loss. No such geometric comparison has been proved here.

# A nonseparable max-affine Moreau relay with affine offsets
Status: complete new candidate, unverified. No additional verifier allocation requested.
Worker max; responds to TASK and guidance66447c4a378fcc32.

## 1. Explicit persistent-error family
Let m>=1 and choose arbitrary real b_0,...,b_(m-1) in[5,11/2].
Use the predetermined length2m schedule
h=(1,b_0,1,b_1,...,1,b_(m-1)).
In R^(m+1) take the standard orthonormal basis e_0,...,e_m, set
rho_i=1/(1+b_i) for0<=i<m,
w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)) for0<=i<m,
w_m=e_m/6,
and set eta=1/36. Define one fixed function
J(z)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=m)(<w_i,z>-eta)},
f(x)=min_z {J(z)+(1/2)||x-z||^2}.                           (1)
Start at x_0=e_0 and choose minimizer0. All quantities in(1) are fixed once h is known. This is not a sequence of independently changed objectives or Hessians.

The function J is finite nonnegative convex and J(0)=0. Its Moreau envelope f is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with f>=0,f(0)=0. To spell out the local certification used below, if w belongs to the subdifferential of J at z and x=z+w, then z is the unique minimizer in(1), grad f(x)=w, and f(x)=J(z)+||w||^2/2. The minimum is unique by the quadratic's strong convexity. Monotonicity of subgradients at two such pairs gives
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>,
which proves the required Lipschitz bound. The differentiability follows directly by comparing the unique minimizing values at x and x+v; the linear term is <x-z,v> and the remainder is bounded by a constant times||v||^2. Convexity follows from joint convexity in(x,z) and partial minimization.

## 2. Every supporting plane, not just adjacent interpolation
We claim that at block i's two queried points the gradient is w_i. At the block start it is e_i; after its first unit step it is e_i-w_i. These claims imply
e_i-(1+b_i)w_i=e_(i+1),                                  (2)
so induction gives the exact entire GD trajectory.

For either query write x=e_i-u w_i with u=0 or1 and z=x-w_i=e_i-(u+1)w_i. Let c=u+1, so1<=c<=2.
The i-th affine plane at z has value
<w_i,z>-eta=rho_i-2c rho_i^2-eta.
Here 2/13<=rho_i<=1/6. The function rho-4rho^2 is decreasing over this interval and its minimum is1/18. Thus the current plane's value is at least1/36>0.

The next plane, when i<m-1, has value
c rho_i rho_(i+1)-eta.
The current plane minus it is
rho_i[1-c(2rho_i+rho_(i+1))]>=0,
because c<=2 and every rho is at most1/6.
When i=m-1 the same formula uses1/6 in place of rho_(i+1), because the terminal plane is w_m=e_m/6, and the same bound holds.

The previous plane, when i>=1, has value
-rho_(i-1)[1-c rho_i]-eta<0.
Every other non-current plane has value -eta, since its two-coordinate support is disjoint from z. The zero plane has value0. Thus the current affine plane dominates EVERY stored plane. It follows that w_i belongs to the subdifferential of J at z. This proves the claimed gradients at both queries and hence(2), including all chronological interactions with old and future planes.

At x_(2m)=e_m set z=(5/6)e_m. The terminal plane has value
<w_m,z>-eta=5/36-1/36=1/9.
The only other possibly nonzero inner product is with w_(m-1), and it is negative. All remaining planes have value-eta, while the zero plane has value0. Therefore grad f(e_m)=w_m and
f(e_m)=1/9+(1/2)(1/36)=1/8.
The initial squared distance is1, so
W_(2m)(h)>=2f(e_m)=1/4.                                  (3)

For general smoothness L>0 use f_L=L f. The updates h_t/L then have exactly the same points, and the normalized objective remains1/4.

The intermediate objective values are also explicit:
f(e_i-u w_i)=rho_i-(2u+1)rho_i^2-eta, u=0,1.
Thus the construction supplies positions, gradients, values and a global function; it does not stop at a PSD matrix or local inequalities.

## 3. Actual growing dimension and nonseparability
The visited block-start points are the m+1 vectors e_0,...,e_m. The vectors w_0,...,w_m are linearly independent: their coordinate matrix is triangular with nonzero diagonal. Their Gram matrix is explicitly real PSD because they are the displayed Euclidean vectors. Its only off-diagonal entries at distinct stages are
<w_i,w_(i+1)>=-rho_i rho_(i+1) for i<m-1,
<w_(m-1),w_m>=-rho_(m-1)/6.
Each w_i is queried twice, so the temporal Gram matrix also has positive repeated-gradient correlations. Energies are2rho_i^2 on the relay stages and1/36 at the terminal point. They vary with the chosen b_i. In particular the accepted orthogonal-moment obstruction0e7e78056dc6258c does not apply.

The function cannot be written as a sum of univariate functions in any fixed orthonormal coordinates. Indeed linear independence of the w_i lets us choose, for any i, a point z with <w_i,z>=eta and <w_j,z><eta for every j!=i. In a neighborhood of z, J is exactly max{0,<w_i,z>-eta}. At x=z+theta w_i with0<theta<1 and sufficiently small surrounding perturbations, its envelope has Hessian
P_i=w_i w_i^T/||w_i||^2.
For two neighboring, nonparallel w_i,w_(i+1), their inner product is nonzero, so the rank-one projectors P_i and P_(i+1) do not commute. All Hessians of a function separable in a fixed orthonormal basis would be diagonal in that basis and would commute wherever they exist. This contradiction proves genuine nonseparability, not merely a rotated scalar witness or an orthogonal direct sum.

## 4. Why this schedule test is not just a large quadratic product
For any scalar quadratic f_lambda(x)=lambda x^2/2 with0<=lambda<=1, one block's multiplier is
p_b(lambda)=(1-lambda)(1-b lambda).
For0<=lambda<=1/6 and5<=b<=11/2, it is nonnegative and at most1-lambda<=exp(-lambda).
For1/6<=lambda<=1, its largest positive value is at most5/36, and its negative minimum has magnitude
(b-1)^2/(4b)<=81/88.
The latter expression increases with b>1, and its endpoint value at b=11/2 is81/88. These statements also follow by checking the vertex and endpoints of the quadratic p_b on the given interval.

Consequently the worst normalized objective over every scalar quadratic satisfies
max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_i p_(b_i)(lambda)^2
 <=max{1/(2 e m),(81/88)^(2m)} ->0.                       (4)
The first term comes from maximizing lambda exp(-2m lambda). A general finite-dimensional convex quadratic diagonalizes orthogonally, and its normalized error is a convex combination of these scalar errors; zero-curvature directions contribute no objective. Thus(4) controls the full quadratic class uniformly in the varying b_i, whereas(3) stays1/4 on the explicitly nonseparable nonlinear function.

This does not assert that scalar nonlinear witnesses fail on these schedules. The point is to give a concrete globally consistent variable-energy high-dimensional trajectory surviving mixed short/long steps even when every quadratic channel converges.

## 5. General block and offset extension
Here is the same candidate family for an arbitrary chronological partition
(block_0,b_0,...,block_(m-1),b_(m-1),block_m),
where s_i is the ordinary block sum, as in the project glossary. Put
M_i=s_i+b_i>0, rho_i=1/M_i for i<m, and choose rho_m>0.
Keep w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)), w_m=rho_m e_m, initial point e_0.
Replace the common offset eta in(1) by arbitrary nonnegative offsets eta_i.

Write c_i=s_i+1. The following EXPLICIT sufficient conditions certify the entire trajectory:
0<=eta_i<=rho_i-2c_i rho_i^2                         for i<m, (5)
eta_i-eta_(i+1)<=rho_i-c_i(2rho_i^2+rho_i rho_(i+1)) for i<m, (6)
0<=eta_m<=rho_m-(s_m+1)rho_m^2.                               (7)
The rho_(i+1) in the last instance of(6) is the terminal scalar rho_m.

During ordinary block i let u be the partial step sum already performed. Then0<=u<=s_i and the intended point is e_i-u w_i. The current plane at its proximal point is
rho_i-2(u+1)rho_i^2-eta_i>=0
by(5). Its difference from the next plane is nonnegative by(6), because the difference decreases with u. Every old adjacent plane has value
-rho_(i-1)[1-(u+1)rho_i]-eta_(i-1)<=0;
indeed(5) forces2c_i rho_i<=1 and hence the bracket is nonnegative. Farther old and future planes have values -eta_j<=0. The checkpoint query is u=s_i, and the full block plus checkpoint transfers exactly from e_i to e_(i+1). During the terminal block the points are (1-u rho_m)e_m; condition(7) makes the terminal plane nonnegative at its proximal points, and all old planes are nonpositive. These checks again retain every supporting plane.

The exact terminal normalized value is
2rho_m-(2s_m+1)rho_m^2-2eta_m.                              (8)
Thus(5)--(7) are a concrete sufficient-condition interface whose payoff is calculated, not an unproved minimax statement.

For fixed rho's one can test the least offsets with the finite recurrence
eta_0=0,
eta_(i+1)=max{0, eta_i+c_i(2rho_i^2+rho_i rho_(i+1))-rho_i}.  (9)
Any feasible nonnegative offset sequence dominates this one coordinatewise, by induction. Hence if(9) violates any upper capacity in(5) or(7), there is no offset sequence satisfying these sufficient conditions for the chosen partition and rho's. If it respects all capacities, it gives the largest payoff(8) among them. The weights rho_i=1/M_i are fixed by the exact coordinate transfers; this is not an arbitrary covariance optimization.

## 6. Exact hostile test and what must change next
For blocks consisting of one unit step followed by4, take the natural relay weights rho_i=1/5 for every nonterminal stage. At any pair of such consecutive stages, the increment in(9) is
2(2/25+1/25)-1/5=1/25,
whereas the capacity in(5) is
1/5-4/25=1/25.
Thus with three or more such nonterminal blocks, eta_2>=2/25 exceeds its capacity1/25. These comparisons are also necessary for the specified gradient sequence: the normals w_0,...,w_m are linearly independent, so w_i can belong to the convex hull of active normals and0 only if its own plane is active at x-w_i. Comparing that plane with0 and with the next plane forces(5) and(6). No nonnegative intercept choice repairs THIS equal-distance, constant-gradient relay on the whole repeated(1,4) schedule. Allowing arbitrary terminal weight does not repair the first three nonterminal blocks. This is a failure of the displayed model, not of all high-dimensional correlated constructions or of the target.

The structural cost is visible in(5): a phase with a fixed direction must support both its incoming and outgoing coordinate components throughout the entire ordinary block. A new mutation must change that within-block geometry (vary direction or energy, or retain nonzero past coordinate residuals), while retaining every old plane. Merely declaring(5)--(7) feasible for arbitrary schedules at the target payoff would be false in this example.

No comparison of this relay family to U_n^ZJ for arbitrary schedules is proved. Its first deliverable is the explicit nonseparable function, exact growing-dimensional trajectory, quantitative mixed-step test, and exact extension/failure interface.

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000785 -->
# Two distinct exact projection faces at the five-step source word

Status: **joint unverified proof draft**, cumulative round17. No optimization
or verifier call. This is a finite structural result, not a local/global
optimality claim and not the original asymptotic minimax theorem.

The normalized performance W_n and B_n are as in accepted facts
1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d. The proof below supplies its own
functions and all ordered-pair checks, rather than relying on an unverified
earlier finding.

## 1. Source word and one common construction convention

Write s=sqrt(2), theta=s-1, nu=theta^2=3-2s,
d0=sqrt(7+4s)-1, gamma=d0/4, and B0=(d0+2)^2. The source word is

    h0=(s,2,s,1+d0,3/2).

The exact recurrence permits this primitive-three-step/dominant-one-step
join, so B_5>=B0. Equality of those two finite recurrence values is not
needed below and is not inferred from a floating-point maximizing split.

For any compact convex K containing0 use

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

The maximizer is the unique projection P_K(x). Its defining normal is
<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every vertex v. Two such normals imply
||P_K(x')-P_K(x)||^2<=<x'-x,P_K(x')-P_K(x)>, proving 1-Lipschitzness.
The inequalities obtained by evaluating the two maxima at each other's
maximizers bound the differentiability remainder by ||x'-x||^2. Thus F_K
is convex differentiable and 1-smooth. Its minimizer set is
K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}: the reverse implication follows by
testing epsilon*g and letting epsilon decrease to zero. If X0 belongs to
the positive cone of K, then0 is a closest minimizer, since
<X0,z><=0 implies ||X0-z||^2>=||X0||^2 for z in K^polar.

## 2. A continuum with x1=g1 and x3=g3

Near the source word, write the five steps as

    h=(a,b,c,1+d,e),   z=b-1,   D=2e+1.

For a parameter r>0 define

    U=1/(a+1)^2,
    kappa=-[z^2+(c+1)r]/[z(c+2)],
    u=(1/(a+1),0),
    v=(kappa,sqrt(r-kappa^2))/(a+1),
    w=-z*u-c*v,
    k=-(d/D)*w,
    J=[z^2(2-c)+c(c^2-2)r]/(c+2).                            (1)

Whenever these vectors satisfy the normal conditions below, use
K=conv(0,u,v,w,k) and the exact trajectory

    x0=(a+1)u, g0=u;
    x1=u,     g1=u;
    x2=-z*u,  g2=v;
    x3=w,     g3=w;
    x4=D*k,   g4=k;
    x5=(e+1)k,g5=k.                                           (2)

Every update follows from (1). The choice of kappa imposes the exact identity
<w-v,-z*u-v>=0. Also ||w||^2=J*U by expansion. The attained payoff is

    E_r(h)=d^2*J/[D*(a+1)^2].                                (3)

At the source a=c=s,b=2,d=d0,e=3/2, (1) reduces to

    kappa=-(r+theta)/s,  J=nu,
    <u,w>/U=r+s-2,  <v,w>/U=(theta-r)/s.                     (4)

The Gram condition r>=kappa^2 is exactly nu<=r<=1. At sample0 the normal
against u is zero. Against v and w it is nonpositive because
kappa<0 and r+s-2<1; against k it follows from
-gamma*(r+s-2)<=gamma*theta<1. Against0 it is negative.
Samples1 and3 have zero residual. At sample2, residual y=-u-v, the normals
against0 andu in units of U are respectively theta*(r-1)/s and r-1.
The w normal is zero by construction. If T=<v,y>=theta*(1-r)*U/s>=0,
the k normal is -(1+gamma)*T. This covers every vertex at sample2.

For samples4 and5 the residual is a nonnegative multiple of k. The normals
against0,w,k hold directly. Against u andv they are equivalent to

    r+s-2 >= -gamma*nu,
    (theta-r)/s >= -gamma*nu.

It follows that the EXACT admissible r interval within this construction is

    r_minus=2-s-gamma*nu <= r <= theta+s*gamma*nu=r_plus.       (5)

Indeed (5) lies strictly inside [nu,1]. Its width is
nu*((s+1)*gamma-1)>0. For instance the rational square bounds
1.4142<s<1.4143 and 3.557<d0+1<3.558 give
gamma>theta and gamma*theta<1, proving these assertions.

Thus the source has a continuum of legal full-model witnesses all giving
E_r(h0)=1/B0: use 2(3+2s)=d0(d0+2) in (3). All old/new normals were retained. This is a continuum inside
the complete feasible set, not a claimed description of every tight primal.

For every fixed r strictly inside (5), all non-identical constraints just
checked are strict. Zero residuals and the w-v equality persist identically
in (1)-(2). Therefore (1)-(3) remain legal for every sufficiently small
perturbation of all five steps, with r held fixed. The square root also
remains real by the strict Gram condition. Since u,v are independent and
z*u+c*v+w=0 with positive coefficients,0 lies inside conv(u,v,w); the
minimizer is then unique and x0=(1,0) has true distance1.

## 3. The associated necessary first-order cone

Let h(t)=h0+t*v+o(t), t decreasing to0 through positive values, where
v=(v0,v1,v2,v3,v4) is the five-coordinate step velocity. Differentiate (3)
at h0. Direct differentiation of J in (1) gives

    L_r(v):=(log E_r(h(t)))'_(t=0)
      =-2*v0/(s+1)+2*v1+[2(s+2)r-2]*v2
         +2*v3/d0-v4/2.                                     (6)

Hence

    liminf_(t down to0) [B0*W_5(h(t))-1]/t
         >= max{L_(r_minus)(v),L_(r_plus)(v)}.                (7)

To justify the endpoints, first apply the lower witness to each fixed
interior r, then take the supremum of the affine function L_r over that
interval. No exchange of an optimized SDP and differentiation is used.

Any direction whose performance decreases to first order below1/B0 must
have both endpoint expressions nonpositive. The balanced dense direction
tested in the closed experiment is

    v=(-p0,q0,-p0,-m0/4,0),
    p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, m0=10s-14.

Here EVERY L_r in (5) is positive. In fact r_plus<.57, so
2(s+2)r-2<1.9. The bounds
.147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .140<m0<.145 and d0>2.55 give

    L_r > .294/2.415+.874-1.9*.149-.145/5.10 > 2/3.           (8)

This explains that local failure by exact common-function witnesses,
independently of the reselected dual array.

## 4. An independent face with a nonzero residual at sample1

The preceding cone is incomplete. A different exact construction works for
ALL a,c,e>=0, b>1 and d>0. Put

    z=b-1,  r=z/(c+1),  D=2e+1,
    U_vec=(1,0), V_vec=(0,sqrt(r)), k=-(d/D)*V_vec,
    K=conv(0,U_vec,V_vec,k),
    X0=(a+b)*U_vec+(c+1)*V_vec.

The full trajectory is

    X1=b*U_vec+(c+1)*V_vec,
    X2=(c+1)*V_vec,
    X3=V_vec,
    X4=D*k,
    X5=(e+1)*k,

with gradients U_vec,U_vec,V_vec,V_vec,k,k in chronological order.
At X0 the normal against V_vec is
r(c+1)-(a+b-1)=-a<=0. Against0 andk it is also nonpositive, since
a+b-1=a+z>0. At X1 the V_vec normal is zero because r(c+1)=z,
while0 andk have nonpositive normals. At X2 the residual is c*V_vec,
so the normals against the orthogonal U_vec,0, and the opposite k all
hold. X3 has zero residual. At X4 andX5 the residual is a nonnegative
multiple of k; U_vec is orthogonal to k and V_vec is opposite to k,
so all normals hold. These are every original projection condition.

Also X0 belongs to the positive cone of U_vec,V_vec, so0 is a closest
minimizer. The exact attained normalized payoff is

    E_orth(h)=d^2*r/[D*((a+b)^2+r(c+1)^2)]
            =d^2*z/[D*(c+1)*((a+b)^2+z(c+1))].               (9)

At h0, (3+2s)^2*theta=7+5s=(s+2)^2+(s+1), proving
E_orth(h0)=1/B0. Its logarithmic derivative is

    L_orth(v)=(2nu-2theta)*(v0+v2)+2nu*v1
                 +2*v3/d0-v4/2.                            (10)

For example the coefficient of v0 in log(9) is
-2(s+2)/(7+5s)=2nu-2theta; the coefficient of v1 is
1-[2(s+2)+(s+1)]/(7+5s)=2nu. The c coefficient equals the a coefficient.
The last two coefficients follow directly from d^2/D.

This family retains a nonzero proximal residual X1-U_vec. It is therefore
not contained in the x1=g1 construction in section2. No ambient-rank or
dual-support argument licenses discarding it.

## 5. A concrete direction missed by the first cone and scalar screens

Take the exact velocity

    v_escape=(1,1/2,-1,-1/20,-3/10).                           (11)

For sufficiently small positive t every actual step of h0+t*v_escape
is positive. The three standard legal lower tests all decrease at t=0:

* The global Huber test has logarithmic derivative
  -2*sum(v_escape)/B0=-3/(10B0)<0.
* The unit-curvature quadratic test has logarithmic derivative
  2*(1/2-1/(20d0)-3/5)=-1/5-1/(10d0)<0.
* The cut at the fourth step has logarithmic derivative
  -1/(10d0)-1/(3+2s)+3/20=-1/(10d0)-nu+3/20<0.

All witnesses in the continuum (5) also decrease. Substitution in (6) gives

    L_r(v_escape)=63/20-2theta-2(s+2)r-1/(10d0)<0.

For an entirely rational bound, (5) gives r_minus>.475; then
63/20-2*.4142-2*3.4142*.475<0, even after dropping the final negative term.

Nevertheless (10) gives

    L_orth(v_escape)=nu+3/20-1/(10d0)>1/4,                   (12)

because nu>.17 and d0>2.5. Thus for all sufficiently small positive t,
the exact legal function in section4 has normalized payoff strictly above
1/B0, and hence W_5(h0+t*v_escape)>1/B0>=1/B_5.

This is a counterexample to the sufficiency of the stated first-order
screen, not a counterexample to the minimax conjecture. No additional
concrete numerical case was evaluated or optimization proposed. The exact
direction (11) is an analytic stress test with its own explicit defeating
function.

The outcome explains a real limitation of the previously selected active
geometry: fixing the first post-step point to equal its gradient deletes
another source-tight face. It does not prove that the enlarged collection
is complete or that the source word is locally optimal. A proof using the
entire active set across growing horizons is still missing.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000786 -->
# Dense full-model discovery and a common-offset mass obstruction

Status: joint **unverified proof draft**, cumulative round 16. The experiment
is closed. No fact_submit is allocated or has been made. This is a named-family
obstruction and a finite discovery report, not the original minimax theorem.

Accepted inputs: b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact benchmark and supplies the
Huber lower bound; 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the complete interpolation/Gram
model. The new argument below is proved in full, not imported from an earlier
unverified finding. No numerical output is a premise of the proof.

## 1. The actual experiment

The public runner `max_dense_full_pep.py` adapts only the tested public source
`max3_c58_full_pep_batch_used.py`, SHA256
e8e1d0ed399b924eff7be7414fa61fd5c5d94dd7966294f6f4dbd84ce8cc5bc5.
The original runner was not executed. The separate public ledger is
`max_exact_benchmark_discovery_budget.json`; it is CLOSED at 16 evaluations
and 98.02878378597961 measured import/build/solve seconds. Its unused time
does not authorize another solve. The closed prior conic and direct ledgers
are unchanged. Totals: 672 conic evaluations, 171.40263413495765 measured
conic seconds; including the separate closed direct batch gives
172.22190110096332 measured seconds.

Every solve uses the full Gram of (x0,g0,...,gn), every distinct ordered pair
on {star,0,...,n}, common function values, optimum zero, and ||x0||^2<=1.
There is no imposed rank, ray, mode, jump support, recursive dual support,
or incoming-terminal deletion. The complete multipliers are reselected.
For M=1+sum h, the computational variables are
Ghat=Gram(x0,M*g0,...,M*gn), fhat=M*f. Diagnostics, flow, full remainders,
and pair residuals are also evaluated in the original model.

Raw files `max_dense_01_*_raw.json` through `max_dense_16_*_raw.json` retain
the evaluated steps and exact recipes, primal values and both Grams, every
ordered-pair label and raw multiplier, both optimum incidences, full raw
remainders, objective gap, original/scaled signs/flow/PSD diagnostics, and
measured cost. `max_dense_discovery_summary.json` indexes paths and hashes.
No eigenvalue or multiplier was repaired or truncated in those raw files.

The one-step exact calibration is h=(3/2), q=1/4, with

 q||x0||^2-2f1+Q10+Q0star+Q1star=||x0-2g0-2g1||^2/4.

The quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x0=1, attains q. The numerical solve matched this.
The second calibration is the deterministic exact-recurrence 15-step word,
evaluated at 80 decimal digits, with root split 9. The full recursively
constructed source array has evaluated flow residual <4.5e-16 and full
matrix coefficient residual <1.8e-15. The solver matched 1/B15 to about
6e-9 relative. Radical evaluation and tie selection are NOT interval
certificates; all candidate split values and the complete tree are retained.

The three dense directions use chronological tilt 0,-1/2,+1/2 in section 2.
Every step changes at n=15 and n=31. A five-step instance changes four steps
and exposes a small geometric subproblem. All 14 concrete proposals were
entered before filtering, and all passed the retained necessary screens.

Selected numerical full-model ratios q*B_n (primal and dual agree at the
shown precision; neither is asserted to be an exact certificate):

| n | tilt | parameter | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 5 | 0 | .02 | 1.02146826 |
| 15 | 0 | .002 | 1.01213546 |
| 15 | -1/2 | .002 | 1.01208733 |
| 15 | +1/2 | .002 | 1.01240829 |
| 15 | 0 | .02 | 1.12811266 |
| 15 | -1/2 | .02 | 1.12763576 |
| 15 | +1/2 | .02 | 1.13106896 |
| 15 | 0 | .1 | 1.82570595 |
| 15 | -1/2 | .1 | 1.82299772 |
| 15 | +1/2 | .1 | 1.84671595 |
| 31 | -1/2 | .0002 | 1.00274382 |
| 31 | -1/2 | .002 | 1.02959946 |
| 31 | -1/2 | .02 | 1.33895652 |
| 31 | +1/2 | .02 | 1.35257699 |

The three n15 parameter .1 cases and the n31 back-tilted case returned
`optimal_inaccurate`. The largest displayed original full-dual negative
eigenvalue magnitude is about6.3e-6; all such diagnostics are retained.
These data do not assert exact feasibility, local or global optimality,
or nonexistence of other dense improving schedules. No apparent small gain
is inferred from the baseline's floating discrepancy.

What the complete model changed: it defeated directions that passed the
global Huber, all single-cut, four fixed-curvature quadratic, and symmetric
plateau-to-quadratic screens. The primal Grams have several substantial
directions, and the reselected arrays have nonzero optimum-source mass.
Raw rank counts are tolerance dependent, particularly as the parameter
shrinks; they are not exact rank theorems.

## 2. The named dense family, enlarged beyond the experiment

Let h^0 be any exact dominant word attaining S_bullet(n), and let k be its
root bridge index. Its root children are exact primitive and dominant source
words, possibly empty. On each nonempty child I of length m_I put

 d_i=h_i^0-1>0,        1/2<=w_i<=3/2,
 W_I=sum_(i in I)w_i,
 A_I=sum w_i*d_i/W_I,  H_I=W_I/(sum w_i/d_i),
 c_I=(A_I+H_I)/2,     v_i=w_i(d_i-c_I),
 V_I=sum_(i in I)v_i.

Choose independent REAL amplitudes t_I for the children, and define

 h_i=h_i^0+t_I*v_i                    (i in child I),
 h_k=h_k^0-(1/4)sum_I t_I*V_I.        (root)

Require every actual h_i>=0. The amplitudes, weights, chosen maximizing
tree, and horizon may all vary. The experiment used one common positive
amplitude and w_i=1+tilt*(2j/(m_I-1)-1), with singleton weight1.
No certificate restriction is made in this definition.

**Claim.** Every such schedule satisfies, for n>=1,

 sum h_i <= S_bullet(n)+(27/8)(n-1),
 W_n(h) >= 1/[B_n+(27/4)(n-1)].                    (1)

In particular, uniformly over this entire enlarged family,

 W_n(h)/U_n^ZJ >= [1+27(n-1)/(4B_n)]^(-1) ->1.    (2)

This is a lower bound for unrestricted worst-case performance on the named
physical schedules. Full dual reselection, higher-rank remainders, both
optimum incidences and arbitrarily many new edges cannot evade it.

## 3. A block inequality from nonnegativity

Consider any nonempty base block h_i^0=1+d_i>1 of length m. Let its minimum
step be h_min, attained at j, and assume weights in [w_min,w_max] with
w_min>0. Define A,H,c,v,V as above. Write d_min=h_min-1.

The elementary weighted arithmetic/harmonic inequality H<=A follows from

 (sum w_i*d_i)(sum w_i/d_i) >= (sum w_i)^2:

expand the difference as sum_(i<j)w_i*w_j*(d_i/d_j+d_j/d_i-2), whose terms
are nonnegative because (d_i-d_j)^2/(d_i*d_j)>=0. Also H>=d_min, since
sum w_i/d_i <= (sum w_i)/d_min. Therefore, with W=sum w_i,

 V=W(A-H)/2=W(c-H)>=0,
 V<=W(c-d_min).                                    (3)

Put delta=c-d_min>=0. If delta=0, (3) forces V=0. If delta>0 and t>=0
keeps the jth step nonnegative, then

 0<=h_min-t*w_j*delta,
 t*delta<=h_min/w_j,
 t*V<=W*h_min/w_j <=m*(w_max/w_min)*h_min.          (4)

If t<0, t*V<=0, so the same upper bound holds. This is valid for each block
separately and permits independent amplitudes. It uses one actual small
step in that block; it is not a bound on a compressed recursion.

More generally the same argument works for any center c satisfying
H<=c<=A and A-c<=c-d_min. The midpoint rule meets these conditions.

## 4. Source words have a bounded small step in every nonempty child

Every source step is strictly greater than1. For a primitive bridge, writing
A=1+sum(left), C=1+sum(right), the positive number b-1 solves
(b-1)^2+(A+C)(b-1)-AC=0. For a dominant bridge it has the form
b=1+A/r with r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2>1 and D>0.

Every nonempty primitive source word contains a step sqrt(2): descend its
finite binary tree to a node with two empty children, where phi(0,0)=sqrt(2).
For a nonempty dominant word, if any primitive child on its dominant spine
is nonempty it contains such a step. Otherwise the last node on that spine
has two empty children, giving psi(0,0)=3/2. Thus every nonempty source word
has h_min<=3/2, without any assumption about maximizing split patterns.

Apply (4) to each root child, with w_max/w_min<=3 and h_min<=3/2. Then

 sum_I t_I V_I <= (9/2)sum_I m_I=(9/2)(n-1).

The root compensation makes the change of the TOTAL physical step sum
exactly (3/4)sum_I t_I V_I. This proves the first inequality of (1), even
when one child is empty or both children are singletons.

For any nonnegative schedule of total sum S, the legal scalar Huber
function of threshold a=1/(1+2S), started at1, has all iterates >=a,
x_n=1-aS and2f(x_n)=a. It has smoothness1 and unique minimizer0.
Consequently W_n(h)>=1/(1+2S), as also proved in b98bb807d514e80d.
Substitute the sum bound to get the second inequality of (1).

## 5. Uniform asymptotics from the exact recurrence

No unverified decimal asymptotic constant is needed. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2)
and P_j=1+S_circle(j). The legal balanced primitive construction gives

 P_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m,

by induction from P_0=1 and the primitive mass join
Omega(A,A)=lambda*A. The dominant mass join is
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. Taking a primitive prefix of length2^m-1
and an empty dominant suffix gives B_(2^m)>=2lambda^m.
Moreover B_(j+1)>=G(1,B_j)>B_j, by the allowed empty-prefix split.
For2^m<=n<2^(m+1),

 B_n/n >= (lambda/2)^m ->infinity.

Together with (1), this proves the uniform lower-bound ratio (2).
No conclusion is asserted about W_n(h) having a matching upper bound.

## 6. Scope, stress tests, and what remains

The calculation covers changes at every index, nonsymmetry, arbitrary
amplitudes for both children, and arbitrary bounded positive weights. Its
obstruction is the common subtractive center and the small source step,
not a fixed terminal library, sparse exception set, fixed support, or Huber
tightness of an upper certificate.

The proof identifies its load-bearing assumptions. An unbounded ratio
w_max/w_min only gives O((w_max/w_min)*n) added mass. Groups without a
bounded small step, a different center failing (3), or index-dependent
amplitudes inside one group leave this argument. Repeated separate
nonnegative mutations also accumulate their own cost and are not silently
covered by the single-round estimate. Removing those assumptions requires
a new construction and a new full-model/certificate argument; the generic
unknown SDP remains blocked as an asymptotic proof route.

This finding does not claim that all dense perturbations fail, or that the
exact benchmark is a local/global minimum over unrestricted schedules.
The original all-horizon/all-schedule target remains open.
