<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000787 -->
# An explicit planar adversary for the five-step dense variation

Status: **joint unverified candidate**, cumulative round 16. No optimizer or
verifier is used in this proof. The full-model experiment suggested looking
at two consecutive equal gradients and a later two-gradient plateau. None
of its floating entries is used as a mathematical premise.

The accepted benchmark/interpolation conventions are b98bb807d514e80d and
1252b8823bb5a290. All additional construction and inequalities are proved
below. This is a finite path obstruction, not an unbounded-horizon result.

## 1. Concrete steps and statement

Put s=sqrt(2), A=3+2s, beta=sqrt(7+4s), B0=(beta+1)^2, and

 p=(16-11s)/3, q=(8s-10)/3, m=10s-14=q-2p.

For0<=t<=1/50 define

 a=s-p*t, b=2+q*t, c=beta-m*t/4,
 h(t)=(a,b,a,c,3/2).                                (1)

This is the exact balanced weighted-mean proposal evaluated as
`n5_balanced_t002`. Indeed the primitive child's gaps are (s-1,1,s-1),
their arithmetic mean is (2s-1)/3, their harmonic mean is9-6s, and their
midpoint is(13-8s)/3. The child direction is(-p,q,-p), of total mass m;
the singleton right child has zero direction, and the root changes by-m/4.

For every t in this interval there is an explicit function F_t:R^2->R,
convex and differentiable with1-Lipschitz gradient and UNIQUE minimizer0,
whose GD trajectory for h(t), started at x0=(1,0), satisfies

 2F_t(x5) = (c-1)^2 J/[4(a+1)^2],                  (2)
 J=[(b-1)^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2]/(a+2),
 B0*2F_t(x5)>=1+7t/10.                             (3)

The exact benchmark obeys B5>=B0. Consequently

 B5*W5(h(t))>=1+7t/10>1       for0<t<=1/50.        (4)

No full certificate, regardless of support, optimum incidence or PSD rank,
can improve the exact five-step benchmark on this path. This conclusion
comes from one legal original-model function, not a restricted dual array.

## 2. Four concrete gradient vectors

Write z=b-1, d=c-1, and define

 kappa= -[z^2+(a+1)/2]/[z(a+2)],
 u=(1/(a+1),0),
 v=(kappa,sqrt(1/2-kappa^2))/(a+1),
 w=-z*u-a*v,             k=-d*w/4.                 (5)

Let U=||u||^2=1/(a+1)^2. Then

 <u,v>=kappa*U, ||v||^2=U/2,
 ||w||^2=J*U,
 <u,w>/U=[a(a+1)/2-2z^2]/[z(a+2)]=:r_u,
 <v,w>/U=[z^2+(1-a-a^2)/2]/(a+2)=:r_v.            (6)

Expanding (5) proves (6). The defining choice of kappa gives the exact
orthogonality identity

 <w-v,-z*u-v>=0.                                   (7)

All radicals in (5) are real. Here are rational bounds proving that claim
and all the normal inequalities used below. Squaring the rational endpoints
gives1.4142<s<1.4143 and3.557<beta<3.558. Hence

 .147<p<.149, .437<q<.439, .140<m<.145,
 1.411<=a<=1.415, 1<=z<=1.01, 2.55<=d<=2.57.        (8)

Every terminating decimal in this proof denotes the corresponding exact
rational. From (8),

 -.654<kappa<-.639, .169<J<.177,
 -.1<r_u<0,               -.062<r_v<0.             (9)

For explicit checks: the numerator in -kappa lies between2.2055 and2.2276,
its denominator between3.411 and3.44915, giving the first bound. Set
N=z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2. The polynomial a(a^2-2)/2 is increasing throughout
(8), so direct endpoint substitution gives .578<N<.603. Dividing by
a+2 gives the asserted J bounds (using the slightly sharper lower
endpoint .585+1.411*(1.411^2-2)/2>.5785 when desired).
The numerator of r_u is at least
1.411*2.411/2-2*1.01^2=-.3392395 and is negative; its denominator is at
least3.411, giving -.1<r_u<0. The numerator of r_v is at least
1+(1-1.415-1.415^2)/2=-.2086125 and is negative; division by at least3.411
gives -.062<r_v<0. Thus1/2-kappa^2>1/2-.654^2>0.

## 3. One compact convex set and EVERY original normal inequality

Let K=conv{0,u,v,w,k}. The vectors u,v are independent and
z*u+a*v+w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Therefore0 lies in the
interior of the triangle conv{u,v,w}, and hence in the interior of K.

Use the proposed full trajectory

 x0=(a+1)u, g0=u;
 x1=u,     g1=u;
 x2=-z*u,  g2=v;
 x3=w,     g3=w;
 x4=-d*w=4k, g4=k;
 x5=(5/2)k,  g5=k.                                 (10)

Every equation x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i follows immediately from (1) and (5).
To prove g_i=Proj_K(x_i), it suffices to check
<r-g_i,x_i-g_i><=0 for all vertices r of K. Linear interpolation then
gives it for every r in K. These checks cover every ordered original pair,
including optimum and terminal pairs, with one common K.

At samples1 and3 the residual x_i-g_i is zero. At sample0 it is a*u.
We have <v,u>/U=kappa<0, <w,u>/U=r_u<0 and
<k,u>/U=-(d/4)r_u<(2.57/4)*.1<1; the vertices0 andu also satisfy the
normal inequality. This checks sample0.

At sample2 its residual is y2=-z*u-v. In units of U, the inequalities
for vertices0 andu are respectively

 <-v,y2>/U=z*kappa+1/2<0,
 <u-v,y2>/U=-z+(z-1)kappa+1/2<=-1/2.

The vertex w has equality by (7), and the vertex v has equality trivially.
Write T=<v,y2>=(-z*kappa-1/2)U>0. By (7), <w,y2>=T. Thus
<k-v,y2>=-(1+d/4)T<0, which checks the last vertex.

At samples4 and5 the residuals are3k and(3/2)k. It is enough to check
<r-k,k><=0. The vertices0,w,k satisfy this directly since k=-d*w/4.
For the other two vertices this condition is

 r_u >= -d*J/4,       r_v >= -d*J/4.

It follows from (9), since d*J/4>2.55*.169/4>.1. Every original
normal inequality has now been checked without dropping remote pairs.

## 4. A global smooth function with the correct initial distance

Define

 F_t(x)=max_(r in K){<x,r>-||r||^2/2}.              (11)

The maximizer is unique and equals Proj_K(x), by completing the square.
If P and P' are the projections of x and x', their normal inequalities
give ||P-P'||^2<=<x-x',P-P'>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating
the maxima in (11) at each other's maximizers yields

 0<=F_t(x')-F_t(x)-<P,x'-x>
      <=<P'-P,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

This proves differentiability with gradient P and1-Lipschitz gradient.
It is finite and convex because K is compact and (11) is a maximum of
affine functions of x. Since0 is interior to K, for every x!=0 a sufficiently
small positive multiple r=epsilon*x belongs to K and makes
<x,r>-||r||^2/2 positive. Therefore F_t(0)=0 and0 is its UNIQUE minimizer.
The initial point in (10) is(1,0), so its true initial distance is exactly1.

Section3 now proves that (10) is the actual GD trajectory of this single
global function. At x5=(5/2)k, (11) gives F_t(x5)=2||k||^2. Thus
2F_t(x5)=4||k||^2=d^2||w||^2/4, proving (2). Replacing F_t by L*F_t
for any L>0 preserves the project's normalized performance under h_i/L.

## 5. Exact payoff comparison over the entire interval

For t=0, a=s,z=1 and J=(2-s)/(2+s)=1/A. Since(a+1)^2=A and
2A=(beta-1)(beta+1), (2) is exactly1/B0.

Set R(t)=B0*d^2*N/[4(a+1)^2(a+2)], with N as in section2. The preceding
bounds give N>0, and differentiation gives

 (log R)'=-m/(2d)+2p/(a+1)+N'/N+p/(a+2),
 N'=2z(2-a)q-p[-z^2+(3a^2-2)/2].                  (12)

Using (8), 2z(2-a)>=1.17 and the square bracket is<1.004. Hence
N'>1.17*.437-1.004*.149>.361. Since N<.603, (12) gives

 (log R)'>-29/1020+98/805+361/603+147/3415>7/10.

The last comparison is a rational inequality, obtained by multiplication
by the positive product of the displayed denominators. Integrating from0
to t and using exp(u)>=1+u proves R(t)>=1+7t/10, namely (3).

Finally the exact benchmark recurrence in b98bb807d514e80d allows the
primitive three-step construction(s,2,s), of mass A=1+sum h, and a
dominant one-step suffix(3/2), of mass4. Its mass join is

 G(A,4)=2A+2+2sqrt(1+2A)=(beta+1)^2=B0.

The optimal primitive mass is at least A and G is increasing in that mass,
so B5>=B0. This argument needs NO decimal comparison of candidate splits.
Multiply the legal payoff by B5 to obtain (4).

## 6. What this explains and does not explain

The constructed path increases total step mass, and its tested finite case
passes the earlier necessary scalar screens. A planar convex body now gives
a direct, exact obstruction on the whole interval0<t<=1/50, even when the
entire upper certificate is reselected. The construction uses a true common
projection body and all original pairs; it is not a formal Gram test alone.

It does not claim to attain the full SDP optimum or to require dimension2
in every worst case. No automatic recursive lift to n15,n31 or unbounded n
is established here. The separate common-offset mass argument covers the
asymptotic limitation of that particular dense family; arbitrary coordinated
redistribution and the original minimax conjecture remain open.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000788 -->
# A uniform tail transfer for core-ended projection defects, and its serial limit

Status: **complete joint proof draft, NOT verifier-accepted**, cumulative round
17. This file is one self-contained candidate package. It does not use another
unverified finding as a lemma. There is no new optimization, numerical replay,
installation, or fact_submit. The 16-evaluation discovery allocation remains
closed at 98.02878378597961 measured seconds.

The performance W_n and exact benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 are those in accepted
facts b98bb807d514e80d and 1252b8823bb5a290. All additional constructions and
estimates are proved below. The initial norm used here is proved to equal the
distance to the ENTIRE minimizer set, not just to an arbitrarily chosen optimum.

The new conclusion has two parts. A fixed core-ended projection defect can
survive an arbitrarily long objective tail with a fixed relative surplus. But
iterating the specified constant-coordinate transfer across balanced primitive
joins necessarily dilutes that surplus. Neither conclusion settles the original
all-schedule minimax problem.

## 1. Projection functions and the meaning of a core endpoint

For a compact convex set K containing 0, put

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completing the square shows that the unique maximizer is P_K(x), the Euclidean
projection of x onto K. A vector g in K is this projection exactly when

    <v-g,x-g> <= 0                    for every v in K.             (1)

For polytopes it suffices to check every listed vertex. The two projection
inequalities for x,x' imply

    ||P_K(x')-P_K(x)||^2 <= <x'-x,P_K(x')-P_K(x)>.

Thus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the two maxima at each other's
maximizers gives

    0 <= F_K(x')-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),x'-x>
      <= <P_K(x')-P_K(x),x'-x> <= ||x'-x||^2.

Consequently F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient
P_K. Furthermore

    argmin F_K = K^polar := {z : <z,g><=0 for every g in K}.          (2)

Indeed the displayed polar inequalities imply F_K(z)=0. Conversely, if
F_K(z)=0, apply its defining inequality to epsilon*g in K and let epsilon
decrease to zero to obtain <z,g><=0.

Fix a finite nonnegative prefix p=(p_0,...,p_(ell-1)) and actual projection
data x_i,g_i for i=0,...,ell, including 0 as an optimum sample. Thus
x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i*g_i, and (1) holds against 0 and every g_j. It suffices to
use K_0=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell). Assume

    x_ell = g_ell = w != 0.                                       (3)

This is the *core endpoint* condition. Define

    A=1+sum p_i,  R_i=sum_(j=i)^(ell-1) p_j,
    y_i=x_i-g_i,  r_init^2=||x_0||^2,
    Delta=A^2||w||^2-r_init^2.

The last quantity is the particular squared-distance defect studied here.
It is not asserted to represent every possible failure of primitive validity.

## 2. One finite buffer works for EVERY negative terminal direction

Choose a nonnegative number beta satisfying

    beta >= max_i (-<g_i,w>)_+,
    beta >= max_(i:R_i>0) (-<w,y_i>)_+/R_i.                        (4)

An empty maximum is zero and (u)_+=max(u,0). These are maxima of finitely many
explicit numbers, so beta is finite. For R_i=0, nonnegativity of all remaining
steps gives x_i=x_ell=w. Uniqueness of projection gives g_i=w and y_i=0; those
indices require no division by zero.

For any tau>0 lift the old gradients and positions into one extra coordinate:

    G_i=(g_i,sqrt(beta)),
    X_i=(x_i,(1+R_i)*sqrt(beta)),
    k=-tau*G_ell,
    K=conv(0,G_0,...,G_ell,k).                                    (5)

Every old sample is still a projection sample for this single K. Against an
old vertex G_j the extra-coordinate difference vanishes, so its normal is
exactly the original normal in (1). Against 0 the new normal is

    -<g_i,y_i>-beta*R_i <= 0.

Here <g_i,y_i>>=0 follows by using the old zero vertex. Against k the normal is

    <-tau*w-g_i,y_i>-(1+tau)*beta*R_i <= 0,                        (6)

because -<g_i,y_i><=0 and -<w,y_i><=beta*R_i. The R_i=0 case is zero.

At every point u*k with u>=1, the projection is k. The zero vertex has
nonpositive normal. An old vertex has

    <G_i-k,k>
      =-tau*(<g_i,w>+beta)-tau^2*(||w||^2+beta) <= 0               (7)

by (4); multiply this by u-1. The k vertex has zero normal. Equations
(6)-(7) check every old/new ordered pair through one common projection body.

The lifted initial point has the exact positive-cone representation

    X_0=G_ell+sum_(i<ell) p_i*G_i.                               (8)

For every z in K^polar, (8) gives <X_0,z><=0. Hence
||X_0-z||^2>=||X_0||^2, with equality at z=0. The true initial distance is

    dist(X_0,argmin F_K)^2 = r_init^2+A^2*beta.                    (9)

This includes duplicated gradients and zero physical steps. The construction
does not impose any restriction on upper-certificate support or PSD rank.

## 3. Arbitrary objective tails and a surplus independent of their length

Let z=(z_0,...,z_(m-1)) be ANY finite nonnegative tail, with total T and
D=1+2T. Choose any bridge 1+d with d>0, and use (5) with tau=d/D. After
the prefix, X_ell=G_ell. The bridge sends this point to -d*G_ell=D*k.
At a tail partial sum t, the iterate is (D-t)*k. Since 0<=t<=T,
D-t>=T+1>=1. Section 2 proves that its gradient is always k. In particular
the final point is (T+1)*k, and

    2F_K(X_final)=D*||k||^2=(d^2/D)*(||w||^2+beta).

Combining with (9) proves the exact attained normalized payoff

    E(p,d,z;beta)
       = (d^2/D)*(||w||^2+beta)/(r_init^2+A^2*beta).               (10)

This is an actual lower witness for W_(ell+1+m)(p,1+d,z). It uses the exact
chronological tail; no optimization over its ordering or gradients was made.

In particular choose the source ConPD bridge

    d=(sqrt(D^2+8*A*D)-D)/4,
    2d^2+Dd=AD.                                                  (11)

This equals psi(sum p_i,T)-1 from the problem's exact recurrence. Its total
physical mass is

    B_concat=2A+2d+D=(D+2d)^2/D,
    d^2/(D*A^2)=1/B_concat.                                     (12)

Therefore (10) becomes

    E=(1+delta)/B_concat,
    delta=Delta/(r_init^2+A^2*beta).                             (13)

If Delta>0, this is a strict positive relative surplus INDEPENDENT of the
tail length, total T, ordering, and values. A single beta from (4) works for
all of them. This strengthens a finite strict-defect implication only for
the explicitly stated projection/core class; it does not normalize an
arbitrary smooth-convex defect into that class.

For any L>0 multiply F_K by L. The physical updates h_i/L then produce the
same normalized payoff. Scaling the argument and objective simultaneously
can normalize the true distance in (9) to one.

## 4. The detected dense direction has an explicit core defect

This section rederives the needed geometry; the round16 planar draft is not
used as a correctness premise. Put

    s=sqrt(2), p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, m0=10s-14=q0-2p0.

For 0<=t<=1/50 set

    a=s-p0*t, b=2+q0*t, zeta=b-1,
    prefix p(t)=(a,b,a), A=1+2a+b.

This is exactly the three-step primitive-child part of the balanced dense
redistribution tested in the closed batch. Define

    U=1/(a+1)^2,
    kappa=-[zeta^2+(a+1)/2]/[zeta*(a+2)],
    u=(1/(a+1),0),
    v=(kappa,sqrt(1/2-kappa^2))/(a+1),
    w=-zeta*u-a*v,
    N=zeta^2*(2-a)+a*(a^2-2)/2, J=N/(a+2).

Expansion gives

    ||u||^2=U, ||v||^2=U/2, <u,v>=kappa*U, ||w||^2=J*U,
    r_u:=<u,w>/U=[a(a+1)/2-2zeta^2]/[zeta(a+2)],
    r_v:=<v,w>/U=[zeta^2+(1-a-a^2)/2]/(a+2),
    <w-v,-zeta*u-v>=0.                                         (14)

All following terminating decimals denote exact rationals. Squaring endpoints
gives 1.4142<s<1.4143 and consequently

    .147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .140<m0<.145,
    1.411<=a<=1.415, 1<=zeta<=1.01,
    -.654<kappa<-.639, .578<N<.603,
    .169<J<.177, -.1<r_u<0, -.062<r_v<0.                         (15)

Here -kappa has numerator between 2.2055 and 2.2276 and denominator between
3.411 and 3.44915. The polynomial a(a^2-2)/2 is increasing on this interval,
so bounding it and zeta^2(2-a) separately proves the N bounds. For r_u the
numerator is at least 1.411*2.411/2-2*1.01^2=-.3392395 and is negative;
its denominator is at least 3.411. For r_v the numerator is at least
1+(1-1.415-1.415^2)/2=-.2086125 and is negative; its denominator is at
least 3.411. These prove the last two bounds. Also .654^2<1/2, so v is real
and u,v are independent.

Use K_0=conv(0,u,v,w) and the prefix data

    x_0=(a+1)u=(1,0), g_0=u;
    x_1=u,             g_1=u;
    x_2=-zeta*u,       g_2=v;
    x_3=w,             g_3=w.                                  (16)

The recursion for (a,b,a) holds exactly. At samples 1 and 3 the residual is
zero. At sample 0 it is a*u; (15) checks its normal against all four vertices.
At sample 2, put y=-zeta*u-v. The normals against 0 and u, in units of U,
are zeta*kappa+1/2<0 and

    -zeta+(zeta-1)*kappa+1/2 <= -1/2.

The w normal is zero by (14), and the v normal is zero by definition. Thus
(16) is an actual prefix trajectory with a core endpoint.

The buffer beta=U/8 satisfies (4). Indeed the only negative endpoint
correlations in (14) have magnitude less than U/10. For the residual test,
sample 0 contributes a*(-r_u)*U/(2a+b)<U/10, sample 1 contributes zero,
and <w,y_2>=<v,y_2>=(-zeta*kappa-1/2)U>0. Sample 3 has no remaining
steps and zero residual.

To bound its defect, set

    R_prim(t)=A^2*J/(a+1)^2.

At t=0, A=3+2s=(s+1)^2 and J=(s-1)^2=1/A, so R_prim(0)=1.
Differentiation, with A'=m0>0, gives

    (log R_prim)'=2m0/A+2p0/(a+1)+N'/N+p0/(a+2),
    N'=2zeta*(2-a)*q0-p0[-zeta^2+(3a^2-2)/2].                   (17)

The first product in N' is greater than 1.17*.437; the square bracket is
less than 1.004. Hence N'>1.17*.437-1.004*.149>.361. From (15),

    (log R_prim)' > 294/2415+361/603+147/3415 > 3/4.

The last inequality follows by multiplication by positive integer
denominators. Integrating and using exp(u)>=1+u gives

    R_prim(t)>=1+3t/4.

Also A<=5.84 and a+1>=2.411, so

    1+A^2*U/8 <= 1+5.84^2/(8*2.411^2) < 7/4.

Consequently the surplus in (13) obeys

    delta(t)=[R_prim(t)-1]/[1+A^2*U/8] >= 3t/7.                 (18)

For t>0 the inequalities are strict where indicated. Every bound is uniform
over the full stated parameter interval and has no numerical solver premise.

## 5. A named unbounded-horizon family is strictly worse than the benchmark

Fix any t in (0,1/50]. For each m>=0 take ANY nonnegative m-step suffix z
whose sum T is at most S_bullet(m). Use prefix p(t) from section 4 and bridge
(11). This is a specified family of physical schedules of length n=m+4.
It allows arbitrary tail order, zero steps, and complete dual re-selection.

Since d<A<6, (12) gives

    B_concat < D+4A < B_m+24 <= B_n+24.

The final comparison follows directly from monotonicity of the exact dominant
recurrence: the empty-prefix split gives
G(1,B_j)=2+B_j/2+sqrt(B_j^2+8B_j)/2>B_j. Also B_n>=2n+1 by the same
recurrence, since G(1,D)>=D+2 and B_0=1.

Equations (13) and (18) prove

    B_n*W_n(p(t),1+d,z)
      >= (1+3t/7)*B_n/(B_n+24).                                (19)

In particular the limit inferior, uniformly over this entire suffix class,
is at least 1+3t/7. Explicitly, for n>=ceil(56/t+12), the right side is at
least 1+3t/14. Thus the fixed prefix defect need not disappear when the
objective tail grows without bound.

This is a LOWER bound showing a particular family is too poor. It is not
an improved upper schedule, not an all-horizon minimax converse, and not
an inference from a finite objective value. It does not assume equality
between a Silver subsequence and the exact B_n benchmark.

## 6. What happens when this transfer is repeated at primitive joins

The same projection calculation has a core-ending version. Append any
nonnegative block of capacity C=1+sum(block)>1 to a core prefix of capacity
A, with bridge 1+r satisfying

    r*(A+C+r)=A*C.

Write A_next=A+C+r and tau=r/C=A/A_next in (5). The bridge reaches C*k;
the block's constant gradient k leaves final point k. The complete normal
audit in section 2 applies because every point in this block is u*k with
u>=1. The capacity and initial norm identities give

    A_next^2||k||^2=A^2(||w||^2+beta),
    r_init,next^2=r_init^2+A^2*beta,
    Delta_next=Delta.                                           (20)

Now iterate this SPECIFIC construction. At level j let the capacity be A_j,
the core endpoint w_j, and initial squared distance R_j^2. The jth join has
tau_j=A_j/A_(j+1) in (0,1). It adds one constant coordinate sqrt(beta_j)
to every old gradient, retains all old vertices, and has

    w_(j+1)=-tau_j*(w_j,sqrt(beta_j)).

Allow ANY beta_j>=0 for which the actual construction is legal. It need not
satisfy the sufficient all-tau bound (4). Let N_j=A_j^2||w_j||^2=R_j^2+Delta,
where (20) makes Delta>0 independent of j.

For j>=1, the old prefix contains the previously queried vertex

    v_old=-w_j/tau_(j-1).

At the first point of the new appended block, the residual is
(C_j-1)*w_(j+1). Since C_j>1, its normal against the lifted v_old forces

    beta_j >= [(1/tau_(j-1)-tau_j)/(1+tau_j)]*||w_j||^2.          (21)

This is an ORIGINAL normal inequality, not a condition imposed for
convenience. To see it, expand
<(v_old,sqrt(beta_j))-w_(j+1),w_(j+1)><=0 and divide by tau_j>0.

It follows from (20)-(21) that

    N_(j+1) >= [(1+1/tau_(j-1))/(1+tau_j)]*N_j.

For r>=1 the factors telescope exactly:

    N_r >= N_1 * (1+tau_0)/(1+tau_(r-1)) * A_(r-1)/A_0.         (22)

Thus Delta/R_r^2 tends to zero whenever A_(r-1) tends to infinity. This
permits arbitrary nonempty appended blocks and arbitrarily retuned legal
buffers in the specified construction. The C_j>1 assumption is essential
to this particular normal argument: if the appended block has zero mass,
its first point is already core and that normal provides no restriction.

For balanced joins C_j=A_j, put lambda=1+sqrt(2). Then
A_(j+1)=lambda*A_j, tau_j=1/lambda, and (21) reads
beta_j>=sqrt(2)*||w_j||^2. Consequently

    N_r >= lambda^(r-1)*N_1,
    Delta/R_r^2 <= Delta/[lambda^(r-1)*N_1-Delta].                (23)

If the appended physical block is an identical copy of the current prefix,
its length obeys ell_r+1=2^r*(ell_0+1). Hence the relative defect carried
by this single-branch construction is O((ell_r+1)^(-log_2(lambda))). This
remains true if a fixed modified three-step seed occurs in a positive
fraction of the physical word: the constructed trajectory uses a constant
gradient on each appended copy and does not exploit that copy's own defect.

An eventual objective-tail transfer can only reduce this relative surplus,
since (13) divides Delta by R_r^2+A_r^2*beta_final>=R_r^2. Therefore serial
constant-coordinate transfers do not automatically turn a fixed finite
defect into a fixed relative obstruction on repeated-copy dense variations.

This is a limit of the specified witness construction. It is NOT an upper
bound on W, a restriction on full PEP ranks or multipliers, or an assertion
that no different joint multi-block construction can retain a fixed gap.

There is an explicit counterexample to deleting C_j>1 from the claim about
arbitrarily selected legal buffers. Start with the one-step prefix (2),
K_0=[-1,1], x_0=g_0=1 and x_1=g_1=-1. Its capacity is A_0=3 and
Delta=8. Append only EMPTY right blocks, C_j=1, and the exact primitive
bridges 1+r_j, where r_j*(A_j+1+r_j)=A_j. Choose every beta_j=0.
Then 0<r_j<1, all sampled points stay in [-1,1], and the same function
F_( [-1,1] ) has gradient equal to the point throughout the trajectory.
Every endpoint is core. The identities A_(j+1)*r_j=A_j and
w_(j+1)=-r_j*w_j imply A_j^2*w_j^2=9, while R_j^2=1.
Also A_(j+1)>A_j+1, so A_j tends to infinity. Thus Delta/R_j^2=8
does NOT decay in this zero-mass-right-block case. Every original normal
is valid because its residual is zero. This example pinpoints exactly why
the nonzero appended mass is needed in (21).

## 7. Scope of the advance

The finite full-model data suggested retaining a nontrivial core-ended
prefix, instead of testing only the global Huber and quadratic trajectories.
The new formula (13) gives a tail-uniform quantitative use of such a prefix.
Equation (19) makes it an actual unbounded-horizon obstruction for a named
class. The separate original-pair inequality (21) identifies precisely why
the obvious recursive extension loses its fixed relative strength.

The remaining bridge is a construction coupling defects in a growing set
of modified child blocks, with all original normals and the total initial
distance retained. No such bridge or improving full upper certificate has
been established here. The original target remains unresolved.

# An exact full coupling for the changed fifteen-step instance

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21.
This answers the finite coupling problem in the refreshed TASK associated
with guidance04cc04960aa1755d. It is not a verifier-accepted fact and does
not establish the original all-schedule asymptotic target.

The specific changed seed from finding1d4af67cf6124842, its actual
seven-step child, its word, its values, and its optimum are retained.
That finding is motivation, not a correctness premise: the data and
realizing-function argument are supplied again here. The only cited
accepted result is fact1252b8823bb5a290, for the original Q convention
and its necessity.

The new numerical option is closed: three optimizer evaluations and one
conservatively charged exact fixed-candidate replay used all four slots
and19.17568400500022 measured seconds. No verifier was called. All source,
raw arrays and original statuses are public. The exact rational coupling
and the arithmetic certificate below are independent of solver status.

## 1. The exact unchanged seed and every seed interpolation row

Let

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    rho=599/600, eps=1/100, E=1/10000,
    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,
    A=1+2a+b.

In Euclidean R^3 with its displayed coordinate order define

    U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho*sqrt(eta),eps),
    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V, g_3=W,
    x_3=W, x_2=W+aV, x_1=W+aV+bU,
    p=x_0=W+aV+(a+b)U.                                  (1)

The GD word is exactly h[0]=(a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i are

    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),
    y_1=zU+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),
    y_2=(Klen-1)V+(0,0,eps), y_3=0.                      (2)

Let e_i=<g_i,y_i>. The four energies are

    e_0=m-1, e_1=z, e_2=(Klen-1)eta, e_3=0.

Here is the complete list of vertex normals <g-g_i,y_i>:

* At i=0, the vertices g=0,V,W give -(m-1), -a,
  and -a-(1-rho)z+E respectively; g=U gives0.
* At i=1, the vertices0,V,W give -z,0,-(1-rho)z+E;
  g=U gives0.
* At i=2, the vertices0,U both give -(Klen-1)eta,
  g=W gives -(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta+E, and g=V gives0.
* At i=3 all vertex normals are0.

The elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply
1.39<a<1.43, 2<b<2.01, 2.38<Klen<2.43, z>1, eta>2/5.
Consequently(1-rho)z>1/600>E and
(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E. Every displayed normal is nonpositive.

For any finite data with these normal conditions, set

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                           (3)

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer in(3) as the
Euclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection inequalities, added,
give ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.
Thus P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the defining maxima at each other's
maximizers bounds the differentiability remainder between0 and
||x'-x||^2. Hence F_K is finite convex differentiable and1-smooth,
with gradient P_K. The displayed vertex normals give P_K(x_i)=g_i.

Expansion of the original residual in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

The optimum rows are Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-e_i<=0.
Thus the normal list checks ALL original seed rows.

The minimizer set is K^polar={v:<v,g><=0 for every g in K}.
Sufficiency follows from(3). For necessity test tg in(3), divide the
resulting inequality by t>0 and let t decrease to0. The positive cone
identity

    p=mU+aV+W                                             (4)

gives <p,v><=0 on K^polar. Therefore0 is a closest minimizer and its
true squared distance is

    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z+E,
    N=||W||^2=rho^2*eta+E.                               (5)

This same retained cone identity will prove true distance after both
joins. No change of optimum or chosen normalization is made.

The seed has positive gradient-energy surplus kappa=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.
Here is a complete exact sign certificate. Put
Rbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substitution and s^2=2 yield

    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,
    P0=19019047380837966731,
    Q0=13474265781546157318,
    D0=40842989603870102500.

All are positive and

    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2
      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.

Squaring positive numbers therefore proves the claimed bound for the
barred ratio. Also Rbar^2>Nbar, since
Rbar^2-Nbar=m^2+eta*(Klen^2-rho^2)>0. Adding E to numerator and denominator
raises the ratio Nbar/Rbar^2, proving the displayed bound for kappa.

## 2. Fully specified frames and the actual seven-step child

For a nonzero column vector t in R^d whose unit vector is not e_1, define

    u_t=t/||t||, v_t=e_1-u_t,
    B(t)=I-2*v_t*v_t^T/(v_t^T*v_t), T_t(q)=B(t)q.         (6)

B(t) is a real symmetric orthogonal matrix, with first column u_t.
The first coordinate of T_t(q) is <q,t>/||t||. All the axes used below
have u_t!=e_1. This also follows from the displayed seed data and the
interval enclosures in section4, so there is no zero denominator.

Write T_W(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2) and
T_p(q)=(c_0,c_1,c_2). In R^5 define

    L0(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2,0,0),
    R0(q)=(-c_0,0,0,c_1,c_2),
    r_0=theta*A, alpha_0=r_0*||W||/R.                   (7)

Both L0 and R0 are actual isometries, and

    <L0(u),alpha_0*R0(v)>
         =-r_0*<u,W><p,v>/R^2.                          (8)

The seven-step child has, for0<=i<=3,

    X_i=L0(x_i), G_i=L0(g_i), Y_i=L0(y_i), F_i=f_i,
    X_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(x_i), G_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(g_i),
    Y_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(y_i), F_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*f_i.

Its word and capacity are

    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a), A_1=lambda*A.

The bridge is exact: alpha_0*R0(p)=-r_0*L0(W), and X_3=G_3=L0(W).
All four seed lists <g_i,W>,<y_i,W>,<p,g_i>,<p,y_i> are nonnegative:
the coordinates in(1)-(2) have nonnegative entries. Formula(8) therefore
makes each cross inner product between a gradient and a residual
nonpositive. Subtracting the nonnegative receiving normal energy gives
every cross Q<=0. Within-child and optimum rows follow from section1
and scaling. Formula(3), now applied to all eight G_i, gives one actual
seven-step function with the prescribed values.

Its initial point P=X_0 has squared norm R^2 and remains in the positive
cone of its retained first-seed gradients by(4). Hence R is still its
true closest-minimizer distance. Its endpoint is

    w=G_7=X_7=alpha_0*R0(W),
    N_1=||w||^2=alpha_0^2*N,
    E_i=<G_i,Y_i>=e_i for i<4,
    E_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*e_i for i<4.                       (9)

This fixes all internal positions, gradients, values and indices of the
children in the mandated fifteen-step problem.

## 3. A rational full transverse block and a real nine-dimensional parent

In the exact coordinate order(7), use the two five-dimensional frames
B(w) and B(P) from(6). For a child vector q write

    B(w)q=(q_l0,q_lT), B(P)q=(q_r0,q_rT),

where the tails are columns in R^4. Fix the rational matrix

    C=(1/100)*[[7,0,7,0], [1,0,0,0],
              [18,-1,-10,0], [-17,0,-11,0]].             (10)

Its squared Frobenius norm is934/10000<1. Thus ||C||_op<1, and
H=(I-C^T*C)^(1/2) is a well-defined real positive-definite matrix.
No eigenvalue clipping, numerical rank choice, or PSD repair is involved.
In R^9=R direct-sum R^4 direct-sum R^4 define

    L(q)=(q_l0,q_lT,0),
    Rmap(q)=(-q_r0,C*q_rT,H*q_rT).                       (11)

They are isometries because C^T*C+H^2=I. This explicitly realizes every
cross inner product; there is no unspecified Gram completion.

The next bridge and scale are

    r_1=theta*A_1=A, alpha_1=r_1*||w||/R,
    h[2]=(h[1],1+r_1,h[1]), A_2=lambda^2*A.               (12)

For0<=i<=7, the parent data are

    (x'_i,g'_i,y'_i,f'_i)=(L X_i,L G_i,L Y_i,F_i),
    (x'_(8+i),g'_(8+i),y'_(8+i),f'_(8+i))
       =(alpha_1 Rmap X_i,alpha_1 Rmap G_i,
         alpha_1 Rmap Y_i,alpha_1^2 F_i).                (13)

The bridge is exact since alpha_1 Rmap(P)=-r_1 L(w) and X_7=G_7=w.
All within-child GD updates and values are preserved. Both children have
the same prescribed optimum0. Every future claim about this parent uses
the genuine last gradient g'_15; it is not deleted or freely reset.

Define the exact bilinear form

    B_C(u,v)=alpha_1[-u_l0*v_r0+u_lT^T*C*v_rT].          (14)

The full cross normal conditions are exactly

    Q'_(i,8+j)=B_C(G_i,Y_j)-alpha_1^2*E_j<=0,
    Q'_(8+i,j)=B_C(Y_j,G_i)-E_j<=0                       (15)

for every0<=i,j<=7. Section4 proves every one. All internal rows are
unchanged or scaled copies of section2; all optimum rows are0 or minus
the corresponding nonnegative normal energy. Applying(3) to all16 g'_i
therefore gives one actual finite convex1-smooth F:R^9->R realizing the
whole supplied fifteen-step trajectory and values.

The retained identity(4), through the two left isometries, proves that
0 is a closest minimizer of this parent as well. Its true radius is R.
The endpoint residual is0 and its normalized terminal objective is

    2F(x'_15)/dist(x'_0,argmin F)^2
        =alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*N/R^2
        =kappa^4/A_2^2.                                 (16)

Thus the whole-run value W_15(h[2]) is at least the explicit quantity(16).
The core surplus satisfies A_2^2 times(16)>1+1/500, by kappa>1+1/2000
and the binomial formula. This does not identify A_2^2 with the benchmark
B_15; the distinction is essential to the target scope.

## 4. All cross normals: a short exact rational interval certificate

Only the receiving residual columns j=0,1,2,4,5,6 are nonzero; columns3,7
vanish exactly by(2),(9). Their energies E_j are strictly positive.
Normalize the first row of(15) by alpha_1^2*E_j and the second by E_j.
The following entries are rational UPPER bounds, each divided by10000.
Rows are i=0,...,7 and columns are j=0,1,2,4,5,6 in that order.

    Left gradient against right residual:
    -9709  -8315  -7614 -15069 -19819 -18128
    -9709  -8315  -7614 -15069 -19819 -18128
    -2848  -2537  -9466 -11562 -14680 -15335
    -2858  -2545  -9464 -11565 -14680 -15329
    -9691  -8210  -7465 -14229 -13721  -9131
    -9691  -8210  -7465 -14229 -13721  -9131
   -17587 -19994 -14117  -2457    -73  -5919
   -17579 -19997 -14136  -2420     -2  -5864

    Right gradient against left residual:
    -8931  -7259  -7138 -16612 -24863 -24113
    -8931  -7259  -7138 -16612 -24863 -24113
    -9791  -9737  -9908 -10659 -13075 -14139
    -9791  -9737  -9907 -10659 -13071 -14133
   -10030 -10003  -9953  -9784  -8371  -7579
   -10030 -10003  -9953  -9784  -8371  -7579
   -10165 -10257 -10157  -9644  -9231  -9290
   -10165 -10257 -10157  -9644  -9232  -9291

For clarity, these are not floating solver values. Here is a complete
procedure specifying how to check the inequalities using only integer
arithmetic, with no search or black-box solver. Use D=10^18, representing
each enclosing interval by integers[l,u] for[l/D,u/D]. Constants k use
[kD,kD]. For each elementary operation round outwards immediately:

* Addition: [l1+l2,u1+u2]. Negation: [-u,-l].
* Multiplication: compute the four products l1*l2,l1*u2,u1*l2,u1*u2;
  divide the minimum by D and round down, and the maximum by D and round up.
* Division, with the denominator interval disjoint from0: take the minimum
  and maximum of the four endpoint quotients (a*D)/b, rounded down and up.
* Square root of a nonnegative interval: lower endpoint floor(sqrt(lD));
  upper endpoint ceil(sqrt(uD)), determined by integer squaring.

These operations enclose their real counterparts: each expression is
continuous on its interval and each extremum is at the indicated endpoint
or, for sqrt, follows from monotonicity. This proves containment by
induction through the defining expressions(1),(2),(5)-(15).

The evaluation order is fixed. Start with sqrt2, then a,b,rho,eps,Klen,z,
m,eta and the exact vectors(1)-(2); use E_i from the explicit positive
formulas. Compute r_0,alpha_0,B(W),B(p), and the eight X,G,Y via(7)-(9).
Then compute B(w),B(P),alpha_1,C, and the two expressions(15), normalize
by their strictly positive energies, and round their upper endpoints
upwards to the grid1/10000. This gives exactly the two displayed tables.
In particular every required normalized cross row is at most-2/10000.

Some intermediate intervals, also with common denominator D, are

    sqrt2:  [1414213562373095048,1414213562373095049]
    a:      [1412734726326775199,1412734726326775201]
    b:      [2004379028329949201,2004379028329949202]
    eta:    [ 416570168687622134, 416570168687622136]
    alpha0: [ 414446744233737068, 414446744233737076]
    alpha1: [ 414680057364296474, 414680057364296582].

Every division interval is disjoint from0. The full interval endpoints
for all96 rows and a literal implementation of the above operations are
public in max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.json and
max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.py. The fixed calculation completed
in0.016976418999547604 seconds. Its logical purpose is the finite list of
rational inequalities displayed here; it is not a numerical optimality
certificate and not a substitute for verifier acceptance of this draft.

There are128 cross rows including the32 identically zero residual-column
rows. The128 within-child rows including diagonals and the33 optimum
incidences/diagonal give289 total original rows including diagonals.
This accounting includes every old/old pair, bridge pair, both optimum
incidences, and both directions at the final sample.

## 5. The quantitative signed-state law and the precise next gap

The transverse repair does not alter the endpoint norm multiplication,
but it changes the signed boundary pairings. The exact update can be
stated for any legal endpoint-saturated child with initial P, endpoint w,
actual vectors G_i,Y_i, and E_i=<G_i,Y_i>. Choose a real transverse
contraction C in the specified frames and scale alpha=r*||w||/R, using
(11),(14). For the parent P'=L(P), w'=alpha*Rmap(w):

    <P',G'_i>=<P,G_i>,       <P',Y'_i>=<P,Y_i>,
    <G'_i,w'>=B_C(G_i,w),   <Y'_i,w'>=B_C(Y_i,w),        (17L)

    <P',G'_(M+i)>=B_C(P,G_i),
    <P',Y'_(M+i)>=B_C(P,Y_i),
    <G'_(M+i),w'>=alpha^2*<G_i,w>,
    <Y'_(M+i),w'>=alpha^2*<Y_i,w>.                      (17R)

Here M is the number of child samples in this section. The energy list
updates to(E,alpha^2*E), the initial
radius stays R by the retained cone representation, and the endpoint
norm becomes alpha^2*||w||^2. For r=theta*A and A'=lambda*A,

    kappa'=kappa^2.                                     (18)

Every sign and transverse term in(17) is retained. The exact legality
test remains the full inequalities(15). The four scalar lists alone do
not determine the transverse vectors, so discarding them would lose the
mechanism that makes the present repair possible.

The construction gives C=0 in the first2-by2 transverse space and the
explicit4-by4 C in(10) for the next join. The resulting actual child now
has15 steps and lives in R^9. Its next balanced parent would have31 steps,
bridge1+theta*A_2, and an8-by8 transverse block. Producing such a block
with all original inequalities, and then a uniform all-depth rule, remain
open. No n>15 computation was run or authorized.

Two naive continuations do not constitute a proof. Setting all future
C=0 discards exactly the transverse terms that repaired the earlier
queried row(2,9). Padding(10) with zero rows and columns preserves the
contraction bound but does not establish the new cross-normal inequalities
because the frames and signed vectors have changed. Neither a verified
31-step parent nor an exact obstruction to all its transverse choices is
claimed. Equations(17)-(18) state the interface that a further construction
has to preserve, rather than treating a finite coupling as an asymptotic
iteration theorem.

## 6. Scope, independent residual proposal, and public evidence

The new data here have x_end=g_end and a three-vector terminal gradient;
they are NOT the two-vector orthogonal seed excluded by the older
63-step all-earlier-coupling separator. The finite fifteen-step repair
therefore does not contradict that scoped earlier obstruction.

A separate joint candidate,
max_nonzero_terminal_residual_and_angle_obstruction.md, changes the
terminal gradient to rho*V+(eps/2)*q_axis and pays a terminal value loss.
It constructs an actual7-step parent but excludes every next coupling of
those fixed children by four original rows. That different value/vector
prescription must not be substituted into the present finite success or
used to declare this original fixed-child problem infeasible.

All words in this manuscript retain the mandated diagnostic schedule.
The quantities A,A_1,A_2 are actual shifted word sums, not the exact
Zhang-Jiang objective profile B_n. Neither(16) nor(18) resolves the original
all-h/all-horizon comparison. The present deliverable is an explicit
finite common-function coupling and its complete unverified proof.

Public evidence lives under the shared project research directory:

* max_changed_geometry15_discovery.py: the baseline and free-block source.
* max_changed_geometry15_rational_replay.py: the fixed rational-block source.
* max_changed_geometry15_raw/attempt_01,02,03.npz and matching .json files:
  original statuses, all Q arrays, full contraction blocks and dual arrays.
* max_changed_geometry15_exact_replay.py and .json: exact defining data,
  outward-integer procedure and all96 rational interval enclosures.
* max_changed_geometry15_discovery_budget.json: pre-recorded attempts,
  final charged count4, actual optimizer count3 and exact-replay count1.
* max_changed_geometry15_prior_handoff.json: hashes of the copied earlier
  sources and all three old raw cases; no old solver was rerun.

No optimization status is used as a proof premise, and no new verified
fact is claimed until the independent fact_submit gate is authorized and
accepts a self-contained submission.

# Full transverse coupling: exact separation of the actual 63-step parent

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, worker max,
ninth launch, cumulative round 20. The verifier has not accepted this result.
The current TASK and public ledger allow zero verification calls. The only
accepted fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for the definition and necessity of the
original smooth-convex inequalities Q. Every other assertion used in this
package is derived below, including the construction of the input children.

## 1. Statement and scope

Put s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda. Choose a>0 and xi>=theta,
and define H=a+1, b=1+H*xi, D=1+3xi+xi^2. The seed word is (a,b,a).
Its actual orthogonal-core transcript is given in section 3. Recursively
form the words h[0]=(a,b,a) and

    A_j=1+sum h[j]=lambda^j A_0,  A_0=H(2+xi),
    r_j=theta A_j,  h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]).              (1)

Thus h[j] has 4*2^j-1 steps. At every join use isometric copies of the
complete child transcripts, leave the left copy unscaled, scale the right
copy by alpha=r_j ||w||/||x_0||, and align its initial point with -r_j w.
Keep the projection values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and the optimum jet 0.
No extra gradient coordinate, translation, or function-value change is made.

The following are the claims of this joint candidate.

1. The most general real cross Gram for this operation is parameterized
   by a completely unrestricted real contraction between the two fixed
   transverse spaces. Section 2 gives its exact PSD condition, both sets of
   cross normals, all bridge/optimum constraints, explicit realization,
   common projection function when feasible, and true distance.
2. For zero transverse cross map at the first three joins, the complete
   7-, 15-, and 31-step children are legal. If xi>theta they have strictly
   positive core defect. Nevertheless, for the two actual 31-step children,
   **every** real transverse map at the 63-step join violates

       Q_(36,30)=e_30 [lambda T(xi)-1]>0,
       T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/D^2,  e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.   (2)

   This includes all maps satisfying the full PSD condition, without a rank,
   sign, symmetry, or boundary-positivity restriction.
3. The previously observed failed row Q_(32,25) is NOT invariant. Its
   transverse coefficient is nonzero. The different row (2) is the exact
   separator; it was not licensed to be inferred from the planar example.
4. If xi>theta, infeasibility persists even when arbitrary transverse maps
   are chosen at every earlier join, and even if different choices are made
   in different subtrees. Every such 63-step construction, with all earlier
   children legal if possible, satisfies the two-row identity

       [(1+xi) Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
          = lambda q-1
          = (xi-theta)(s*xi+lambda)/D >0,
       q=xi(2+xi)/D.                                    (3)

   The two weights are strictly positive. Thus the claimed parent cannot
   satisfy all original Q inequalities. This broader claim needs xi>theta;
   (2), for the fixed rank-one-built children, also covers xi=theta.
5. Re-selecting arbitrary real function values cannot repair either
   obstruction while the displayed positions, gradients, and optimum at
   zero are retained. The projection values are forced separately on every
   embedded seed copy, as proved at the end of section 5.

For the particular modified-word interval requested by TASK, let

    p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3,
    a=s-p0*t, b=2+q0*t, 0<=t<=1/50,
    xi=(b-1)/(a+1).                                     (4)

Then a>0 and xi>=theta, with strict inequality for t>0. The SAME algebraic
seed t=1/100 was used for every discovery comparison. Explicitly it is

    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    H=(284+311s)/300, xi=(290+8s)/(284+311s),
    A_0=(143+105s)/50.                                  (5)

The words, physical child transcripts, and normalization are unchanged
between the zero-map and full-map 63-step tests. These claims exclude this
specified recursive projection construction. They do not exclude different
seed transcripts, lifts, translations, different optimum alignment, or operations, and
do not resolve the original all-schedule, all-horizon exact-profile goal.

## 2. All real transverse freedom and every original constraint

A child input consists of a finite nonnegative word p of length ell and
vectors x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, including all duplicates and the endpoint.
Define y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, w=x_ell=g_ell!=0, and require

    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, e_i>=0.              (6)

Set K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell) and

    F_K(x)=max_(v in K)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_star=0.                (7)

Completing the square proves that the maximizer is P_K(x), the Euclidean
projection. Its unique minimizer condition is
<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K. Thus (6) makes g_i=P_K(x_i).
Adding this condition at two points gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(z)||^2<=<x-z,P_K(x)-P_K(z)>, hence P_K is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the two maxima at the opposite maximizers gives
0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=||d||^2. Consequently F_K is differentiable,
convex and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K, and it interpolates (7).

The original residual from fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                 (8)

Expansion of the inner products proves this identity. In particular,
Q_i,star=0 and Q_star,i=-e_i. These include both optimum incidences.

The minimizer set of F_K is K^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in K}.
For necessity, test epsilon*v in (7), divide by epsilon, and let epsilon
decrease to zero. Sufficiency follows directly from (7), including v=0.
The update equations imply x_0=w+sum_(i<ell)p_i g_i. For every minimizer z,
<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Zero is therefore a closest
minimizer: the TRUE distance is R=||x_0||>0.

For two children L,R, set R_R=||x_R,0||, e=w_L/||w_L||, d=x_R,0/R_R,
alpha=r||w_L||/R_R for the bridge 1+r, where r>0. Let E=e^perp in the
left child span and Dspace=d^perp in the right child span. Write
u_perp=u-<u,e>e and v_perp=v-<v,d>d.

Every allowed pair of real isometries J_L,J_R satisfies J_R d=-J_L e.
It therefore has cross form

    B_C(u,v)=<J_L u,J_R v>
       =-<u,e><v,d>+<u_perp,C v_perp>,                    (9)

where C:Dspace->E is linear and ||C||<=1. To prove necessity, project
J_R v_perp orthogonally onto J_L E; its norm is at most ||v_perp||.
All axial/transverse cross terms vanish because the transverse images are
orthogonal to the common axis. No other restriction on C follows.

Conversely, for any real C with ||C||<=1, put S_C=(I-C^T C)^(1/2), the
positive semidefinite real square root. In R direct-sum E direct-sum Dspace,
define

    J_L u=(<u,e>,u_perp,0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>,C v_perp,S_C v_perp).                   (10)

The equality C^T C+S_C^2=I proves both isometries, their alignment, and (9).
Zero eigenvalues are retained; nothing is clipped or discarded. In chosen
orthonormal transverse bases, the exact PSD condition is

    [[I,C],[C^T,I]] >=0.                                (11)

Indeed its quadratic form at (z,v) is
||z+C v||^2+<v,(I-C^T C)v>. Setting z=-Cv proves necessity, and the same
identity proves sufficiency. Thus (11) is equivalent to realizability,
including singular cases. For the fixed 31-step children their full spans
have dimension 9 and C is an arbitrary 8 by 8 real matrix. The ranks 2,3,5,9
of the zero-map seed and successive joins follow from the direct-sum
realization (10): the two spans intersect exactly in their aligned axis.
The gradients span each child space, because the seed U,V do and every
join retains both embedded spanning families.

Use J_L(x_L,i,g_L,i,y_L,i) as all left data and
alpha J_R(x_R,j,g_R,j,y_R,j) as all right data. Let m=ell_L+1, so that
right child sample j has parent index m+j. The bridge equation is exact:

    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=alpha J_R x_R,0.               (12)

Left values remain f_L,i, and right values are alpha^2 f_R,j. All original
constraints are precisely the following list:

    Q_(i,k)=Q^L_(i,k)                                      (left/left),
    Q_(m+j,m+l)=alpha^2 Q^R_(j,l)                         (right/right),
    Q_(m+j,i)=alpha B_C(y_L,i,g_R,j)-e_L,i,               (13L)
    Q_(i,m+j)=alpha B_C(g_L,i,y_R,j)-alpha^2 e_R,j,       (13R)
    Q_(i,star)=Q_(m+j,star)=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-e_L,i, Q_(star,m+j)=-alpha^2 e_R,j.

Every i,k ranges over the entire left sample list and every j,l over the
entire right sample list. Diagonal rows are zero. There are no other rows.
In particular the left endpoint/bridge sample i=ell_L has y_L,i=0, so
Q_(m+j,ell_L)=0, while its reverse rows are

    Q_(ell_L,m+j)
      = r||w_L||^2/R_R^2 [-<x_R,0,y_R,j>-r e_R,j].       (14)

These rows are invariant under C. All terminal-residual rows are zero
because y_R,ell_R=0. If a left residual happens to equal a_pos*w_L with
a_pos>0 and its gradient is w_L, the other invariant rows are

    Q_(m+j,i)=a_pos||w_L||^2[-r c_R,j-1],
    c_R,j=<g_R,j,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                          (15)

Equations (13L)-(13R) are the TWO full sets of original cross normals;
neither is replaced by a sign condition or by PSD. If and only if all their
rows are nonpositive, (7) for the parent convex hull constructs one common
function, checks every Q, and gives true initial distance R_L. The endpoint
is W=alpha J_R w_R. For capacities A=1+sum p_L and B=1+sum p_R, and
P=A+B+r, put kappa_L=A^2||w_L||^2/R_L^2 and
kappa_R=B^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2. Direct substitution gives

    kappa_parent=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 B^2).       (16)

The choice rP=AB yields their product. For identical capacities the
positive solution is r=theta A and P=lambda A, giving (1). This ratio law
does not imply legality. No ratio is used as an actual payoff when (13)
fails.

## 3. The actual seed, positive interval, and complete legal children

Let U,V be orthogonal vectors with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=xi. Define

    (x_0,g_0)=(H(1+xi)U+HV,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+HV,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(HV,V), (x_3,g_3)=(V,V).                 (17)

Its word is (a,b,a), and direct subtraction checks all three updates.
Its residuals are

    y_0=(a+Hxi)U+HV, y_1=Hxi U+HV, y_2=aV, y_3=0.

At samples 0 and 1 the normal against V is respectively -a and 0; the
normal against 0 is respectively -(a+Hxi) and -Hxi. The normals against U
are zero. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 both equal -a xi and
the normal against V is zero. At sample 3 all normals are zero. These
list all distinct vertices of conv(0,U,V), including duplicate gradients.
Thus every Q is nonpositive, with e_i>0 for i=0,1,2. The seed is an actual
instance of (6)-(7), and

    R^2=H^2 D, ||w_0||^2=xi,
    k_0=<x_0,w_0>/R^2=xi/(HD),
    zeta_0=||w_0||^2/R^2=xi/(H^2D),
    kappa_0=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa_0-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                    (18)

Since xi^2+2xi-1=(xi-theta)(xi+lambda), the defect is positive precisely
when xi>theta. To check (4), the rational inequalities 7/5<s<10/7 follow
by squaring. They imply 0<p0<1/5 and q0>2/5, so a>0 throughout the stated
interval. Also H=lambda-p0 t>0 and
xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H, whose coefficient is positive. This proves the
interval and positive-defect assertions without floating arithmetic.

Here is a complete legality check for the fixed zero-map children, needed
to ensure that the final obstruction is not caused by illegal inputs.
For e_i>0 retain the signed lists

    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i,
    c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i.

All gradient samples are retained in a_i,c_i. Residuals with e_i=0 are
exactly zero and satisfy all their rows automatically. For two identical
children with C=0, (13) is equivalent to

    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,
    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                             (19)

The seed lists are nonnegative. Their attained maxima are

    max a_i=1, max B_i=1, max c_i=c_*=(1+xi)/(HD),
    max D_i=A_0.

For the last assertion, D_1=A_0, D_2=H, and
D_0=H(1+xi)+H^2xi/(a+Hxi)<=H(2+xi). Also B_0=Hxi/(a+Hxi)<=1,
B_1=B_2=1. Hence the first zero-map join is legal by (19).

For a zero-map join the exact transported lists are

    a_left=-k/(r zeta) a,   a_right=a,
    B_left=-rk B,          B_right=B,
    c_left=c,              c_right=-rk c,
    D_left=D,              D_right=-k/(r zeta) D,
    k_new=-r k^2,          zeta_new=r^2 zeta^2.           (20)

These formulas follow from (9) with C=0 and the definitions; the right
energies and endpoint squared norms carry alpha^2 and the left initial
norm is unchanged. Set omega=1/(2+xi), q=A_0 k_0=xi(2+xi)/D,
u=lambda omega, and v=theta q. For xi>=theta, u<=1, while 0<q<1 because
D-xi(2+xi)=1+xi. After the first join the attained extreme intervals are

    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*],
    D_i in[-u A_0,A_0].                                (21)

Induction in (20) gives, for j>=1,
A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and k_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j).
The new copying factors in (20) are positive and at most one. Each old
extremum is retained by the unmultiplied copy of its corresponding list.
Therefore (21) remains the exact list of extrema at every later formal
zero-map join. For j>=1, (19) becomes

    r_j v c_* = lambda^(j-2) T(xi) <=1,
    u A_0 <= r_j.                                      (22)

The polynomial identity

    D^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=1+2xi+3xi^2+xi^3>0

shows 0<T(xi)<1. Thus j=1 and j=2 satisfy both inequalities (22), since
r_1=A_0 and r_2=lambda A_0. This proves legality of h[1],h[2],h[3], with
all original normals. Formula (16) gives kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j) at these
legal levels. Their functions, gradients, values and TRUE distance are
those in section 2, not just formal Gram matrices.

## 4. Why the old failed row moves, but a different row cannot move

For the actual fixed child h[3], index 0 is the original seed gradient U,
and the initial point is the original seed x_0 under the leftmost isometry.
Consequently the squared norm of its component orthogonal to x_0 is

    ||(g_0)_perp||^2=1-(1+xi)^2/D=xi/D>0.              (23)

Child residual y_25 comes from seed residual y_1 inside the first left
half of h[1], followed by two right-copy embeddings. Let beta=alpha_1
alpha_2>0 be their combined scale. The h[1] endpoint w_1 and its residual
y_1 satisfy

    ||y_1||^2=H^2 xi(1+xi),
    <y_1,w_1>=-r_0 xi^2/D,
    ||w_1||^2=r_0^2 xi^2/(H^2D).

These follow by substituting (17) into (9) at C=0. The right-copy
isometries carry BOTH vectors with the same beta. Thus the left residual
component orthogonal to w_3 has squared norm

    ||(y_25)_perp||^2
      =beta^2 H^2 xi [1+xi-xi/D]>0.                   (24)

Equations (23)-(24) prove that

    Q_(32,25)(C)=Q_(32,25)(0)
        +alpha_3 <(y_25)_perp,C(g_0)_perp>             (25)

has a nonzero linear coefficient. For example, the norm-one map
v -> -<(g_0)_perp,v>(y_25)_perp/
      (||(g_0)_perp|| ||(y_25)_perp||)
strictly decreases this row. This assertion concerns that row only; it
does not claim simultaneous feasibility of the other rows.

Now use the DIFFERENT residual y_30. At every join the final two seed
gradients remain equal to the new endpoint w. Since the last step is a,

    g_30=g_31=w_3, x_30=H w_3, y_30=a w_3,
    e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.                                (26)

This is an actual sampled residual, not a boundary surrogate. It has zero
transverse component. At the FIRST zero-map join the right copy of seed
gradient 0 has index 4. Formula (20) gives c_4=-r_0 k_0 c_*=-v c_*.
Its two later LEFT embeddings preserve this gradient/initial pairing and
R^2, so it remains c_4=-v c_* in h[3]. At the final join, child gradient 4
has parent index 32+4=36. Substitution of (26) into (15) gives exactly (2).

To establish its sign on the entire stated interval, logarithmic
differentiation and multiplication by the positive denominator gives

    [T'(xi)/T(xi)] xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D
       =2+xi+xi^2+xi^3>0.

Also T(theta)=2-s, by theta^2+2theta=1. Therefore
lambda T(xi)-1 >= lambda(2-s)-1 = s-1=theta>0.
This is a one-row exact affine separation. Its PSD multiplier can be
zero: after enforcing the alignment, the left side of this single
original required inequality is a positive constant on the ENTIRE
realizable cross-Gram set, indeed on the affine space of all C. No
floating solver verdict is used in the proof.

For comparison, the old row has Q_(32,25)(0)/e_25=lambda T(xi)-1 by (21):
B_25=-v and c_0=c_*. This equality does not make it invariant; (25) gives
its nonzero derivative. The invariant bridge-gradient obstruction for the
separate planar core is unnecessary here. No planar formula has been
applied to this orthogonal core.

## 5. A two-row separator when earlier cross maps are also free

We now rederive a stronger obstruction without assuming C=0 at ANY earlier
join. Every first join still has the exact bridge identity

    H(1+xi) g_4 + H g_6 = -r_0 w_0.                    (27)

Here g_4 is the scaled right copy of U, g_6 the scaled right copy of V,
and w_0 the left seed endpoint. Indeed the left side is the isometric
image of alpha_0 x_seed,0, so (12) proves (27) for every allowed transverse
map. Take inner product with the retained initial point x_0 and divide by
R^2. Left-copy embeddings preserve this equality and all its scalars.
Thus in the leftmost 7-step subtree of ANY 31-step right child,

    [(1+xi)c_4+c_6]/(2+xi)
        =-r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.                     (28)

Both gradients remain at indices 4 and 6 of that child. No agreement
between choices in different subtrees is needed: their initial point and
initial norm equal the common seed's up to an isometry, and (28) has the
same right side. The left 31-step child also retains its last seed pair,
so it satisfies (26) with its own nonzero w_3 and the SAME positive a.

Using (15) on the final join and averaging the rows j=4,6 with the
positive weights (1+xi)/(2+xi),1/(2+xi), the result is

    [(1+xi)Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
      =r_3 theta k_0-1 =lambda A_0 k_0-1
      =lambda xi(2+xi)/D-1.

The final factorization is

    lambda xi(2+xi)-D=(xi-theta)(s xi+lambda).           (29)

For xi>theta this proves (3) strictly, contradicting the required two
nonpositive original Q rows. The entire fixed-seed repeated operation
is therefore infeasible at length 63 for positive defect, regardless of
all transverse choices at all depths. This conclusion is conditional
only on the precisely specified isometric copies, bridge, projection
values, and absence of lifts/other changes. It is not a no-go theorem for
other children or for the unrestricted worst-case problem.

At xi=theta the right side of (3) is zero, so this argument does NOT
exclude arbitrary earlier couplings at length 63 in that limiting case.
For completeness, the same retained average at a later top bridge r_j
would require lambda^(j-2)q<=1. At j=4 this fails even when xi=theta,
since lambda^2 q=lambda>1. This is a textual statement about the recursive
rule, not a numerical evaluation above the assigned horizon cap.

Function values are rigid on these data. To prove this without assuming
the projection model as a restriction, give a seed arbitrary values
f_i=f_i^proj+delta_i, with f_star=0 and g_star=x_star=0, and require all
original Q inequalities. At sample 3, x_3=g_3 and y_3=0. Its two optimum
inequalities read delta_3<=0 and -delta_3<=0, so delta_3=0. Since g_2=g_3,
the two original rows (2,3),(3,2) both had zero projection residual and
now force delta_2=delta_3=0. The rows (2,1) and (1,3) also had zero
projection residual: the first is <V-U,Hxi U+HV>=0, and the second uses
y_3=0. They force delta_2<=delta_1<=delta_3, hence delta_1=0. Finally
g_0=g_1 makes both rows (0,1),(1,0) zero before the value perturbation,
and therefore delta_0=delta_1=0. Every step uses original Q rows.

An isometry followed by a positive homothety preserves these zero
residuals and the property x_3=g_3. Every queried sample in the recursive
construction belongs to a four-sample seed copy, since joins add only the
bridge step between existing endpoints, without adding another sample
type. Apply the preceding eight-row argument to each copy together with
the COMMON optimum sample 0. All its delta_i vanish. Thus every function
value is forced, even when arbitrary cross maps were used in building
the child spaces. Equations (2)-(3) exclude every convex differentiable
1-smooth interpolant of these positions and gradients having a minimizer
at 0; merely changing its values or its representation does not escape.
This does not address a changed optimum sample or altered vector data.

## 6. What signed state must be retained, and why this operation stops

An exact reusable state for arbitrary transverse joins may keep the full
Gram of (x_0,w,g_0,...,g_ell,y_0,...,y_ell), the word, and the energies
e_i. These data determine the projected transverse Gram blocks, (13),
and (11), including every nullspace. Four scalar extrema alone do not
parameterize an arbitrary transverse feasibility problem.

There is a small necessary signed part of that state which already
separates the present family. If a child has a residual y_i=a_pos*w and
g_i=w, a_pos>0, (15) forces

    c_R,j>=-1/r for EVERY right gradient j.             (30)

Thus the left endpoint-facing residual must be paired with the entire
signed list of right initial/gradient correlations. It cannot be deleted
as an irrelevant transverse direction. Any retained convex combination
of these c_R,j must obey the same bound. The positive combination (28)
forces r theta k_0<=1, which contradicts the actual fourth bridge for a
positive-defect seed. This invariant survives arbitrary transverse
rotations and contractions at all earlier levels.

A broader finite-depth obstruction uses only a few seed features. Consider
ANY nonnegative seed word p of capacity A_0=1+sum p_i with an actual
projection core, initial norm R>0, endpoint w, and
k_0=<x_0,w>/R^2>0. Suppose one of its samples has g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w with gamma,delta>0. Use the same unlifted isometric copying
operation with balanced bridges r_j=theta lambda^j A_0, allowing every
transverse map at every join. At the first join the GD identity gives

    sum p_i g_right,i + g_right,terminal = -r_0 w.

The nonnegative weights sum to A_0. Their weighted average of the signed
initial/gradient correlations is therefore -r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.
Embedding this whole first join always into the left child preserves
those correlations. Separately, embedding the displayed endpoint-facing
sample always into the right child preserves g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w relative to the current endpoint. At a later top join (15),
with the factor gamma retained, requires every right correlation to be
at least -gamma/r_j. Averaging them forces

    r_j theta k_0 <= gamma.                             (32)

Because r_j grows as lambda^j and k_0>0, this inequality eventually fails.
Thus this larger class of FIXED seeds with positive initial/endpoint
pairing and one such residual cannot be iterated indefinitely solely by
choosing transverse correlations. The statement uses projection values;
the independent value-rigidity argument in section 5 covers arbitrary
value selection for the specific orthogonal seeds of the main claim.
It does not cover seeds without the displayed positive pairing or
endpoint-facing sample, depth-dependent changes of seed data, or lifts.

For a general feasible join, the signed boundary pairings transport as

    <g_left,W>=alpha B_C(g_L,w_R),
    <y_left,W>=alpha B_C(y_L,w_R),
    <g_right,W>=alpha^2<g_R,w_R>,
    <y_right,W>=alpha^2<y_R,w_R>,
    <x_parent,0,g_left>=<x_L,0,g_L>,
    <x_parent,0,y_left>=<x_L,0,y_L>,
    <x_parent,0,g_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,g_R),
    <x_parent,0,y_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,y_R).            (31)

The cross entries of the full retained Gram are (9), with factor alpha
when exactly one vector comes from the right child. Internal right
entries have factor alpha^2. Equations (9)-(16),(31) give an exact full
coupling rule at any growing horizon WHEN feasible. They assert no
iteration theorem without proving feasibility at every step. Here the
concrete obstruction (3) decides it: arbitrary transverse freedom alone
does not make this seed family iterable.

## 7. Discovery ledger, calibration, and evidence preservation

The three recorded optimization evaluations all used t=1/100 and the
same explicit seed (5). The smaller calibration was h[2] of length 15,
whose legality is proved in section 3. Both h[4] cases used the SAME
actual h[3] children and the SAME h[4] word.

1. Length 15, C fixed to zero: numerical normalized residual objective
   8.7710378984e-11, maximum original Q about 8.33e-17.
2. Length 63, C fixed to zero: objective 0.4164822178621603;
   Q_(32,25) approximately 0.01256893369169749.
3. Length 63, full unrestricted 8 by 8 C: objective
   0.41648221754700987; largest dual weights are the duplicate original
   rows (36,30),(37,30). The contraction block is strictly PSD in this
   numerical minimax-residual solution, with smallest eigenvalue about
   0.597687. It is NOT a feasible interpolant: the positive residuals
   were neither discarded nor repaired.

The common exact predicted normalized row residual is lambda T(xi)-1,
approximately 0.416482217535. Discovery used 3 of 8 allowed optimization
evaluations and 18.623366069994517 measured build-plus-solve seconds of
60, including imports, model construction, and solver compilation. There
were 2 distinct seed/parent cases out of the allowed 4. Each attempt was
logged BEFORE evaluation and updated with actual cost afterward.

All 4,225 rows of the 63-step ordered-pair/optimum/diagonal matrix were
kept, along with full contraction PSD. The positive row scales alter only
the residual objective, not the feasibility set. Raw child coordinates,
all words, orthonormal bases, all original Q values, the entire C, all Q
dual entries, the PSD dual, the scalar dual, and complete contraction
eigenvalues are saved. No eigenvalue was clipped, no PSD repair applied,
and no nullspace deleted. Source and raw-file SHA256 hashes are recorded
in the public ledger. The executable source is
workers/max/research/max_general_transverse_discovery.py; raw files are
workers/max/research/transverse_raw/attempt_01 through attempt_03 (.npz
and .json). The ledger is research/max_general_transverse_discovery_budget.json.
The separate tiny exact audit max_transverse_exact_audit.py passed 19
checks in Q(sqrt(2)), including the seed formulas, both separator signs,
the factorization (29), and the two polynomial identities used in the
legality proof. It makes no optimization or verification call. A read-only
inspection of the saved arrays gave the identical invariant Q_(36,30)
in both length-63 runs and the two-row residual approximately
0.00256492296668. These are diagnostic consistency checks, not acceptance
by the proof verifier.

The derivations in sections 1-6 use no numerical output as a premise.
No installation, verifier call, additional solver replay, external
publication, or subagent was used. No allowance renews. This document is
an unverified proof candidate, and the project's original target remains
unresolved.

## 8. Scope test: a changed actual seed delays this particular separator

This is a separate construction on the SAME three-step word (5), not a
replacement of the fixed children in the preceding discovery comparisons.
It sharpens the scope of the result; it is NOT a legal 63-step repair.
There was no optimization evaluation on this changed seed.

Keep the exact a,b of (5), and set

    rho=599/600, Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b,
    eta=z/Klen, Rnew^2=m^2+Klen*z, A=1+2a+b.

Take orthogonal U,V with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=eta, and define

    (x_0,g_0)=(mU+Klen V,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+Klen V,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(Klen V,V), (x_3,g_3)=(rho V,rho V).      (33)

The residuals are y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V, y_1=zU+Klen V,
y_2=(Klen-1)V, y_3=0. The three GD updates have steps a,b,a.
Here Klen>1 and 0<rho<1. At samples 0 and 1 the normals against V are
-a and 0, and the normals against 0 are -(m-1),-z. Normals against rho V
are no larger than those against V because all the V/residual pairings
are nonnegative. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 equal
-(Klen-1)eta, and the normal against rho V equals
-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta. At sample 3 all residuals vanish. Normals against
each sample's own gradient vanish. This checks every distinct vertex of
conv(0,U,V,rho V). Section 2 supplies the actual common function, values,
and TRUE distance Rnew, since x_0=rho V+(a+b)U+aV is a nonnegative
combination of the actual gradients.

This seed still has positive defect, with

    kappa_new=A^2 rho^2 eta/Rnew^2 >1+1/2000.            (34)

It also has an explicit legal first transverse join and an optimal signed
initial/gradient minimum at that join. Take r_0=theta A and use transverse
bases U on the left and
(Klen sqrt(eta)/Rnew, -m/Rnew) on the right in the orthonormal coordinates
(U,V/sqrt(eta)). The latter vector is perpendicular to x_0/Rnew and has
norm one. Let the scalar cross map be c in [0,1]. The scaled physical
cross matrix on the pair (U,V) is exactly

    r_0 rho eta/Rnew^2 * [[Klen*c, -m*c],[-m,-z]].       (35)

This follows directly from (9) with w=rho V and
alpha=r_0 rho sqrt(eta)/Rnew. It is realized by (10), with scalar
S_C=sqrt(1-c^2), so no PSD correction is implicit.

All cross gradient/residual inner products in BOTH directions are
nonpositive. To check the only possibly positive terms, remove the
positive prefactor in (35). Pair a right U with the three nonzero left
residuals; the results are

    Klen[(m-1)c-m], Klen[zc-m], -m(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c<=1 and m-z=a+1>0. Pair a left U with
the three nonzero right residuals; the results are

    -Klen*c, -Klen*(a+1)*c, -m*c*(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c>=0. Every remaining pair uses the V row
or column of (35), whose entries are nonpositive, with nonnegative
residual coefficients; rho V contributes an additional positive factor.
The fourth residuals are zero. Subtracting the nonnegative energies in
(13) checks every cross Q. Thus this is an actual legal seven-step join
for EVERY c in [0,1], with true distance Rnew and ratio kappa_new^2.

The retained signed correlations of its right U,V gradients are

    c_U=r_0 rho*m*z*(c-1)/Rnew^4,
    c_V=-r_0 rho*eta*(m^2 c+Klen*z)/Rnew^4.

Choose

    c_opt=(a+1)Klen/[m(m+Klen)] in (0,1).               (36)

Then c_U=c_V=-r_0 k_new/(m+Klen), where k_new=rho*z/Rnew^2.
The right rho V gradient has correlation rho*c_V and is less negative;
the left gradients have positive initial correlations. Equation
m*g_right,U+Klen*g_right,V=-r_0 w proves that their weighted average
has this value for ANY transverse coupling, so (36) maximizes the
minimum signed initial/gradient correlation over every allowed coupling
at the first join. It is an exact argument, not a numerical optimizer.

The last queried gradient in (33) is w/rho, rather than w. Thus the
endpoint-facing row threshold (30) becomes c_R,j>=-1/(rho*r). The
positive weighted combination inherited from these two first-join
gradients would, at the fourth balanced bridge r_3=theta lambda^3 A,
have normalized residual Charge_new-1, where

    Charge_new=r_3 rho*r_0 k_new/(m+Klen)<1-1/4000.     (37)

Consequently that inherited two-row certificate is strictly NEGATIVE
for this changed actual seed; it cannot exclude the 63-step parent.
Neither existence of legal intervening 15-,31-step general-coupling
children nor feasibility of its 63-step parent is asserted here.

For a mechanically checkable sign audit of (34),(37), their exact
expressions in Q(sqrt(2)) are

    kappa_new-1 =
      [-19019047380837966731+13474265781546157318*s]
          /40842989603870102500,

    1-Charge_new =
      [341554841617983701-241357907739927883*s]
          /603687956995287500.

For the first margin, write the displayed positive integers in its
numerator and denominator as P1,Q1,D1, so the numerator is -P1+Q1*s.
Then

    2(2000 Q1)^2-(2000 P1+D1)^2
       =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.

Both compared quantities are positive, so this proves (34). For the
second margin write its numerator as P2-Q2*s and its denominator as D2.
The quantity 4000 P2-D2 is positive, and

    (4000 P2-D2)^2-2(4000 Q2)^2
       =789713290761361657120990345679724250000>0.

This proves (37). The seven exact sign/formula checks are recorded in
max_transverse_changed_seed_scope_audit.json. This is trivial scalar
arithmetic, not a parameter sweep, conic evaluation, or verification call.

Finally, this example does NOT evade (32): it still has k_new>0 and the
endpoint-facing constants gamma=1/rho and delta=(Klen-1)/rho>0. Fixed
copies therefore fail at some later depth under arbitrary transverse
coupling. The example demonstrates a real scope distinction between the
specific 63-step separator and the broader eventual obstruction, and
identifies why changing only this scalar terminal ratio is insufficient
for an all-depth construction.
