# A priced nonzero terminal residual and an original-row separation

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21,
the final authorized round of the existing ninth launch. This residual
construction and separator were derived analytically, without an optimizer
or new mathematical computation. A later separate four-slot diagnostic
for the ORIGINAL zero-terminal-residual seed is recorded in the companion
max_exact_changed_geometry15_parent.md. No verifier call, installation,
or subagent was used for either. None of the claims below is a new fact.
The original all-schedule exact-profile target remains open.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation convention
and its necessity. All new constructions and estimates are derived here;
earlier unverified findings are motivation, not proof premises. Local
notation is defined independently below.

## 1. An unavoidable terminal price

Normalize the optimum sample to x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and smoothness to1.
For actual terminal position x, gradient w, value f, and residual v=x-w,
the original optimum inequality is

    Q_(end,star)=f-<w,x>+||w||^2/2
                =f-||x||^2/2+||v||^2/2 <=0.             (1)

Thus a change with x fixed and v nonzero MUST lower an old saturated value
f_old=||x||^2/2 by at least ||v||^2/2. Keeping that old value as well as x
and optimum0 would force v=0. The construction below keeps every earlier
point, gradient, and value and pays exactly this required terminal loss.
It does not pretend that the terminal value can be held fixed.

For projection jets the price is an equality:

    f=<x,w>-||w||^2/2,
    J=2f=||w||^2+2<w,v>=||x||^2-||v||^2.               (2)

A physical joining step of size1+r lands at

    d=x-(1+r)w=v-rw,
    Z=||d||^2=r^2||w||^2-2r<w,v>+||v||^2.              (3)

In particular alignment with -rw would be incorrect when v is nonzero.

## 2. The complete reusable boundary contract

A child has a finite nonnegative word p of length ell, positions x_i and
gradients g_i for0<=i<=ell satisfying x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i*g_i. Write

    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>.

Require all vertex normals and the optimum normals:

    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, and e_i>=0.           (4)

Also supply a nonnegative cone representation of x_0 in the gradients
g_0,...,g_ell. This is a separate input: a nonzero terminal residual does
not automatically give it. Define

    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    A=1+sum p_i, R^2=||x_0||^2>0,
    w=g_ell, v=y_ell, J=||w||^2+2<w,v>.

Here and throughout, a convex hull contains all listed gradients, including
duplicates and the genuine queried gradient at every joining endpoint.

Completing the square proves that the maximizer defining F_K is the unique
Euclidean projection P_K(x). Adding the two projection optimality
inequalities gives

    ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.

It follows that P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the two defining maxima at
each other's maximizers bounds
F_K(x')-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),x'-x> between0 and ||x'-x||^2.
Thus F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.
Condition(4) proves that it realizes every supplied jet.

The minimizers are exactly

    K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.

For sufficiency use the maximum defining F_K. For necessity, F_K(z)=0
implies <z,tg>-||tg||^2/2<=0 for every0<t<=1 and g in K; divide by t
and let t decrease to0. The supplied cone representation gives
<x_0,z><=0 on K^polar. Hence
||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2, with equality at z=0: the true closest-minimizer
distance is R. This is not a choice of a favorable optimum.

Expanding the original residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-e_i.   (5)

Consequently(4) checks every original row, not a selected subset.

Take two such children L,R, of capacities A,C and initial radii R_L,R_R.
For r>0 assume d=v_L-r*w_L is nonzero, and put Z=||d||^2. Set

    e=d/sqrt(Z), b=x_R,0/R_R, alpha=sqrt(Z)/R_R,
    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),
    J_R z=(<z,b>, 0, z-<z,b>b).                          (6)

The common space is R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum b^perp. These are
actual isometries. Embed all left data by J_L and all right data by
alpha*J_R. The bridge1+r is exact because

    J_L(x_L,ell-(1+r)w_L)=J_L d=alpha*J_R x_R,0.

The cross Gram is explicitly

    <J_L u,alpha*J_R z>=<u,d><x_R,0,z>/R_R^2.             (7)

Prescribe the left values unchanged and the right values multiplied by
alpha^2. Every within-child and optimum row is retained. The complete
remaining conditions, including all endpoint and bridge incidences, are

    <d,y_L,i><x_R,0,g_R,j>/R_R^2 <= e_L,i,               (8L)
    <d,g_L,i><x_R,0,y_R,j> <= Z*e_R,j                    (8R)

for every i,j. These are necessary and sufficient for this specified
embedding and value prescription, by(5) and(7). If they hold, the convex
hull of0 and all parent gradients gives one actual F_K. The retained left
cone representation gives its true initial radius R_L.

The physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its final gradient and residual
are alpha*J_R w_R and alpha*J_R v_R; both survive. Its exact energy is

    J_parent=Z*J_R/R_R^2.                                (9)

Writing kappa_L=A^2*J_L/R_L^2 and kappa_R=C^2*J_R/R_R^2,
if r*P=A*C then

    kappa_parent=[Z/(r^2*J_L)]*kappa_L*kappa_R.            (10)

In terms of tau=<w_L,v_L>/||w_L||^2 and nu=||v_L||^2/||w_L||^2,
the first factor is

    (1-2tau/r+nu/r^2)/(1+2tau).                          (11)

This keeps BOTH child energies and every terminal term. It is an energy
identity conditional on legality, not a claim that arbitrary children join.

## 3. One terminal-gradient change on the same physical word

Let

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    rho=599/600, delta=1-rho=1/600,
    0<eps<=1/100, E=eps^2,
    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50.

Take orthogonal unit vectors u,v_axis,q_axis, and define

    U=u, V=sqrt(eta)*v_axis,
    x_3=rho*V+eps*q_axis,
    w=g_3=rho*V+(eps/2)*q_axis,
    v=y_3=(eps/2)*q_axis,
    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V,
    x_2=x_3+aV, x_1=x_2+bU, x_0=x_1+aU.                (12)

The word is exactly(a,b,a), unchanged from the physical diagnostic word.
For eps=1/100 the earlier positions and gradients are exactly those of
the previously proposed three-vector seed. Only its terminal gradient is
changed. Allowing smaller eps changes the seed's q-coordinate amplitude
but never its word. We will exhibit the exact full-coupling obstruction
at eps=1/10000.

The residuals and normal energies are

    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+eps*q_axis, e_0=m-1,
    y_1=zU+Klen*V+eps*q_axis,     e_1=z,
    y_2=(Klen-1)V+eps*q_axis,    e_2=(Klen-1)eta,
    y_3=(eps/2)q_axis,          e_3=E/4.                  (13)

Here is the complete seed normal check against vertices0,U,V,w:

* At sample0, the normals against0,V,w respectively are
  -(m-1), -a, -a-delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.
* At sample1, they are -z, 0, -delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.
* At sample2, the normals against0 and U both equal -(Klen-1)eta,
  the w normal is -delta*(Klen-1)eta+E/2, and the V normal is0.
* At sample3, the normals against0,U,V all equal -E/4; the w normal is0.

The elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply

    139/100<a<143/100, 2<b<201/100,
    238/100<Klen<243/100, z>1, eta>2/5.

They give delta*z>1/600>E/2 and
delta*(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E/2. All listed normals are nonpositive;
all four e_i are positive. The function in section2 therefore realizes
these jets and every original Q. There is a genuine nonzero terminal
residual. No seed sample has both gradient and positive residual parallel
to the terminal gradient w.

For the true distance use the explicit positive cone representation

    x_0=mU+(a-rho)V+2w,                                 (14)

whose coefficients are positive. Define

    R^2=m^2+Klen*z+E, N=||w||^2=rho^2*eta+E/4,
    J=2f_3=rho^2*eta+3E/4,
    <w,v>=||v||^2=E/4.                                  (15)

In particular all earlier values are unchanged by replacing the old
g_3=x_3 by w. The terminal value decreases by E/8, exactly the amount
forced by(1). The construction carries this loss into J; it is not hidden
in a new normalization.

For completeness the gradient-energy surplus is positive too. Put
Rbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substituting the displayed a,b,rho,
m,Klen,z,A into [A^2*rho^2*z-Klen*m^2-Klen^2*z]/
[Klen*(m^2+Klen*z)] and using s^2=2 gives

    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,
    P0=19019047380837966731,
    Q0=13474265781546157318,
    D0=40842989603870102500.

All three integers are positive, and the exact integer identity

    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2
      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0

proves A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2>1+1/2000 by squaring positive numbers.
Also Rbar^2>9 and Nbar<1, so Rbar^2>4Nbar. Adding E/4 to its numerator
Nbar and E to its denominator Rbar^2 raises their ratio. Therefore

    kappa_N,0=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.                        (16)

This paragraph supplies the algebra and sign certificate within this
joint candidate; it does not assume an earlier unverified fact.

## 4. An actual seven-step parent with the residual retained

Choose r_0=theta*A>2. For any r>1 the true outgoing vector is

    d_r=v-rw=-r*rho*V+((1-r)eps/2)*q_axis,
    Z(r)=||d_r||^2=r^2*Nbar+(r-1)^2*E/4.                 (17)

Every seed gradient and residual has nonnegative coordinates in
(u,v_axis,q_axis), and d_r has nonpositive coordinates. Moreover
<x_0,g_i> and <x_0,y_i> are nonnegative. Both inequalities(8L),(8R)
hold for every pair at r=r_0: their left sides are nonpositive and their
right sides are nonnegative. This includes i=3 or j=3, so the actual
queried joining gradient and its nonzero residual are checked.

Set alpha_0^2=Z(r_0)/R^2 and use the explicit embeddings(6). The convex
hull construction is one actual smooth convex function for

    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a).                             (18)

Its closest distance is R by the retained cone representation(14).
Its terminal data are

    w_1=alpha_0*J_R w, v_1=alpha_0*J_R v,
    J_1=alpha_0^2*J, A_1=lambda*A.

The nonzero terminal residual survives in the parent. Every left e_i is
retained and every right e_i is multiplied by alpha_0^2; none is deleted.

For this seed put tau=E/(4N). Equation(15) gives nu=tau, and the terminal
shape is invariant under the right-copy scaling. Since Nbar>39/100 and
E<=1/10000, one has

    0<tau<1/15000.

On the formal balanced word recurrence

    A_j=lambda^j*A, r_j=theta*A_j,
    h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]),

the unchanged construction, if legal, would have exactly

    kappa_N,j+1=(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)*kappa_N,j^2,
    kappa_E,j=(1+2tau)*kappa_N,j,
    kappa_E,j+1=[(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)/(1+2tau)]
                    *kappa_E,j^2.                       (19)

Here kappa_N,j=A_j^2*||w_j||^2/R^2 and
kappa_E,j=A_j^2*J_j/R^2. For r_j>1 the first factor in the first line
exceeds1-tau. Thus the scalar energy calculation alone stays positive:

    kappa_N,j >= (1-tau)^(2^j-1)*kappa_N,0^(2^j)>1,       (20)

because(1-1/15000)(1+1/2000)>1. Equations(19)-(20) do NOT supply
legality at j>=2. The next sections exhibit the missing original rows.
They explain why the scalar surplus cannot be interpreted as a parent.

## 5. The next specified rank-one join fails at queried rows

Retain the actual seven-step child(18), including its specified first
embeddings and values. Its initial point is the retained left p=x_0,
with norm R. The next balanced bridge has r_1=theta*A_1=A. Abbreviate

    Z0=Z(r_0), Z1=Z(r_1), D=m+Klen=A-delta,
    H0=r_0*rho*z+(r_0-1)E/2,
    H1=r_1*rho*z+(r_1-1)E/2.

The true outgoing vector of the seven-step child is

    d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),
    ||d_1||^2=alpha_0^2*Z1,
    alpha_1^2=||d_1||^2/R^2.                             (21)

The prospective fifteen-step parent has eight samples from the left
child, then eight samples from the right child; its right-child index j
has original parent index8+j. In the rank-one construction(6), expanding
the two cross rows gives

    Q_(2,9)=alpha_1^2*z*(C2-1),
    C2=r_0*rho*eta*H1*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1),                  (22)

    Q_(6,13)=alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*z*(C6-1),
    C6=r_1*rho*eta*H0*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1).                 (23)

For(22), child gradient2 is J_L V, while its residual1 and initial point
are retained from the seed. Use

    <J_L V,d_1>=r_0*rho*eta*H1/R^2,
    <p,y_1>=zD+E, e_1=z.

For(23), child gradient6 and residual5 are
alpha_0*J_R V and alpha_0*J_R y_1. Therefore

    <g_6,d_1>=-alpha_0^2*r_1*rho*eta,
    <p,y_5>=-H0*(zD+E)/R^2,
    e_5=alpha_0^2*z.

Substitution in(7) and(5) gives(22)-(23), without suppressing any
within-child row or optimum row. Every index2,9,6,13 is queried in the
fifteen-step word.

Here is a common exact lower bound for C2 and C6. At E=0 both equal

    C0=Klen*(A-delta)/(theta*A^2*rho^2).                  (24)

The numerator factors in(22)-(23) increase with E. Also
Z(r)<=r^2*(Nbar+E/4) for r>1. Consequently

    C2,C6 >= C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))^2.                         (25)

Squaring rational endpoints proves14142/10000<s<14143/10000.
Substitution into a,b,A yields Klen>2.411, A-delta>5.828,
A<5.8301, theta<.4143, and rho^2<.9967. All terminating decimals here
are exact rationals. Thus

    Klen*(A-delta)>2.411*5.828=14.051308,
    theta*A^2*rho^2<.4143*(5.8301)^2*.9967<14.036,
    14.036*(1+1/1000)=14.050036<14.051308.

This proves C0>1+1/1000. Since E/(4Nbar)<1/15000,

    (1+1/15000)^2<1+1/7000,
    (1+1/2000)(1+1/7000)<1+1/1000,

and(25) proves

    C2>1+1/2000, C6>1+1/2000.                            (26)

The next rank-one join is therefore illegal for the entire interval
0<eps<=1/100, despite the positive scalar recursion(20).

## 6. A quantitative angle bound on every transverse coupling

This section allows ALL transverse couplings in the fifteen-step join,
while keeping the actual seven-step child(18) fixed. The first parent
has its explicit embeddings(6). No claim about changing that earlier
embedding is made.

Let L,R be arbitrary linear isometries of the two copies of this child
into any common Euclidean space, and keep left scale1. A legal bridge
forces

    alpha_1*R p=L d_1,

where alpha_1 is necessarily the positive number in(21). Let P_d denote
orthogonal projection perpendicular to d_1 inside the left child space,
and P_p perpendicular to p inside the right child space. Orthogonal
decomposition gives the exact identity

    <L g_6,alpha_1*R y_5>
      =<g_6,d_1><p,y_5>/R^2
          +alpha_1*<L P_d g_6,R P_p y_5>.                (27)

Both mixed parallel/perpendicular terms vanish by the forced alignment.
By the ordinary Cauchy-Schwarz inequality the second term is bounded
below by -alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||. This is valid in every
dimension and for every real positive-semidefinite completion; no
particular contraction or matrix rank is imposed.

Because g_6=alpha_0*J_R V and d_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),
the transverse norm is exactly

    ||P_d g_6||
      =alpha_0*sqrt(eta-r_1^2*rho^2*eta^2/Z1)
      =alpha_0*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)/(2sqrt(Z1)).         (28)

Further ||P_p y_5||<=alpha_0*||y_1||. Divide the possible correction
in(27) by the positive row scale alpha_1^2*e_5, where e_5=alpha_0^2*z.
Equations(21),(28) imply the upper bound

    Tcorr <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)*||y_1||
                     /(2sqrt(Z0)*Z1*z)
          <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*||y_1||
                     /(2r_0*r_1^2*rho^3*eta*z).         (29)

The second inequality uses Z0>=r_0^2*rho^2*eta and
Z1>=r_1^2*rho^2*eta. From the rational bounds in section3,

    R^2<15, ||y_1||<2, r_1-1<5,
    r_0>2, r_1>5, rho^3>97/100, eta>2/5, z>1.

For the first two bounds, explicitly
R^2<(3.44)^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<15 and
||y_1||^2<1.01^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<4.
It follows from(29) that

    Tcorr <[150/(2*2*25*(97/100)*(2/5))]*eps<4eps.       (30)

The aligned contribution of(27) is still exactly(23). Thus for EVERY
coupling with the required physical alignment and the prescribed values,

    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5) >= C6-1-Tcorr
                              >1/2000-4eps.             (31)

In particular at the explicit eps=1/10000,

    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/10000>0.                  (32)

This is an exact original-row separation for all transverse choices at
the next join of these fixed actual seven-step children. Both children
are individually legal, have nonzero terminal residual, and retain the
strict energy surplus(16); their prospective fifteen-step parent cannot
exist with this alignment, internal data, and value prescription.

More generally any successful next join inside this parametrized family
must satisfy the explicit compatibility condition

    alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||
           >=alpha_1^2*e_5*(C6-1).                      (33)

For0<eps<=1/100 the bounds above give the simpler necessary condition
eps>1/8000. It is only necessary. Increasing eps beyond this bound does
not prove feasibility. The obstruction is to arbitrarily small changes
of this specified geometry, not to every nonlinear geometry.

## 7. The retained terminal normal gives a stronger four-row separation

The original interpolation rows give more information than the norm bound
in section6. This section excludes the next join for the entire interval
0<eps<=1/100, including the original q-coordinate amplitude1/100.
The actual seven-step child and its first embeddings remain fixed.

Two exact vector identities in that child are decisive:

    d_1=-r_1*rho*g_6-(r_1-1)*v_1,
    y_5=z*g_4+(a-rho)*g_6+2*g_7.                         (34)

For the first identity, use w_1=rho*g_6+v_1 and d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1.
For the second, use the seed identity
y_1=zU+(a-rho)V+2w, and apply alpha_0*J_R.
Every coefficient in the second identity is positive. The energy of the
left endpoint residual v_1 is

    e_7=<g_7,v_1>=alpha_0^2*E/4.

Let

    k=(r_1-1)/(r_1*rho)>0, T_y=z+(a-rho)+2=m+delta.

Use arbitrary second-join isometries L,R with
alpha_1*R p=L d_1, as in section6, and write G_i,Y_i for the resulting
parent gradient and residual at original index i. The first identity(34)
implies

    <G_6,Y_13>=-alpha_1^2*<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)
                        -k*<L v_1,Y_13>.

The second identity(34) says

    Y_13=z*G_12+(a-rho)*G_14+2*G_15.

Since Q_(j,7)=<G_j,L v_1>-e_7 for j=12,14,15, the following nonnegative
combination of ORIGINAL interpolation rows has no free transverse term:

    S_rows=Q_(6,13)+k[z*Q_(12,7)+(a-rho)*Q_(14,7)+2*Q_(15,7)]
          =alpha_1^2[-<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)-e_5]-k*T_y*e_7
          =alpha_1^2*e_5*[I-1-k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)],   (35)

where

    I=H0*(D+E/z)/(r_1*rho*Z0).                           (36)

Here e_5=alpha_0^2*z and the initial pairing <p,y_5> was calculated
explicitly in section5. The identity does not impose a rank or an angle
condition. The queried row(6,13) and the rows entering the queried bridge
endpoint7 all must hold. The term(15,7) is OUTGOING from the unqueried
final sample, so even the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction retains
it. There is no use of a deleted incoming-final row.

At E=0 expression(36) equals C0 from(24). Its numerator factors increase
with E, and Z0<=r_0^2*(Nbar+E/4). Hence

    I>=C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))>1+1/1200,                       (37)

because C0>1+1/1000, E/(4Nbar)<1/15000, and
(1+1/1200)(1+1/15000)<1+1/1000.

The terminal-normal charge is bounded without dropping it. We have
k<1 because r_1*delta<6/600<1, and T_y<7/2. Also r_0>12/5 and
r_1>29/5 follow from the rational bounds on s and A. Equations(17),(21),
Nbar>39/100, and R^2<15 give

    alpha_1^2=Z0*Z1/R^4
       >[(12/5)^2*(29/5)^2*(39/100)^2]/225>1/8.          (38)

The final rational comparison in(38) is, on clearing denominators,
8*144*841*1521>625*10000*225; the two sides are1473593472 and1406250000.
Consequently

    0<k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)<7E<=7/10000.

Combining this with(35),(37) gives the strict positive separation

    S_rows/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/1200-7/10000=1/7500>0.      (39)

All four coefficients in S_rows are positive. Therefore all four original
rows cannot be nonpositive. This excludes EVERY transverse coupling at
the next join of the displayed actual seven-step children for every
0<eps<=1/100. The terminal residual was not erased: its original normal
is the source of the finite charge in(35), and this charge is too small
to repair the compatibility gap.

More generally the exact necessary compatibility condition for the same
first-parent family is

    I-1 <= k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2).                        (40)

Within0<eps<=1/100, the coarse estimates already require
E>1/8400, in conflict with E<=1/10000. This is a necessary condition
only; satisfying(40) would not imply the other original rows or a parent.

## 8. Exact scope and the smallest unresolved object

There are now three distinct statements, all at UNVERIFIED draft level:

1. For every0<eps<=1/100, (12) is an actual changed terminal seed and(18)
   is one actual seven-step parent. All original interpolation rows,
   values, the physical bridge, both child effects, normal energies, and
   true closest distance are supplied explicitly.
2. For that fixed first parent, the next rank-one operation fails at
   original queried rows(2,9) and(6,13) throughout this interval.
3. For every0<eps<=1/100, EVERY transverse coupling at the next join
   fails by the nonnegative four-row combination(35)-(39). This permits
   arbitrary ambient dimension, but fixes the first parent's data and
   all displayed values. The weaker angle estimate remains a separately
   derived diagnostic; it is not the scope of the final separation.

The next concrete unresolved instance can be specified without a solver:
take the SAME formulas(12),(17),(18) with eps=1/25, preserving the word,
rho, and all other definitions. This is still an actual legal seed and
first parent. Indeed Klen>12/5 and eta>2/5 imply
delta*(Klen-1)eta>7/7500>1/1250=E/2, and delta*z>1/600>E/2.
These verify the only potentially delicate seed normals; the sign proof
of(8L),(8R) still applies. The positive cone, terminal price, and exact
energy law are unchanged. Formula(16) remains valid because its monotonic
ratio argument holds for every E>0. This larger amplitude is not excluded
by the bounds used to prove(39), and no legality of its fifteen-step join
is asserted. Its smallest pending parent is the explicit word h[2] with
the true alignment(21), all prescribed values, and all original Q rows.

Another remaining freedom is to change the FIRST seven-step embedding,
even at eps=1/100, and then form a parent. Sections6-7 do not cover
arbitrary earlier couplings. General value-height changes are also outside
this result. Thus this is a proper-family obstruction, not a claim that
all terminal residuals or all nonlinear geometry are impossible.

The physical word was retained throughout. A_j=1+sum h[j] is its actual
shifted stepsize sum, NOT the exact Zhang-Jiang objective denominator
B_(4*2^j-1). The actual seed and seven-step objective lower bounds are
respectively J/R^2 and Z0*J/R^4. No identification of these numbers with
1/B_n is made, no new horizon-dependent word is substituted, and no
asymptotic implication for the original target follows from this finite
construction or this proper-family obstruction.

Background audit only: Taylor, Hendrickx, and Glineur,
"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods", arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 proof and Corollary1,
were reread in downloads/1502.05666.txt. Their result is not imported as
a new unverified black box; the used interpolation necessity is the
accepted fact cited above and the realizing projection function is proved
directly in section2.

# One-envelope arc relay with exact closest-minimizer normalization
Status: complete constructive candidate, UNVERIFIED. No additional verifier call.
Worker max. The source of the local circular-arc idea is Ye and Liu, "Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent", arXiv:2609.09152v2, Lemma3.1 and AppendixA.1. That PDF and its proof were downloaded and read. Matlas queries did not retrieve the exact paper. The necessary local statement is re-proved below; no unverified external theorem is used as a black box. The one-envelope coupling, activation buffer, exact scale and minimizer-distance calculation below are the proposed mutation.

## 1. Statement and exact computable bound
Partition a prescribed finite nonnegative schedule as
(block_0,b_0,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),
with ordinary sums s_i. Fix epsilon>0,gamma>0 and require
b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2), 0<=i<k.
Define c=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2), R=sqrt(epsilon^2+(1+gamma)^2).

For each ordinary block independently, the finite backward recursion in Section2 supplies horizontal and vertical displacements P_i^arc,Q_i^arc. They are exact deterministic functions of the ordered steps in that block. Define
A_i^arc=P_i^arc+epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon,
M_i^arc=P_i^arc+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon},
rho_i^arc=1/M_i^arc.
Put
D_0=1, D_(i+1)=D_i rho_i^arc(b_i-1).
Here D_i is an activation length, not the Markov residual weight from earlier findings.

There is one finite max-affine nonnegative J on R^(k+1), and f=env_1 J, whose exact GD trajectory for the whole supplied schedule starts at e_0. Its closest minimizer x_star is explicitly identified, with
||e_0-x_star||^2
=(rho_0^arc)^2[(epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2].
Its terminal value is
2 f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).
Consequently, for k>=1,
W_n(h)>=
 (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2](1+2s_k)}
 times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^arc]^2.           (1)
For k=0 the construction is the ordinary one-sided Huber witness1/(1+2s_0).

There are explicit mass-only bounds. Set
T_arc=c(1+gamma)/(epsilon gamma),
H_arc=exp(T_arc)-1,
A_arc=epsilon+exp(T_arc)/epsilon.
Then P_i^arc<=c s_i, Q_i^arc<=H_arc, and
M_i^arc<=c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_arc}.
Thus (1) implies the uniform, still restricted-checkpoint bound
W_n(h)>=
 (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+c s_0)^2+exp(2T_arc)](1+2s_k)}
 times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/(c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_arc})]^2.  (2)
There is no factor1/2 multiplying the component gradients: all nonsmooth pieces are assembled first and one Moreau envelope gives global smoothness. The exact cost is the activation buffer, which changes each transfer numerator to b_i-1.

## 2. Local arc trajectory, proved from explicit normals
Let V(q)=(p(q),-q), where
p(q)=sqrt(R^2-(q+gamma)^2), 0<=q<=1.
Then V(0)=(c,0), V(1)=(epsilon,-1),
epsilon<=p(q)<=c, and 0<=q/p(q)<=1/epsilon.
Let K be the convex hull of0 and this compact arc. It lies in the disk centered at(0,gamma) with radius R, and in the half-plane with second coordinate<=0.

For a block with m ordinary steps alpha_1,...,alpha_m, set
r_m=(u_m,-y_m)=(epsilon,-1), v_m=V(1).
Work backward. Given r_j, set Delta_j=y_j+gamma-alpha_j gamma.
If Delta_j<=u_j gamma/c, set v_(j-1)=(c,0).
Otherwise set
E_j=sqrt(u_j^2+Delta_j^2),
p_(j-1)=R u_j/E_j,
q_(j-1)=R Delta_j/E_j-gamma,
v_(j-1)=(p_(j-1),-q_(j-1)).
Finally set r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j v_(j-1).
This is a finite textual recursion, not a numerical optimization or feasibility assumption.

We prove inductively u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon, v_j in the arc, and v_j the Euclidean projection of r_j onto K. At j=m these statements hold. In the turning branch,
gamma/c<Delta_j/u_j<=(1+gamma)/epsilon.
The increasing function t -> Rt/sqrt(1+t^2)-gamma takes values0 and1 at the two displayed endpoints. Hence0<q_(j-1)<=1 and the formula indeed gives V(q_(j-1)). In the other branch it gives V(0). Adding alpha_j times a vector with p>=epsilon,q>=0 preserves u>=epsilon,y>=1; the new ratio y/u is a convex combination of the preceding y/u and q/p, so remains<=1/epsilon.

In the turning branch, direct substitution gives
r_(j-1)-v_(j-1)
=[E_j/R+alpha_j-1][v_(j-1)-(0,gamma)].
The coefficient is nonnegative: the reverse triangle inequality gives
E_j>=sqrt(u_j^2+(y_j+gamma)^2)-alpha_j gamma>=R-alpha_j gamma,
so the coefficient is>=alpha_j(1-gamma/R)>=0.
The vector in brackets is an outward disk normal and therefore a normal to K at v_(j-1). In the capped branch put mu=u_j/c+alpha_j-1. Its branch inequality gives y_j<=gamma mu, so mu>0, and
r_(j-1)-(c,0)=mu(c,-gamma)+(0,gamma mu-y_j).
Both summands are nonnegative multiples of normals to K at(c,0). Thus, in both branches,
<r_(j-1)-v_(j-1),w-v_(j-1)><=0 for every w in K.
Expanding squared distances proves the claimed projection property directly. These formulas additionally prove u_j>=p_j at every index.

Set
P^arc=sum_j alpha_j p_(j-1), Q^arc=sum_j alpha_j q_(j-1).
Then r_0=(epsilon+P^arc,-1-Q^arc), and P^arc<=c sum_j alpha_j.
Once the capped branch occurs, it persists toward the beginning: after a capped update y stays fixed, u increases by alpha_j c, and y+gamma<=gamma u_new/c; subtracting any subsequent nonnegative alpha gamma preserves the cap criterion. Thus every positive q contribution belongs to one contiguous turning portion.

Here is a self-contained, crude bound for that portion. Write sigma_j=(y_j+gamma)/u_j. At a turning update,
sigma_(j-1)=sigma_j-alpha_j gamma/u_j,
log(u_(j-1)/u_j)
=log(1+alpha_j p_(j-1)/u_j)
<= (c/gamma)(sigma_j-sigma_(j-1)).
Summing over the turning portion gives its total multiplicative increase of u at most exp(T_arc), because the terminal sigma is(1+gamma)/epsilon and the final sigma is nonnegative. Since each turning increase of u is at least epsilon alpha_j, the sum of its alpha_j is at most exp(T_arc)-1. The cap contributes q=0, so
Q^arc<=exp(T_arc)-1.
This proves all bounds stated in Section1 without the paper's more delicate turning estimate.

## 3. One local max-affine piece and its proximal margins
For one block suppress the index i and use its exact P^arc,Q^arc,b,M^arc,rho^arc. Put
z_circ=(1,0)-rho^arc r_0,
U=1-rho^arc(P^arc+epsilon b)>=0,
Y=rho^arc Q^arc.
Let V_fin consist of0,(c,0),v_0,...,v_m and define
phi(z)=rho^arc max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.
All data are fixed once the block and checkpoint are given.
The projection inequality from Section2 remains true after restricting K to conv(V_fin), since each projected vector v_j remains in that smaller set. Therefore v_j maximizes <v,r_j-v_j> over V_fin. At
x_j=z_circ+rho^arc r_j,
the point x_j-rho^arc v_j is a proximal point of phi, and grad env_1 phi(x_j)=rho^arc v_j. This follows from the subgradient optimality equation, not from an independent interpolation assertion.

The local GD path starts at(1,0), has first coordinate nonincreasing and second coordinate nondecreasing through the ordinary block, reaches
(U+rho^arc epsilon b,Y),
and the checkpoint reaches
(U,Y+rho^arc b).
The following exact zero regions and proximal margins are needed for global gluing:
(a) phi(x,y)=0 whenever x<=0,y>=0;
(b) phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y;
(c) every ordinary or checkpoint-query proximal point x_j-rho^arc v_j has first coordinate>=U>=0 and second coordinate in[0,Y+rho^arc].

To prove(a), each nonzero v=(p,-q) has p>=0,q>=0,q<=p/epsilon. At(0,0),
<v,-z_circ>=-p+rho^arc[p(epsilon+P^arc)+q(1+Q^arc)]
<=-p+rho^arc p A^arc<=0.
Monotonicity in the two coordinates then proves the whole region.
At(U,Y), each affine argument is
rho^arc[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0
because b>=1+epsilon^(-2); increasing y preserves this, proving(b).
For(c), the proximal first coordinate minus U equals
rho^arc[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0.
Its second coordinate is rho^arc(1+Q^arc-y_j+q_j), which lies between0 and rho^arc(1+Q^arc): y_j<=1+Q^arc, q_j>=0 give the lower bound, while y_j>=1>=q_j gives the upper bound.
All these checks concern the nonsmooth piece phi, as needed for the one-envelope construction.

## 4. Global maximum and the activation buffer
Set ell_0=0 and recursively
ell_(i+1)=D_i rho_i^arc(1+Q_i^arc)=D_i(Y_i+rho_i^arc).
On coordinates0,...,k define
Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k.
Let delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), and define the terminal affine hinge
Phi_k(z)=delta max{z_k-ell_k,0}.
Now take the ONE nonsmooth function
J(z)=max{Phi_0(z),...,Phi_k(z)}
and its ONE envelope
f(x)=min_z{J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.
Each Phi_i is nonnegative finite max-affine; all its nonzero affine pieces have nonpositive value at0 by Section3(a), since ell_i>=0. Hence J>=0,J(0)=0. Its envelope is globally finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth. To verify the last assertions directly, the unique proximal minimizer z(x) exists by the quadratic's coercivity and strict convexity. The subgradient equation x-z(x) in partial J(z(x)), the two minimizing inequalities, and subgradient monotonicity yield grad f(x)=x-z(x) and
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.
Thus every stated sample, including the minimum, belongs to one globally legal function.

Inductively, at the beginning of block i, coordinates j<i are frozen at ell_j+D_j U_j, coordinate i equals ell_i+D_i, and all coordinates j>i are0. During the current block use the local path of Section3 in coordinates i,i+1. At its candidate GLOBAL proximal point:
- the current piece Phi_i has the prescribed local subgradient;
- piece i-1, if present, has its first argument frozen at U_(i-1), and its second argument at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), because the current proximal first coordinate is>=ell_i; Section3(b) makes it0;
- all earlier pieces stay in their post-checkpoint zero regions;
- piece i+1, if present, has first coordinate at most ell_(i+1) by Section3(c), and its second coordinate0; Section3(a) makes it0;
- every later piece is also0 by Section3(a), or by the terminal hinge's threshold.
Thus the current piece dominates all other pieces at the candidate proximal point, even when its value is0. Its subgradient is consequently a subgradient of J. This proves every actual gradient and GD update, with no old or future affine plane discarded.

After checkpoint i, the new coordinate equals
D_i(Y_i+rho_i^arc b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_i rho_i^arc(b_i-1)
=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),
and coordinate i is frozen at ell_i+D_i U_i, proving the induction.
The buffer D_i rho_i^arc is exactly what prevents future activation at the previous proximal query.

In the terminal block the only moving coordinate has initial height D_k above ell_k. The constant gradient delta is legal at every query and at the terminal point, because for every cumulative partial sum0<=u<=s_k,
D_k-(u+1)delta>=0.
All earlier pieces remain0 by the same post-checkpoint check. Therefore
f(x_n)=delta D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta^2=D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].

## 5. The actual nearest minimizer
The minimizer set of f equals {J=0}: both terms in its defining minimum are nonnegative, and the minimum is attained.
Define x_star by its first two coordinates
(x_star)_0=1-rho_0^arc(epsilon+P_0^arc),
(x_star)_1=rho_0^arc(1+Q_0^arc)=ell_1,
and all later coordinates0.
This is the first component's translated center z_circ. Hence Phi_0(x_star)=0. The next component has first input0 and second input0, so is0 by Section3(a); all later components are0 as well. For k=1 the terminal coordinate is exactly its threshold ell_1. Thus x_star is a GLOBAL minimizer, regardless of later block choices.

It is the closest one. Every affine piece of Phi_0 is active with value0 there. Moreover
e_0-x_star=rho_0^arc(epsilon+P_0^arc,-1-Q_0^arc,0,...,0)
is the following nonnegative combination of two active normal vectors:
[(P_0^arc-epsilon Q_0^arc)/c] rho_0^arc(c,0,0,...)
 +(1+Q_0^arc) rho_0^arc(epsilon,-1,0,...).
The first coefficient is nonnegative since every p_j>=epsilon q_j. For any minimizer z, its inner product with z-x_star is therefore nonpositive. Squared-distance expansion proves
||e_0-z||^2>=||e_0-x_star||^2.
This establishes the EXACT initial distance in Section1. Dividing the terminal value by it cancels the first local scale, and gives(1). It does not assume the origin is closest.

## 6. An explicit unbounded-horizon test with all quadratic errors vanishing
Take epsilon=gamma=1/10. Then c=11/10,T_arc=121,
A_arc=1/10+10 exp(121).
Choose any fixed integer N>=max{10,A_arc}. For each m>=1 consider m repetitions of a block of N unit steps followed by checkpoint3N, with no terminal ordinary steps.
The checkpoint condition3N>=101 holds. For every block,
P_i^arc<=11N/10, A_i^arc<=11N/10+A_arc<=21N/10,
M_i^arc<=11N/10+max{3N/10,A_arc}<=21N/10.
The initial distance factor also obeys
sqrt((epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2)
<=A_0^arc<=21N/10:
the first inequality follows from 1+Q>=0 and epsilon<=1, since the sum P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon dominates the Euclidean norm.
Thus every normalized transfer factor is at least
(3N-1)/(21N/10)>=29/21.
The explicit high-dimensional nonlinear construction gives
W_(m(N+1))(h)>=(29/21)^(2m).                            (3)

For the same schedules, a scalar quadratic eigenvalue lambda in[0,1] has block multiplier
q_N(lambda)=(1-lambda)^N(1-3N lambda).
If0<=lambda<=1/(3N), its magnitude is at most exp(-N lambda).
If lambda>=1/(3N), it is at most
(3N lambda-1)exp(-N lambda)<=3exp(-4/3)<4/5.
The last strict inequality follows already by summing the degree5 Taylor lower bound for exp(4/3), which exceeds15/4. Therefore every finite-dimensional convex quadratic has normalized terminal objective at most
max{1/(2 e m N),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.
The dimension of the nonlinear witness is m+1 and its sampled checkpoint gradients contain m independent directions proportional to epsilon e_i-e_(i+1), followed by the terminal e_m direction.

The function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal coordinate system. Each coupled endpoint normal epsilon e_i-e_(i+1) can be exposed alone against the zero plane: choose a recession direction with all coordinates negative, with t_(j+1)<epsilon t_j for j!=i and t_(i+1)=epsilon t_i for j=i, where the coordinates are -t_j. Every other local arc normal then has negative inner product with that direction, while the selected endpoint has zero inner product. Starting on the selected affine hyperplane and moving sufficiently far along this direction makes every other nonzero plane strictly negative. The terminal normal e_m is exposed similarly with final coordinate0 and sufficiently negative preceding coordinates. Near each resulting hinge, the envelope has the rank-one Hessian projector onto that normal. Adjacent coupled normals, or the last coupled normal and e_m, are nonorthogonal and nonparallel, so the corresponding projectors do not commute. A function separable in one orthonormal basis would have commuting Hessians. This proves the assertion.

## 7. What remains open
This mutation permits arbitrary nonnegative ordinary steps and varying within-block gradient directions, retains all planes, gives exact nonzero offsets, supplies a finite real PSD Gram realization, and computes the true closest-minimizer distance. It has a concrete exponential-error family whose quadratic channel converges.

It does NOT prove the exact profile. The constants c>1,A_arc and the checkpoint threshold remain; the norm/cost bounds do not yet imply an arbitrary-schedule comparison to(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ. Taking epsilon,gamma small makes c approach1 but makes A_arc very large. No claim is made that optimizing the partition and parameters eliminates that loss. Formula(1), not a postulated covariance-feasibility assertion, is the new exact candidate interface.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000799 -->
# Orthogonal-moment obstruction — candidate for verification

Orthogonal-moment obstruction for randomized gradient-descent adversaries. Define c_0=0 and c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2 sqrt(c_k+1) for all integers k>=0, and define beta_n=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2. Let n>=1 be an integer, L>0, and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n be any predetermined schedule with finite entries. Consider any probability distribution on legal instances in a common finite-dimensional Euclidean space: each realization consists of a convex differentiable f:R^d->R with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and an initial point x_0 with ||x_0-x_star||=1. For each realization let x_{i+1}=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n, and set g_i=grad f(x_i)/L, including g_n. Assume all data used in expectations are measurable and E<g_i,g_j>=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n. Then
E[2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)] <= beta_n <= 2/n^2.
The deterministic conclusion follows by taking a point-mass distribution: every legal trajectory whose n+1 gradients are mutually orthogonal has normalized last-iterate objective error at most beta_n. Moreover n^2 beta_n tends to 2. Consequently, for any positive comparison sequence V_n with n^2 V_n tending to infinity, every family of such randomized adversaries has expected normalized error divided by V_n tending to zero, uniformly over schedules and distributions satisfying the assumptions. This bounds the strength of the orthogonal-moment adversary class; it is not an upper bound on the unrestricted worst-case error W_n(h), and it does not resolve the project's unrestricted minimax target.

All expectations below refer to the distribution in the statement. No external result is invoked without a derivation.

1. Normalize and prove the required smooth-convex inequality.
In each realization define F(x)=(f(x)-f(x_star))/L. This is convex, has 1-Lipschitz gradient, has minimum value0 at x_star, and its gradient at x_i is g_i. Its gradient at a minimizer is0: if a differentiable function has a minimum, each directional derivative there is0.
For any differentiable function H with 1-Lipschitz gradient and any vectors u,v, the fundamental theorem of calculus along the line segment gives
H(u+v)-H(u)-<grad H(u),v>
=integral_0^1 <grad H(u+tv)-grad H(u),v>dt <= ||v||^2/2.
For fixed y define H(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>. Convexity and grad H(y)=0 imply H(y)<=H(z) for all z. Apply the preceding inequality at u=x and v=-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)). Since grad H(x)=grad F(x)-grad F(y), this gives
H(y)<=H(x)-||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
Rearranging proves, for every x,y,
F(x)>=F(y)+<grad F(y),x-y>+||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. (I)
All uses of smooth convex interpolation below are this necessary inequality, now proved directly.

2. Integrability and notation.
Write X=x_0-x_star, p_i=E F(x_i), q_i=E||g_i||^2, and a_i=E<X,g_i> for 0<=i<=n. The notation p_i denotes an expected value, whereas F and g_i can be random. Since ||X||=1 in every realization, E||X||^2=1.
The recursion and the Lipschitz gradient imply
||x_i-x_star||<=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1+h_t)
and ||g_i||<=||x_i-x_star||. Empty products equal1. The line-segment bound in part1 at x_star gives 0<=F(x_i)<=||x_i-x_star||^2/2. Thus every expectation used here is finite even though the finite steps have no uniform upper bound.
If some q_i=0, nonnegativity implies g_i=0 almost surely. Then x_i is a minimizer almost surely by convexity, and all later iterates equal x_i. Therefore p_n=0, and the required inequality follows since beta_n>0. It remains to handle q_i>0 for every i.

3. Lower bounds forced on the initial displacement.
From (I) against the minimizer, p_n>=q_n/2. Set b=p_n-q_n/2>=0.
Apply (I) with x=x_i, y=x_{i+1}. The GD identity is x_i-x_{i+1}=h_i g_i. Expected orthogonality gives E<g_{i+1},g_i>=0 and E||g_i-g_{i+1}||^2=q_i+q_{i+1}. Hence
p_i-p_{i+1}>=(q_i+q_{i+1})/2
for each 0<=i<n. Summing these inequalities from i through n-1 yields
p_i>=p_n+q_i/2+sum_{k=i+1}^{n-1}q_k+q_n/2
when i<n.
Apply (I) with x=x_star and y=x_i. It gives
E<g_i,x_i-x_star>>=p_i+q_i/2.
The recursion gives x_i-x_star=X-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t. Expected orthogonality therefore gives E<g_i,x_i-x_star>=a_i. For every i, including i=n, we conclude
a_i >= T_i, where T_i=b+sum_{k=i}^n q_k>0. (II)
For i<n this follows by combining the last two displayed inequalities; for i=n it is a_n>=p_n+q_n/2=b+q_n=T_n.

4. A finite-dimensional second-moment inequality.
Expand the nonnegative expectation of a squared norm:
0<=E||X-sum_{i=0}^n(a_i/q_i)g_i||^2
=1-2sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i+sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i
=1-sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i.
The cross terms in the double sum vanish by expected orthogonality. Since a_i>=T_i>0 and q_i>0, (II) gives
1>=sum_{i=0}^n T_i^2/q_i. (III)
This step works directly in the original finite-dimensional space and does not require that any random sample's gradients be orthogonal.

5. Eliminate the intermediate gradient energies exactly.
For 0<=k<=n, claim
sum_{i=0}^{k-1} T_i^2/q_i >= c_k T_k,
where the empty sum for k=0 is0. The claim holds at k=0. Suppose k<n and it holds at k. Since T_k=q_k+T_{k+1},
c_k T_k+T_k^2/q_k
=(c_k+1)q_k+(c_k+2)T_{k+1}+T_{k+1}^2/q_k.
The nonnegativity of
(sqrt(c_k+1)*sqrt(q_k)-T_{k+1}/sqrt(q_k))^2
bounds the sum of the first and third terms below by 2sqrt(c_k+1)T_{k+1}. Therefore the last display is at least c_{k+1}T_{k+1}, proving the induction.
Taking k=n in this claim and adding the final term gives from (III)
1>=c_n T_n+T_n^2/q_n
=q_n z(z+c_n),
where z=T_n/q_n=1+b/q_n>=1.
Since 2p_n=2b+q_n=q_n(2z-1), it follows that
2p_n <= (2z-1)/(z(z+c_n)).
Put r=(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))/2. Then c_n=2r(r-1) and r>=1. The exact identity
z(z+c_n)-r^2(2z-1)=(z-r)^2>=0
implies
(2z-1)/(z(z+c_n))<=1/r^2=beta_n.
Because ||x_0-x_star||^2=1 in each realization, 2p_n is the expected normalized error in the statement.

6. Estimate and asymptotics for beta_n.
An induction gives c_k>=k^2: it holds at0; if it holds at k, then
c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1)>=k^2+2+2k>=(k+1)^2.
The relation c_n=2r(r-1)<=2r^2 now gives beta_n=1/r^2<=2/c_n<=2/n^2 for n>=1.
For the asymptotic assertion put s_k=sqrt(c_k+1), so s_0=1 and
s_{k+1}=sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1).
Rationalization gives
s_{k+1}-s_k=1+1/(sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1)+s_k+1).
Thus s_k>=k+1 and
s_{k+1}-s_k<=1+1/[2(k+2)].
Summing shows
k+1<=s_k<=k+1+(1/2)sum_{j=2}^{k+1}1/j
<=k+1+(1/2)log(k+1),
where the last bound follows by integrating 1/t on intervals [j-1,j]. Consequently s_k/k tends to1 and c_k/k^2 tends to1. Substitution into beta_n gives n^2 beta_n tending to2.
Finally, 0<=expected normalized error/V_n<=beta_n/V_n, which tends to0 whenever n^2V_n tends to infinity. Every bound depends only on n, giving the asserted uniformity. No interchange of infimum and expectation has occurred.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000800 -->
# Positive-value periodic Markov relay with signed history
Status: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED. No new verifier call.
This tests the specific repair suggested after the minimum-reset and residual-sign obstructions: allow positive proximal function values, and check actual past-plane deficits even when the signed residual is positive.

## 1. A parameterized model
Fix a in(0,1), chi>=1, T>=2, and kappa in[0,T-2]. Assume
T>=2a chi,                                               (1)
(T-2-kappa)-(T-kappa)chi^2+T chi/a>=0.                     (2)
For any number m>=1 of blocks, in R^(m+1) set
zeta_0=e_0,
zeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).
Define alpha_i=chi for even i and alpha_i=1 for odd i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Thus
<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|
and all omega_i are independent.

Each real block consists of one unit step followed by
b_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i.
Take the ONE finite max-affine function
J(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}
and f=env_1 J. Start at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0.
We prove that block i queries X_i and X_i-omega_i with gradient omega_i at both, and that
X_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.
The terminal point is X_m with gradient omega_m.

The function f is legal globally: J is finite nonnegative convex with J(0)=0, and its envelope is finite convex differentiable1-smooth. For precision, the proximal minimization has a unique minimizer by the quadratic term; its subgradient equation implies grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and monotonicity gives
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.
At any point where plane i is active at x-omega_i, that subgradient equation yields the claimed gradient and value
f(x)=<omega_i,x>-alpha_i^2/2-kappa alpha_i^2.
Thus it remains to compare every affine plane at the indicated proximal points.

## 2. Exact signed residuals
Let Z_i=X_i-T omega_i and put
B_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).
The update gives
Z_0=0,
Z_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).
Direct multiplication of Markov correlations proves
<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,
<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,
where
S_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                     (3)
Indeed zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to all zeta_j with j>=l+1, and its inner product with earlier zeta_j is(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j). This proves(3) by summing the update; no sign of S is assumed.

At a real query x=X_i-u omega_i with u=0 or1, its proposed proximal point is
Z_i+d omega_i, d=T-u-1>=T-2.
The terminal query has d=T-1 and obeys the same lower bound.
The current affine plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=0.
The current-minus-j-th plane difference is
d alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)
 +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2          (4)
for j<i, with C_ij=(-a)^(i-j); for j>i the same formula has S=0.
Every coefficient of d is positive: at even separation the amplitudes agree and C<1, while at odd separation C<0. Hence it suffices to check d=T-2.

Future planes are already controlled. At even separation their offsets agree and their deficit is d alpha_i^2(1-C)>=0. At odd separation the deficit is
(d-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2+d a^|i-j| alpha_i alpha_j>=0.
The substantive point is the full past history.

## 3. All past planes, including positive residuals
For even separation i-j=2q, q>=1, let z=alpha_j=alpha_i and y be the opposite amplitude. Formula(3) is the exact geometric sum
S_(j,i)=(T/a)(y-a z)(1-a^(2q)).
Substituting into(4) gives, at d=T-2,
(1-a^(2q)) z[-2z+(T/a)y]>=0
by(1), since z/y is either chi or1/chi.

For odd separation i-j=2q+1, q>=0, let z=alpha_j and y=alpha_i be the opposite amplitude. Now
S_(j,i)=(T/a)[y-a z+a^(2q)(a z-a^2 y)].
Substitution into(4), still at d=T-2, gives
F_infty(y,z)+a^(2q)[T z^2-2a yz],                       (5)
where
F_infty(y,z)=(T-2-kappa)y^2-(T-kappa)z^2+(T/a)yz.
The second term in(5) is nonnegative by(1).
Among the two choices(y,z)=(1,chi),(chi,1), the first has smaller F_infty: the difference is
2(T-1-kappa)(chi^2-1)>=0.
Its nonnegativity is precisely(2). This proves every old-plane comparison, at every lag, with the signed residual retained. Together with the future and zero-plane checks it proves the entire trajectory in Section1.

The exact sampled values are
f(X_i-u omega_i)=(T-u-1/2-kappa)alpha_i^2.
At a checkpoint query u=1, the nonsmooth proximal value is
J(X_i-2omega_i)=(T-2-kappa)alpha_i^2.                    (6)
It is strictly positive whenever kappa<T-2. Thus these checkpoints are not minimum resets.

## 4. Closest minimizer and payoff
The minimizer set of f is exactly
P_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for every i}.
The point y_proj=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0 lies in P_min: for even i the amplitude equals alpha_0 and the correlation is at most1; for odd i the correlation is negative. Constraint0 is active, and
X_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0
is a positive multiple of its normal. For any z in P_min this gives
<X_0-y_proj,z-y_proj><=0,
so squared-distance expansion proves that y_proj is the unique closest minimizer and
dist(X_0,P_min)^2=(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.
The normalized terminal value of this one instance is therefore exactly
2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2 / [(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2].        (7)
For even m, alpha_m=alpha_0 and this is
[2(T-kappa)-1]/(T-kappa)^2.
All smooth-convex interpolation inequalities, including the optimum sample, hold because all samples have been checked on this one explicit envelope.

## 5. Rational parameters, quadratic stability, and a strict failure of the old test
Choose
a=9/10, chi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.
Condition(1) has positive margin
T-2a chi=7/50.
The left side of(2) is exactly37/375>0.
The alternating checkpoint sizes are
b_even=463/135, b_odd=131/30,
both in[3,5]. Therefore for m=2r and every integer r>=1 the length4r schedule
(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated r times
has the explicitly realized normalized error
W_(4r)(h)>=85/121.                                     (8)
At every checkpoint its proximal value is alpha_i^2/5>0.

This model violates the earlier nonpositive-residual criterion. For any even j with j+2<=m,
S_(j,j+2)=(T/a)(1-a^2)(1-a chi)<0
because a chi=27/25>1. Thus
<Z_(j+2),zeta_j> >0,
and D_j>=a D_(j+1) is false at every such pair. Nevertheless the exact plane comparisons above hold. This is an explicit repair, not an assumption that local correlation checks suffice.

For comparison, each quadratic block multiplier is(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).
For0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda).
For1/5<=lambda<=1, its positive value is at most8/25, since b_i>=3; the magnitude of its negative minimum is at most4/5, since b_i<=5 and max_(b<=5,b>=3)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.
Hence the full finite-dimensional quadratic normalized objective after m blocks is at most
max{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.
The nonlinear persistent value is not a large quadratic product.

The omega_i are independent, adjacent ones have negative inner product, and their nonnegative-offset max-affine system has isolated zero/plane hinges. At such hinges the envelope has rank-one Hessian projectors onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving that this function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal basis. Independence permits exposing each hinge by solving for one affine value0 and all others strictly negative. The dimension m+1 grows with the horizon and the gradient energies alternate between36/25 and1.

## 6. Scope
This is a parameterized, fully signed-history construction with positive checkpoint values and a concrete rational periodic test outside the earlier residual-sign interface. It does not claim that(1)--(2), or a generalization of them, can realize an arbitrary schedule at the benchmark profile. The endpoint values of the exact all-past comparison now show where positive residuals can be paid for by real face margins and offsets. A future extension must solve those inequalities quantitatively for nonperiodic data, rather than imposing S>=0 again or assuming feasibility at target strength.

## 7. Uniform survival of one arbitrary final step
For the rational parameters in Section5, let m be even and m>=32. Append ANY prescribed finite nonnegative step t after the2m-step periodic prefix. The same one fixed function above gives
W_(2m+1)(h,t) >= (245/19008)^2 >1/10000,                 (9)
uniformly in t and m.
This uses actual old planes and does not assume a monotone terminal objective.

Write L_j(z)=<omega_j,z>-kappa alpha_j^2 for the affine pieces of J and set c_tail=a^m. Since m is even, alpha_m=alpha_0=6/5. At the endpoint X_m, the exact residual sums from Section3 give
L_0(X_m)=38/375+(46/15)c_tail,
L_(m-1)(X_m)=-19/6.
The directional slopes along omega_m are
<omega_0,omega_m>=(36/25)c_tail,
<omega_(m-1),omega_m>=-27/25.
For clarity, the first value follows from
S_(0,m)=-(46/225)(1-c_tail),
<Z_m,omega_0>=(92/375)(1-c_tail),
<omega_m,omega_0>=(36/25)c_tail,
and X_m=Z_m+T omega_m. The second follows directly from the preceding block update.

Define
lambda_tail=3/(3+4c_tail),
w_tail=lambda_tail omega_0+(1-lambda_tail)omega_(m-1),
eta_tail=lambda_tail kappa alpha_0^2+(1-lambda_tail)kappa alpha_(m-1)^2.
The affine combination L_tail=lambda_tail L_0+(1-lambda_tail)L_(m-1) has zero slope along omega_m, since
lambda_tail(36/25)c_tail-(1-lambda_tail)(27/25)=0.
Thus, for EVERY real t,
L_tail(X_m-t omega_m)
=[38/125-(52/15)c_tail]/(3+4c_tail).                     (10)

For m>=32, c_tail<=1/20. Indeed a^8<9/20 and(9/20)^4<1/20. Consequently the numerator in(10) is at least49/375 and its denominator is at most16/5, so
L_tail(X_m-t omega_m)>=49/1200=:d_tail.
Moreover convexity of the squared norm gives
||w_tail||^2<=lambda_tail alpha_0^2+(1-lambda_tail)alpha_(m-1)^2<=36/25=:C_tail.

Here is the envelope lower bound without an external formula. Since J is at least both0 and L_tail, it is at least theta_tail L_tail for any theta_tail in[0,1]. Completing the square in its Moreau minimization gives, at x=X_m-t omega_m,
f(x)>=theta_tail L_tail(x)-theta_tail^2||w_tail||^2/2.
Take theta_tail=d_tail/C_tail=49/1728<1. Then
2f(x)>=d_tail^2/C_tail.
The initial squared distance to the exact nearest minimizer, from Section4, is4356/625. Division yields
2f(x)/(4356/625)
 >=[(49/1200)/( (6/5)(66/25) )]^2
 =(245/19008)^2>1/10000.
Because grad f(X_m)=omega_m was proved above, x=X_m-t omega_m is exactly the result of the appended GD step. This proves(9), including t=0 and arbitrarily large finite t.

The construction is therefore robust to any one-step terminal repair of this periodic prefix. A general arbitrary-length suffix is not covered by this argument: after the first appended step its gradient can change, so the cancellation of one fixed direction cannot be reused without a new proof.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000801 -->
# Exact two-step annihilator of the rational periodic Markov witness
Status: complete hostile test, UNVERIFIED. This concerns the specified witness family only; it is NOT an upper bound on unrestricted W.

Use the explicit rational periodic construction in research/max_positive_value_periodic_relay.md:
a=9/10,chi=6/5,T=23/10,kappa=1/10,
alpha_even=chi,alpha_odd=1,
omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i,
<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|,
J=max(0,max_i L_i), L_i(x)=<omega_i,x>-kappa alpha_i^2,
f=env_1 J,
and endpoint X_m after the2m-step periodic prefix.
Take any EVEN m=2r>=2, so there are N=r+1 even normals.
The explicit two appended steps are
t_1=115/54,
t_2=max{1,(38/375)/E_N},
where
E_N=(36/25)(1+81/100)/[2+(N-2)(1-81/100)].
Then the same f has exact terminal value0 after these two GD steps.

Here is the full algebra of the hostile test. The trajectory/legality proof of the prefix is included in the associated global-memory extended candidate; no new claim that those findings are accepted facts is made.

1. The first extra step uses grad f(X_m)=omega_m, hence Y=X_m-t_1 omega_m.
For an even index j<m, put c_j=a^(m-j). The signed-history formula yields
L_j(X_m)=38/375+(46/15)c_j,
<omega_j,omega_m>=(36/25)c_j.
The choice t_1=(46/15)/(36/25)=115/54 therefore gives
L_j(Y)=A_tail:=38/375
for EVERY even j, including j=m since
L_m(Y)=(T-kappa-t_1)chi^2=38/375.
For an odd j<m, the same formula gives
S_(j,m)=(T/a)[chi-a+a^(m-j-1)(a-a^2 chi)].
Since <omega_j,omega_m>=-chi a^(m-j), substitution shows that the decaying terms cancel at this same t_1:
L_j(Y)=-13/15
for EVERY odd j.
Thus the first step equalizes all positive even-plane values exactly, while every odd plane has the same negative value.

2. Write rho=a^2=81/100 and let D_N=2+(N-2)(1-rho).
Choose weights on the even normals:
lambda_0=lambda_(N-1)=1/D_N,
lambda_l=(1-rho)/D_N for0<l<N-1.
They are positive and sum to1. Set
w=sum_(l=0)^(N-1)lambda_l omega_(2l).
Because the even Gram matrix has entries chi^2 rho^|l-v|, finite geometric summation gives
<omega_(2l),w>=chi^2(1+rho)/D_N=E_N
at EVERY even index. Therefore ||w||^2=E_N.

For each odd index2l+1, its inner products with the entire even span coincide with those of
-[a/(chi(1+a^2))](omega_(2l)+omega_(2l+2)).
This follows by substituting the geometric correlations on each side of l. Consequently
<omega_(2l+1),w>
=-[2a/(chi(1+a^2))]E_N=-(150/181)E_N.

3. Put tau=min{1,A_tail/E_N}.
At the candidate proximal point Y-tau w, every even affine piece has the common value
A_tail-tau E_N>=0.
Every odd piece has value
-13/15+tau(150/181)E_N
<=-13/15+(150/181)A_tail
=-13/15+76/905<0.
If the even value is positive, tau=1 and w is a convex combination of precisely those active normals. If the even value is0, tau w is a convex combination of0 and the active even normals. In both cases
tau w belongs to the subdifferential of J at Y-tau w.
The Moreau optimality equation proves grad f(Y)=tau w.

The second appended step t_2=A_tail/(tau E_N)=max{1,A_tail/E_N} therefore reaches
Z=Y-(A_tail/E_N)w.
All even planes have value0 at Z, while every odd plane has the negative value -13/15+76/905. Thus J(Z)=0 and f(Z)=0, proving exact annihilation.

For even m>=32 this coexists with the uniform one-extra-step lower bound in6192a365859c116f: a single appended gradient step cannot remove the remote-plane error, but the first step above reorganizes all even planes to a common height, enabling the second step to remove them together.

This explicitly refutes an attempted extension from one arbitrary terminal step to arbitrary-length suffixes for THIS fixed rational periodic witness. It does not rule out the nonstationary modulation family366067681df90ad9, changes of parameters, the arc construction, or other legal functions on this padded schedule. The next repair must break the parity-level equalization or use an adversary selected with the entire suffix in view.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000802 -->
# Positive-value joining of two nonidentical cells

Status: UNVERIFIED joint construction and obstruction. No new verifier call is
allocated. Worker max, 2026-09-26. This is a concrete two-block interface, not the
uniform exact-profile theorem. All supporting calculations needed for this
candidate are included; earlier unverified findings are not used as facts.

## 1. Locally constructible data for arbitrary ordinary steps

A cell has a finite list of normals v=(p,-q) with p>0, 0<=q<=1, including
v_end=(epsilon,-1) and v_cap=(c,0), where epsilon,c>0. Its local queries
r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and selected normals v_j satisfy

    r_(j+1)=r_j-alpha_j v_j,   r_m=v_end,
    <v_j,r_j-v_j> >= <v,r_j-v_j> for every cell normal v,
    u_j>=p_j, y_j>=1>=q_j,
    <v_end,r_j-v_j> >=0.                                  (1)

The selected proximal height h_j=<v_j,r_j-v_j> is nonnegative. For the cells
used below, p>=epsilon q, with equality only at v_end. The endpoint has h_m=0.
These are explicit local data properties, not assumed global interpolation.

Here is one construction for EVERY finite nonnegative ordinary schedule.
Choose epsilon,gamma>0 and set

    c=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2),
    R=sqrt(epsilon^2+(1+gamma)^2),
    V(q)=(sqrt(R^2-(q+gamma)^2),-q), 0<=q<=1.

Start backward from r_m=V(1). Given r_j=(u,-y) and preceding step alpha>=0,
put Delta=y+gamma-alpha gamma. If Delta<=u gamma/c, select v_prev=(c,0).
Otherwise put E=sqrt(u^2+Delta^2) and select

    v_prev=(R u/E,-(R Delta/E-gamma)).

Set r_prev=r_j+alpha v_prev. Retain the selected normals, both endpoints,
and no zero normal. Thus the data require a deterministic finite recursion,
not a feasibility search. Write r_0=(R_x,-R_y), so

    R_x=epsilon+sum alpha_j p_j,
    R_y=1+sum alpha_j q_j.                                 (2)

For completeness, the recursion has the stated properties. Inductively
u>=epsilon, y>=1 and y/u<=1/epsilon. In the turning case
gamma/c<Delta/u<=(1+gamma)/epsilon, so the displayed normal belongs to the
arc. Adding alpha times a normal preserves those three inequalities because
q/p<=1/epsilon. In the turning case

    r_prev-v_prev=(E/R+alpha-1)(v_prev-(0,gamma)).

The coefficient is nonnegative: E>=sqrt(u^2+(y+gamma)^2)-alpha gamma
>=R-alpha gamma. The displayed direction is an outward normal to the disk
centered at (0,gamma), of radius R, containing the whole arc. In the cap case
put M=u/c+alpha-1. The branch inequality gives y<=gamma M and M>0, and

    r_prev-(c,0)=M(c,-gamma)+(0,gamma M-y).

Both terms are normals to the convex hull of the arc and zero at its cap.
Consequently <r_prev-v_prev,v-v_prev><=0 for every arc point and zero.
This proves the maximizing inequality and h_prev>=0. These formulas also
give u_prev>=p_prev. For the endpoint-normal inequality in (1), the turning
formula has inner product equal to a nonnegative coefficient times
epsilon p+q+gamma; in the cap case it equals epsilon(u_prev-c)+y_prev>=0.
All assertions hold at the terminal point too. Restricting the arc to the
finite retained normals preserves every maximizing inequality.

No external theorem is needed for this local proof. Its circular-arc design
was prompted by Ye--Liu, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Lemma 3.1 and Appendix A.1,
whose proof was previously downloaded and read. It is not imported as a
black box or promoted to a fact here.

## 2. Geometry and all cross-block plane inequalities

Construct two cells A and B, independently, for their actual prescribed
ordinary steps. They may use different epsilon, gamma and normal lists.
Embed A in a Euclidean plane and give it gradient scale d_A>0. Translate its
center to zero. Give every A plane the SAME positive value F_A at that center:

    L_v^A(z)=F_A+d_A <v,z>.

The initial point is x_0=d_A r_0^A; all A queries and gradients are
x_j=d_A r_j^A and g_j=d_A v_j^A. Their proposed proximal points are
z_j=d_A(r_j^A-v_j^A), with current plane value F_A+d_A^2 h_j^A.

Execute a prescribed checkpoint b>1 at the final A query. Define

    lambda_A=sqrt(1+epsilon_A^2),
    u=-v_end^A/lambda_A,
    D=(b-1)d_A lambda_A,
    Y=D u.

The checkpoint indeed sends d_A v_end^A to Y. Choose a unit vector e
orthogonal to the A plane. Let r_0^B=(R_x,-R_y). Choose a scale d>0 and put

    theta=D-d R_x >=0,
    p_B=theta u+d R_y e.

Embed the B normal (p,-q) as p u-q e. Its affine plane is

    L_(p,q)^B(z)=F_B+d <p u-q e,z-p_B>,

where F_B>0. The B initial point p_B+d(R_x u-R_y e) equals Y exactly.
Its queries and gradients are p_B+d(u_j u-y_j e) and d(p_j u-q_j e).

Define the positive directional slopes

    a_v=d_A(epsilon_A p+q)/lambda_A,
    mu=min_(v in A) a_v,
    nu=d_A lambda_A.

For the arc cells mu>0 and mu<=nu. Define

    I(d)=max_(p,q in B) [-d p theta+d^2 R_y q].

ALL cross-block plane inequalities hold if and only if

    I(d) <= F_A-F_B <= mu theta.                           (3)

Proof, including nonadjacent queries and normals: at any A proximal point,
its u coordinate is -d_A<v_end^A,r_j^A-v_j^A>/lambda_A<=0 and its e
coordinate is zero. Since every B normal has p>0, its affine value there
is <= its value at zero, namely F_B-d p theta+d^2 R_y q. The selected A
value is >=F_A. The left inequality of (3) is therefore sufficient at
EVERY A query for EVERY future B plane; it is necessary at the terminal
A proximal point zero, where the current value is exactly F_A.

At every B proximal point, its A-plane projection is
[theta+d(u_j-p_j)]u, whose coefficient is >=theta. Every old A plane
there has value <=F_A-a_v theta<=F_A-mu theta. The selected B value is
>=F_B. The right inequality is sufficient at EVERY B query for EVERY old
A plane; it is necessary at the terminal B proximal point p_B, where
the A-plane coefficient is exactly theta and the B value is F_B.

Take ONE nonsmooth function and ONE Moreau envelope:

    J(z)=max(0, all L_v^A(z), all L_w^B(z)),
    f(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].

By (1) and (3), each prescribed normal is an active-plane subgradient at
the proposed proximal point, so x-z belongs to partial J(z). This is the
optimality condition for that proximal point and proves every gradient and
the full GD recursion for (ordinary A, b, ordinary B). No future or remote
plane is discarded. The proximal values are F_A+d_A^2 h_j^A and
F_B+d^2 h_j^B; the function values add one half of the respective squared
gradient norms. In particular the terminal objective satisfies

    2 f_end = 2F_B+d^2(1+epsilon_B^2),                     (4)

provided the zero set discussed below is nonempty, so min f=0.

The smoothness claim can also be checked without an external import. The
strictly convex coercive proximal objective has a unique minimizer z(x).
Its optimality equation is x-z(x) in partial J(z(x)). Monotonicity of the
subgradient gives ||g(x)-g(y)||^2<=<g(x)-g(y),x-y>, with g=x-z(x).
The two minimizing inequalities and this continuity imply grad f=g.
Thus f is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth on the full Euclidean space.

The Gram data are explicit: A--A products are d_A^2< v,v'>, B--B products
are d^2(pp'+qq'), and every A--B product is

    <d_A v,d(p'u-q'e)> = -d p' a_v.                       (5)

In particular this is one coupled nonseparable function in three dimensions,
not a direct sum of independent local smooth functions.

## 3. Exact scale loss and a constructive value choice

For arc B, the function q/(mu+d p(q)) is strictly increasing, because p is
decreasing. Thus I(d)<=mu theta is equivalent to the SINGLE endpoint test

    d^2 R_y <= (D-d R_x)(mu+d epsilon_B).                  (6)

The cap test is automatic. For arbitrary finite B normals the same equivalence
holds when p(q) is decreasing and the endpoint is included, including the
two-plane cells in Section 5. Solving the quadratic gives

    d <= d_max,
    d_max = [D epsilon_B-mu R_x
             +sqrt((mu R_x-D epsilon_B)^2
                    +4D mu(R_y+epsilon_B R_x))]
             /[2(R_y+epsilon_B R_x)].                    (7)

The positive root lies strictly between zero and D/R_x. When mu=0 this
reduces to the minimum-reset bound

    d_reset = D/(R_x+R_y/epsilon_B).

Positive inherited values can improve this scale when mu>0, but do not remove
its geometric cost. Setting theta=0 forces the endpoint B plane at the old
center to equal F_B+d^2 R_y>F_B, whereas (3) requires F_A<=F_B. This is a
contradiction. Thus a pure new-coordinate join with no advance theta is
impossible even with freely chosen positive values. The substantive changed
state in this construction is the positive advance theta and the retained
level difference, rather than a reset at a common minimum.

Here is a completely prescribed feasible choice, without solving global
interpolation. For ANY two ordinary schedules and ANY bridge b>1, choose

    d=D/[2(R_x+R_y/epsilon_B)],
    theta=D-d R_x,
    F_A=F_B= [d c_B nu theta]/[2(nu+d c_B)].               (8)

Then each B plane at the old center has negative increment, since
q/p<=1/epsilon_B and d(R_x+R_y/epsilon_B)=D/2. Thus I(d)<=0. The other
inequality in (3) holds because theta>0. The minimizer feasibility condition
in Section 4 holds with slack. This proves a NONPERIODIC sufficient criterion
in the actual prescribed steps: construct each ordinary block by the explicit
backward recursion and use (8). It does not say that the resulting normalized
payoff has the target size, or that the joined object can be iterated after
discarding its old planes.

More generally take delta=max(0,I(d)), F_A=F_B+delta. Whenever
delta<nu theta, a legal positive choice is any

    0<F_B<= d c_B(nu theta-delta)/(nu+d c_B).              (9)

If mu<nu, this includes d=d_max. The extra restriction is essential: global
minimum normalization must not be inferred merely from the nonnegativity of J.

## 4. Exact global minimum and distance

Take coordinates t=<u,z>, w=<w_A,z>, z_e=<e,z>, where
w_A=(-1,-epsilon_A)/lambda_A in the A plane. For an A normal put

    b_v=d_A(p-epsilon_A q)/lambda_A.

The endpoint has a_v=nu,b_v=0; every other retained arc normal has b_v>0.
The full zero polyhedron {J=0} is exactly

    F_A/nu <= t <= theta-F_B/(d c_B),
    w >= W(t):=max_(v in A, v!=v_end) (F_A-a_v t)/b_v,
    z_e >= Z(t):=max_(p,q in B, q>0)
                         [d R_y+F_B/(d q)+(p/q)(t-theta)]. (10)

Consequently it is nonempty exactly when

    theta >= F_A/nu+F_B/(d c_B).                          (11)

There is no omitted plane in (10): the endpoint A plane and cap B plane give
the t interval, and all other A and B planes give the two displayed maxima.
The minima of f are exactly this zero polyhedron, since both terms in the
envelope definition are nonnegative and its minimum is attained.

The initial point has coordinates

    t_0=-d_A(epsilon_A R_x^A+R_y^A)/lambda_A,
    w_0=d_A(-R_x^A+epsilon_A R_y^A)/lambda_A,
    z_0=0.

Thus its squared distance to the ACTUAL nearest minimizer is exactly

    dist_0^2 = min over t in the interval (10) of
       (t-t_0)^2 + (max(W(t),w_0)-w_0)^2 + max(Z(t),0)^2. (12)

This is an explicit scalar piecewise-quadratic formula determined by the
normal lists: the breakpoints of the displayed finite maxima and the vertex
of each resulting quadratic give a finite closed rule for the minimum. It
is a normalization calculation, not a hidden hypothesis about interpolation
feasibility. Together (4) and (12) give the exact normalized payoff of this
particular instance, [2F_B+d^2(1+epsilon_B^2)]/dist_0^2. Section 5 evaluates
the actual nearest minimizer in rational closed form.

## 5. Rational nonidentical example with all values and normalization

Cell A has normals (1,0) and (3/4,-1), one ordinary step 5, and d_A=1.
Its local queries are (23/4,-1) and (3/4,-1). At the first proximal point
(19/4,-1), the cap inner product is 76/16 and the endpoint inner product
is 73/16; at the final proximal point zero they agree. Hence this two-plane
cell is legal directly, independently of the arc recursion.

Take bridge b=3. Then u=(-3/5,4/5,0), D=5/2, mu=3/5, nu=5/4.
Cell B has normals (1,0),(1/2,-1) and ordinary step 3, with local queries
(7/2,-1),(1/2,-1). Its first proximal cap and endpoint inner products
are 5/2 and 9/4, respectively. Set

    d=3/5, theta=2/5, F_A=7/25, F_B=1/25,
    p_B=(-6/25,8/25,3/5).

Here I(d)=6/25=mu theta=F_A-F_B. Formula (7) gives d_max=3/5, whereas
d_reset=5/11. This improves squared transmitted scale by 1089/625 before
normalization; the actual normalized comparison is evaluated below.

In Cartesian coordinates (x,y,z), the ENTIRE nonsmooth function is

    J=max(0,L_0,L_1,L_2,L_3),
    L_0=7/25+x,
    L_1=7/25+(3/4)x-y,
    L_2=-(9/25)x+(12/25)y-1/5,
    L_3=7/25-(9/50)x+(6/25)y-(3/5)z.

Its envelope realizes schedule (5,3,3), with exact queries and gradients

    x_0=(23/4,-1,0),          g_0=(1,0,0),
    x_1=(3/4,-1,0),           g_1=(3/4,-1,0),
    x_2=(-3/2,2,0),           g_2=(-9/25,12/25,0),
    x_3=(-21/50,14/25,0),     g_3=(-9/50,6/25,-3/5).

The complete plane-value check at the four proximal points x_i-g_i is

    i    L_0       L_1       L_2       L_3       chosen
    0    503/100   1937/400  -239/100  -163/200  L_0
    1    7/25      7/25     -1/5       7/25     L_1
    2   -43/50    -419/200    47/50     17/20    L_2
    3    1/25     -11/50      1/25      1/25    L_3

Every chosen value is nonnegative and dominates ALL four planes. The
proximal values are therefore (503/100,7/25,47/50,1/25). The function values
are (553/100,849/800,28/25,53/200). In particular the two checkpoint proximal
values F_A and F_B are strictly positive. All pairwise correlations can be
read from the displayed exact gradients; no stochastic realization is used.

The zero polyhedron, in t=<u,(x,y)> and w=<(-4/5,-3/5),(x,y)>, is

    28/125<=t<=1/3,
    w>=7/20-3t/4,
    z>=7/15+t/2.

The initial coordinates are t_0=-17/4,w_0=-4,z_0=0. Its squared distance
at fixed feasible t is

    (t+17/4)^2+(87/20-3t/4)^2+(7/15+t/2)^2.

One half of its derivative is (29/16)t+293/240>0 throughout the feasible
interval. Its unique minimum is at t=28/125, giving

    x_star=(-7/25,7/100,217/375),
    ||x_0-x_star||^2=42570737/1125000,
    2 f(x_3)/||x_0-x_star||^2=596250/42570737.             (13)

This is a lower bound on W_3(5,3,3), not its exact value.

For comparison WITHIN THE SAME JOIN GEOMETRY, put both levels to zero and
choose d=d_reset=5/11. The same schedule and same initial point are legal.
Origin is a global minimizer and is nearest: x_0=5(1,0,0)+(3/4,-1,0)
is a positive combination of the two active A normals there. Its distance
squared is 545/16; the terminal numerator is 125/484. The normalized payoff
is 100/13189. Thus the positive-value join improves this particular normalized
baseline by the exact factor 14298075/7740134, not merely by an unnormalized
gradient comparison. This numerical factor has no unrestricted minimax claim.

## 6. Hostile schedules and the exact failure of memoryless iteration

The minimum-reset obstruction survives in a precise form: theta=0 is
impossible, and the nonzero allowed theta is constrained by both (6) and
the actual-minimum condition (11). Formula (7), rather than the cost-free
scale D/R_x, is the quantitative transfer law.

For the recursive hostile schedules H_0=(),
H_(r+1)=(H_r,b_r,H_r), b_r=(13/5)^(r-17), the two ordinary blocks H_r are
legitimate inputs to the arc recursion. When r>=18, b_r>1, so the explicit
choice (8) gives a legal positive-value join for the actual schedule H_(r+1).
It does not require b_r>=sum H_r+2. More generally ANY two nonidentical
ordinary lists with any bridge b>1 satisfy this constructive criterion.
No comparison of (12) with the full exact benchmark is inferred. In
particular the known Huber calibration of this recursive family is not a
new solution of the arbitrary-schedule problem.

There is a concrete closure failure if the joined object is compressed to
the B cell alone. Its outgoing unit direction is

    u_B=-(epsilon_B u-e)/sqrt(1+epsilon_B^2).

Every OLD A normal has POSITIVE slope in that direction:

    <d_A v,u_B>=epsilon_B a_v/sqrt(1+epsilon_B^2)>0.        (14)

The B normals have negative outgoing slopes. Thus the entire inherited
plane family cannot be replaced by a decreasing envelope based only on
F_B and the B normals. In the rational example L_0(p_B)=F_B and
<g_0,u_B>=3sqrt(5)/25>0. Immediately along p_B+s u_B, s>0, this remote
plane rises strictly above F_B while all B planes decrease. This is an
exact remote-plane counterexample to that proposed state compression,
not a prohibition on three-block joins retaining the old data or choosing
the function with knowledge of the complete schedule.

The next substantive variable is therefore the whole signed directional
profile of inherited planes at the new center, or a proved sufficient
envelope retaining its increasing branches. A further periodic example
would not resolve this closure issue. No extra verifier call was made.
